- `RouteExecutor` platform-end resolution, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` start-side selection, and `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` suffix search must all use the same shared backend pathfinding home/helper. The implementation may extend the existing backend pathfinding code in `PathDispatcher.find_paths()` / `PathDispatcher._bfs_find_paths(...)` or extract that code into a `RailwayPathfinder` class, but it must not create a second disconnected pathfinder with different comparison or direction rules. That shared helper must be callable before any live `Path.resource_sequence` exists, must return the ordered candidate walk it used for comparison, and must be the single source for candidate-length comparison, direction filtering, current-span end selection, and tie-breaking rather than letting different layers reimplement those rules independently.
- The dispatcher path-acquisition API surface is exactly `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` for a new authority and `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` for growing an existing live authority. V1 must not introduce a third acquisition wrapper or route code path with a third acquisition contract.

**Path field schema**

This is the single canonical field set for the dispatcher-owned `Path` entity in v1.

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path_id` | `str` | Unique dispatcher path identifier |
| `status` | `PathStatus` | One of `ACTIVE`, `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED` |
| `train_id` | `str` | Assigned train |
| `path_tiles` | `List[path_tile]` | Full ordered train-tail -> authority-end geometry. Derived read: this is the concatenation of `resource_sequence[*].path_tiles` in `sequence_index` order, rebuilt on every successful create/extend. It is never mutated independently. |
| `resource_sequence` | `List[resource_sequence entry]` | Ordered interleaved block/switch resources. This is the single source of truth for path geometry. |
| `current_resource_index` | `int` | `sequence_index` of the current path-progress anchor resource: head-based once a true head exists, otherwise initialized to the authority-side resource at the physical train end closer to the path end |
| `requested_destination_block_id` | `str` | Caller's current requested block target carried by this live path |
| `requested_destination_direction` | `str` | Resolved block-relative traversal constraint at the requested target (`"forward"` or `"reverse"` only); used for compatibility/filtering, not exact terminal-frame geometry |
| `authority_end_block_id` | `str` | Current live authority end block |
| `last_status_updated_time` | `float` | System-time Unix timestamp when `status` last changed |

- `Path` is the entity name for the dispatcher-owned resource-sequence authority record itself. Shared contracts must refer back to this table instead of rediscovering the field set from scattered prose.

**Path status state machine**

This is the single canonical state-machine contract for `Path.status` in v1. `status` is the lifecycle enum. Shared contracts must not invent extra path lifecycle states or path-owned pending cleanup flags.

| From `status` | Trigger | To `status` | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (none) | `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` succeeds | `ACTIVE` | Path record is inserted into `paths_by_id` and indexed as the train's active authority. Writes `last_status_updated_time` to the synchronous create-time system-time Unix timestamp. |
| `ACTIVE` | `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` is called from the post-physics Phase 3 completion check and the active path's live authority reaches the path's requested target block and direction | `COMPLETED` | The **Active-path completion procedure** owns ordering. Dispatcher commits the post-physics controller stop check plus the path-owned requested-target check; it does not infer completion from precise train position or require physical head occupancy in the requested block. |
| `ACTIVE` | `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` | `CANCELLED` | Not-yet-entered authority resources are released synchronously; controller authority is cleared; `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` is cleared; `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(...)` is notified. |
| `COMPLETED` | any | `COMPLETED` | Terminal. `cancel_path(...)` on a `COMPLETED` path fails with reason `"already completed"`. |
| `CANCELLED` | any | `CANCELLED` | Terminal. `cancel_path(...)` on a `CANCELLED` path fails with reason `"already cancelled"`. |

- `status` is set once per lifecycle transition by the originating synchronous call. No later stopped cleanup pass re-transitions status or writes a pending field.
- Physical `occupied_by` (block or switch) is outside this state machine. It is cleared only by controller-reported tail-vacate observations committed through `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` and never by `status` transitions.
- A train may have at most one `ACTIVE` path at a time; retained `COMPLETED` / `CANCELLED` rows do not block a fresh `create_path(...)`.
- In a stored live path, `resource_sequence[-1]` must always be a `block` entry. A v1 path may contain switch resources internally, but its live authority end is always expressed by `authority_end_block_id` on a block resource, never on a terminal switch.

**path_tile entry schema**

This is the single canonical definition of one tile step inside `Path.path_tiles` and inside each `resource_sequence[*].path_tiles` slice.

- Each `path_tile` entry carries exactly:
  - `x`
  - `y`
  - `entry_edge`
  - `exit_edge`
  - `resource_sequence_index` — the parent `Path.resource_sequence[*].sequence_index`.
- `entry_edge` and `exit_edge` are required on every tile entry; shared contracts must not infer them later from neighboring coordinates.
- For same-block / same-tile synthetic movement, tile-entry `entry_edge` and `exit_edge` follow the train's intended forward cab direction for that move. If the move stays inside a single tile and no cab is selected, dispatcher must reject the request rather than inventing a traversal frame.

**resource_sequence entry schema**

This is the single canonical definition of a `resource_sequence` entry. Every site that describes the controller path plan, dispatcher/UI authority projections, the `to_dict()` exports, or any other projection of authority geometry refers back to this schema by name rather than restating it.

- Each `resource_sequence` entry carries exactly:
  - `sequence_index` — position of the entry in the path's train-tail -> authority-end traversal order.
  - `resource_type` — `"block"` or `"switch"`.
  - `resource_id` — stable network resource ID for the block or switch.
  - `path_tiles` — the contiguous ordered slice of canonical `path_tile` entries belonging to that resource, in train-tail -> authority-end traversal order. Every nested tile entry in this slice has `resource_sequence_index == sequence_index`.
  - `entry_edge` — the directed traversal-frame entry edge for that resource in train-tail -> authority-end traversal order.
  - `exit_edge` — the directed traversal-frame exit edge for that resource in train-tail -> authority-end traversal order.
- No other fields are required in v1.
- Ordered tiles alone are not enough for same-block / same-tile movement because a single tile can be traversed in either direction and the stop frame changes with the chosen entry side; `entry_edge` and `exit_edge` are required fields on every entry.
- `entry_edge` must equal the first nested `path_tiles[0].entry_edge`, and `exit_edge` must equal the last nested `path_tiles[-1].exit_edge`; they are stored redundantly so resource-level consumers can read the traversal frame without unpacking the whole tile slice.
- For switch resources, the required switch state is not stored as a fixed field on the entry. It is derived at acquisition time from the planner-facing `TrackSegment.enumerate_traversals(entry_edge)` contract on the switch tile, using the `(entry_edge, exit_edge)` pair selected for that resource's `path_tiles` slice. This derivation replaces storing `switch_positions` as a separate dict on the path.
- These per-resource tile slices plus explicit resource/tile entry-exit edges are the geometry contract for controller/UI consumers after `Train.current_path` is removed.
- Replace `current_block_index` with `current_resource_index`, which should track the `sequence_index` of the current path-progress anchor resource, not the first matching raw resource ID. Once a cab is selected or live progress updates establish a true head, this index is strictly head-based. Before that, it is initialized to the authority-side resource at the physical train end that is closer to the path end under the same comparison used by automatic cab selection.
- Remove `blocks_cleared`; the desired model does not need a stored `resources_cleared` replacement in v1.
- Remove `flank_protection_switches` from `Path`; flank protection should live on the switch entity and be acquired automatically when the switch is locked.
- Add the requested target fields on `Path`: `requested_destination_block_id` and `requested_destination_direction`. `requested_destination_direction` is the caller's already-resolved block-relative traversal constraint for the requested target and is always `"forward"` or `"reverse"` by the time dispatcher stores it. It is used to filter compatible terminal candidates, to choose a legal terminal traversal frame, and as part of the dispatcher completion predicate; no stop-centimeter field is stored on the path.
- Add the current authorized target field on `Path`: `authority_end_block_id`. This is the live authority end block whose forward boundary the controller brakes toward using its safety-margin policy.
- The live terminal frame still comes from explicit directed geometry on the relevant terminal `resource_sequence` block entry (`path_tiles`, `entry_edge`, `exit_edge`). For a same-block / same-tile synthetic authority, that traversal frame must still be explicit in the path contract; the spec must not rely on readers inferring direction from tile coordinates alone or from `requested_destination_direction` alone.
- When the live authority already reaches the requested destination, `authority_end_block_id == requested_destination_block_id` and the terminal block entry's computed block-relative traversal direction equals `requested_destination_direction`. When the live authority is a best-effort truncated prefix, the requested target fields remain unchanged while `authority_end_block_id` describes the temporary current authority boundary.
- Remove overlap / approach-lock data from `Path` in the desired model; they are not part of the new design and must not be retained for old approach-lock policy, cancellation gating, or history in v1. Do not add a path-owned pending cleanup field. Physical `occupied_by` state and train-owned switch protection change only through controller-reported resource entry/tail-vacate observations committed by `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)`.
- `PathDispatcher` should treat each `Path` record as the authoritative dispatcher-owned database entry, keyed by `path_id` in `paths_by_id[path_id]`, with `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` holding the one active authority path for that train. The controller inbox is only a pushed projection of that current authority.
- Dispatcher-owned path records should remain available while active and, after termination, as retained `CANCELLED` or `COMPLETED` records for history/inspection. In v1, retain that history indefinitely rather than aging it out. Update `last_status_updated_time` on every `status` transition for recordkeeping, but authority release must not depend on the controller inbox surviving.
- `PathDispatcher`'s retained path database should be the authoritative historical store for every path it has created. After `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` has been cleared, controller progress reports resolve active authority only against the new active authority, while delayed release or occupancy reporting for a block or switch that remains under the train resolves from the resource itself (`BlockManager` state for blocks, `Switch.occupied_by` / `Switch.train_protection_claims` for switches). `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` must reject while that train still has an `ACTIVE` path, but once the old path is no longer active retained history rows do not by themselves block creation; only ordinary live resource conflicts remain.
- In the desired model, `train_id` is mandatory for every dispatcher authority path, including ordinary route-driven paths and one-off operator routes. Frontend/API callers must be updated to supply it; anonymous authority paths are out of scope for this model. Future authority on reserved resources is tracked through `Block.reserved_by_path` (written by `BlockManager.reserve_blocks_for_path(...)`) and `Switch.locks` (keyed by `(path_id, claim_role, source_switch_id)`, where main claims use `source_switch_id = None`, and each claim stores `path_id`, `claim_role`, `train_id`, and `locked_state`); the block-level `Block.reserve_for_path(train_id, direction)` method itself no longer takes `path_id`.
- There is no v1 compatibility adapter for legacy path-request payloads. Repo callers, REST handlers, and UI/API layers must migrate directly to the new `create_path(...)` / `extend_path(...)` contract rather than expecting dispatcher to reinterpret old request shapes.

**Path API migration**

- Remove the legacy path-request contract entirely in v1. Do not keep or shim old caller payloads that were built around caller-supplied start/end fields, legacy result wrappers, or anonymous/manual complete-path flows.
- The canonical replacement write interfaces are `PathDispatcher.create_path(requested_destination_block_id, train_id, requested_destination_direction, acquisition_mode=...)`, `PathDispatcher.extend_path(path_id, requested_destination_block_id, requested_destination_direction, acquisition_mode=...)`, and `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)`. `extend_path(...)` is the same request contract as `create_path(...)` plus the leading `path_id` that identifies which live path is being grown in place. `cancellation_kind` and free-form `reason` are diagnostic in v1; callers must not branch on either when deciding planning or routing behavior. Create/extend branching uses the structured `result_kind` / `failure_kind` fields in the create/extend result object instead of parsing `reason`.
- The canonical replacement read interfaces are the controller-owned `received_path_plan` projection for active authority geometry, `TrainController.get_current_span()` for current physical span, `TrainController.get_lookahead()` for live speed/limit diagnostics, and a dispatcher-owned progress projection such as `PathDispatcher.get_active_path_progress(train_id) -> {path_id, status, current_resource_index}` when UI/debug consumers need live `current_resource_index`. That projection is resolved from dispatcher-owned path registries (for example `active_path_id_by_train` plus `paths_by_id`) and returns the currently active path bound to that `train_id`; it does not read train/controller mirror fields.
- Route-managed planning remains point-to-point per call. `RouteExecutor` never asks dispatcher/pathfinder to solve a whole multi-waypoint route in one search. Each `create_path(...)` or `extend_path(...)` request targets exactly one currently resolved waypoint block at a time, and longer waypoint chains are formed by concatenating repeated successful point-to-point segments onto the same live path.

**`create_path()` flow**

1. Before candidate search or any side effect, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` must reject the request if `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` already references an `ACTIVE` path. A train can have only one active authority plan at a time. This failure happens with no path ID allocation, switch acquisition, block reservation, path push, switch-state mutation, or `paths_by_id` record insertion. Callers must use `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` to grow that same live path, or wait until the old path has transitioned out of `ACTIVE` before calling `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` again.
1. Candidate search should enumerate direction-compatible resource sequences from the train's current occupied span to `requested_destination_block_id`, reject any candidate that uses repeated resources, reject any candidate that traverses a disabled block or disabled switch, reject any candidate whose traversal violates a block's `Block.allowed_travel_directions`, and reject any candidate that reaches the terminal block in a way that does not satisfy `requested_destination_direction`. On a single `create_path(...)` call, dispatcher then selects exactly one candidate per the canonical comparison order `tile_count`, then `walk_length_cm`, then `candidate_rank`, with `end_a` used only as the final deterministic dead-heat tie-break if those metrics still remain exactly tied across the remaining start-side choices, and applies `acquisition_mode` to that one candidate. There is no fall-through to a second candidate within the same call. Higher-level alternative-target attempts are owned by `RouteExecutor`: in one eligible Phase 1 pass it may try additional distinct resolved `(requested_destination_block_id, requested_destination_direction)` targets, but it must not call `create_path(...)` twice for the same resolved pair in that pass. After every distinct same-tick target has failed without authority creation or growth, planning waits until the next eligible tick.
1. Dispatcher must build and store the exact directed terminal frame for the requested target (`path_tiles`, `entry_edge`, `exit_edge`) as part of the terminal `resource_sequence` block entry. For an ordinary live path, dispatcher derives that frame from the chosen terminal block traversal. For same-block / same-tile normalization, dispatcher derives the needed local frame from the `train_id`'s current occupied blocks and, when the move stays inside a single tile, the currently selected cab direction. When the requested target was resolved from a multi-block platform, `RouteExecutor` must already have resolved one concrete terminal block for that run before calling dispatcher, and the terminal frame uses that already-resolved terminal block directly; dispatcher does not choose between platform blocks itself.
1. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` should take `requested_destination_block_id`, `train_id`, `requested_destination_direction`, and `acquisition_mode`; it should not require a caller-supplied `start_block_id`, a stop-centimeter value, or a second opaque waypoint/platform stop-context payload. Dispatcher derives the planning start side from `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train_id).get_current_span()` plus controller cab state by calling the same shared network-graph walk query that `RouteExecutor` uses. If a cab is selected, the only start-side candidate is the current head end implied by that cab. If no cab is selected, compare both physical ends of the train against the already resolved terminal block and use the end whose successful graph walk is shorter. If one physical end is currently on a switch resource, map that end to the one occupied block inward from that end of the current span before running the candidate comparison; if no such inward occupied block exists for that end, that end is not a valid start-side candidate. The canonical comparison order is: lower candidate `tile_count`, then lower candidate `walk_length_cm`, then lower candidate `candidate_rank`, and if those metrics remain exactly tied between the remaining start-side choices use `end_a` as the final deterministic dead-heat tie-break. Dispatcher must not introduce any other layer-local ad hoc tie-break. Depending on the caller, that shared graph query may return only distance metrics or the ordered tile/block walk used to build `resource_sequence`.
1. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` should also take an explicit `acquisition_mode` parameter of type `PathDispatcher.AcquisitionMode`, an enum owned by `PathDispatcher` with v1 values `all_or_nothing` and `best_effort`.
1. Candidate search must become direction-aware. The shared ordered-walk entry point (named `RailwayPathfinder.find_ordered_walk(...)` in this document if the implementation extracts a class from the existing dispatcher search) must reject candidates whose traversal uses a disabled block or disabled switch, reject candidates whose traversal violates `Block.allowed_travel_directions`, and reject candidates whose terminal traversal does not satisfy `requested_destination_direction`, and same-block / same-tile synthetic path construction must use `requested_destination_direction` only as a constraint while dispatcher still derives the exact local terminal frame from explicit geometry. For a direct `block_id` target, `requested_destination_direction` is interpreted against that block's immutable internal traversal order: `"forward"` means the increasing direction of that block-local order and `"reverse"` means the decreasing direction. `RouteExecutor` must resolve any waypoint-authored `"either"` into one concrete block-relative `"forward"` or `"reverse"` before calling `create_path(...)` or `extend_path(...)`; dispatcher never receives `"either"` in the v1 path contract. If dispatcher still cannot build one unique terminal frame for the chosen move, path creation must fail rather than guessing.
1. Stop-position centimeter inputs are not part of `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` or `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` in v1. The controller computes the braking target from the authority boundary and its canonical safety margin.
1. Allow `create_path(...)` for same-block repositioning when the train's current occupied span already includes `requested_destination_block_id`, but treat that as a dispatcher capability for explicit one-off route workflows rather than as the normal route-side handling for an already covered stop waypoint. Argument naming is consistent throughout the desired model: `requested_destination_block_id` is the caller's current requested target passed into `create_path(...)` / `extend_path(...)`, and `authority_end_block_id` is the current live-authority end stored on the `Path`.
1. For an explicit same-block repositioning request, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` owns deciding what tiles/resources must be assigned based on the train's current occupied span and the requested destination block/direction. It does not accept or store a separate centimeter stop target for that local move; the controller uses the ordinary safety-margin stop logic. If the required movement stays on ordinary track tiles inside one block, dispatcher may build a synthetic local path with one block resource whose `path_tiles` slice covers the local movement. If that movement stays inside a single tile, dispatcher must still use `requested_destination_direction` plus the train's currently selected cab direction to validate and disambiguate the local traversal frame. If those disagree, or if no cab is selected for this same-tile edge case, dispatcher must reject the request with a defined failure reason such as `"same-tile synthetic movement requires a selected cab direction"` rather than guessing.
1. In `all_or_nothing` mode, if the full chosen candidate path cannot be acquired, roll back and return failure; no live path is created. Dispatcher does not fall through to a second candidate inside the same call.
1. In `best_effort` mode, if the full chosen candidate cannot be acquired, `PathDispatcher` should truncate the authority to the furthest forward block-terminated resource prefix it can acquire on that candidate and still create one live path. Dispatcher does not fall through to a second candidate inside the same call; if that candidate cannot produce any storable live `resource_sequence` because the train has no usable occupied-span seed, because no block-terminated prefix can be acquired, or because of the head-on-switch failure below, the call fails and `RouteExecutor` may then try a different distinct resolved target later in that same eligible Phase 1 pass or on a later eligible tick, but not the same resolved pair twice in one pass. That truncated live path keeps the same `path_id`, preserves the requested target fields, and sets `authority_end_block_id` to the last block in the current authority. The controller does not need a special "you are already at your authority end" branch: it enters the ordinary running flow, and ATC/lookahead handles the geometry using the current authority boundary plus safety margin. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` never returns `result_kind = blocked_no_growth` in v1. A successful create has created a brand-new path record with at least one block resource in its live `resource_sequence`; if the live authority reaches the requested target, return `result_kind = reached_requested_target`, otherwise return `result_kind = truncated_grew`. If no block-terminated live authority can be stored, return `success=False` with a structured `failure_kind` rather than storing a no-growth create result.
    - Special case: if the furthest acquirable prefix ends on a `switch` resource, dispatcher must truncate again to the previous `block` resource before storing the live path, so the stored live authority is always block-terminated.
    - If the train's covered span itself is empty so no non-empty live `resource_sequence` can be formed for the new path, `PathDispatcher.create_path(..., acquisition_mode=best_effort)` must fail.
    - Exception: if the train head is currently on a switch resource and no forward block beyond that switch can be acquired, `PathDispatcher.create_path(..., acquisition_mode=best_effort)` must fail with `failure_kind = head_on_switch_no_block_authority` rather than storing a head-on-switch holding authority. A natural-language failure reason such as `"cannot create best-effort authority while head is on a switch"` is sufficient in v1.
1. Allocate the new `path_id` before switch acquisition so `Switch.locks` and flank-protection claims can be keyed by that same path ID. Once the chosen acquisition result succeeds, create the dispatcher-owned path record directly in `ACTIVE` state and push the controller path plan immediately. There should be no persisted intermediate `SET` state in the desired model.
1. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` seeds `resource_sequence` from the train's already-occupied resource span. Start-side selection uses the canonical rule already defined in the previous step: cab-selected -> head-side occupied block; no cab -> both end blocks compared by `(tile_count, walk_length_cm, candidate_rank, end_a)`. The stored sequence is `[already-occupied resources from the opposite span end through the chosen start side] + [forward candidate from start side to requested target]`. The already-occupied backward portion is not re-reserved; only the forward suffix is subject to `all_or_nothing` vs `best_effort` acquisition. `current_resource_index` is initialized to the head resource (cab selected) or to the authority-side resource at the closer span end (no cab). On each seeded entry, the side toward the path direction is `exit_edge` and the opposite side is `entry_edge`. Covered-task normalization may prevent route-side calls for already-covered `task_kind == "waypoint"` tasks, but it must not skip an unserved `task_kind == "stop"` merely because the target block is already covered; same-block stop and reposition authorities still flow through this same `create_path(...)` contract.
1. Build `resource_sequence` in traversal order so both blocks and switches are tracked in one sequence.
1. Store both target contracts on the `Path`: the resolved requested target (`requested_destination_block_id`, `requested_destination_direction`) and the current authorized target (`authority_end_block_id`).
1. The active-path guard has already run before acquisition. A retained `CANCELLED` / `COMPLETED` row or retained history row does not by itself block `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)`; only ordinary resource conflicts still apply.
1. Store the new dispatcher-owned path record in `paths_by_id[path_id]` and set `active_path_id_by_train[train_id] = path_id`.
1. Do not copy reserved-block state onto `Train`; future reservations remain dispatcher-owned on resources and in the controller-facing authority projection until head-entry consumes them.
1. Push the canonical **received_path_plan schema** to the controller after successful creation. Its nested authority geometry must follow the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema** and **path_tile entry schema**.
1. The pushed plan must preserve an explicit traversal frame for every block and switch entry; readers must not try to recover that frame later from tile coordinates alone.

**`create_path()` / `extend_path()` return contract**

- `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` and `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` return a small result object with exactly `success: bool`, `path_id: Optional[str]`, `resource_sequence: Optional[List[resource_sequence entry]]`, `requested_destination_block_id: Optional[str]`, `requested_destination_direction: Optional[str]`, `authority_end_block_id: Optional[str]`, `result_kind: Optional[PathAcquisitionResultKind]`, `failure_kind: Optional[PathAcquisitionFailureKind]`, and `reason: Optional[str]`.
- `PathAcquisitionResultKind` has v1 values:
  - `reached_requested_target`: the live authority reaches the requested destination block/direction for this attempt.
  - `truncated_grew`: `best_effort` acquired at least one new forward resource but the live authority is still short of the requested target.
  - `blocked_no_growth`: `best_effort` proved the current live path is still a valid prefix toward the requested target but could not acquire the next forward resource because of live occupancy/reservation/lock conflict on this tick; valid only for `extend_path(...)` against an existing live path. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` never returns this result kind.
- `PathAcquisitionFailureKind` has v1 values:
  - `no_viable_path`: no topology-compatible, direction-compatible suffix exists to the requested target under disabled-resource and repeated-resource rules.
  - `blocked_before_target`: a viable candidate exists but the requested authority could not be acquired to the requested target. In `all_or_nothing`, this means the full candidate could not be acquired. In `best_effort` create, this means no block-terminated live authority could be stored for the chosen candidate.
  - `invalid_reverse`: `extend_path(...)` would reverse at the current authority end.
  - `invalid_request`: required request state is missing or ambiguous, such as same-tile synthetic movement without a selected cab.
  - `head_on_switch_no_block_authority`: `best_effort` could not form a block-terminated authority because the train head is on a switch and no forward block beyond that switch can be acquired.
  - `missing_or_inactive_path`: `extend_path(...)` was called with a missing or non-`ACTIVE` path.
- On success, `path_id`, `resource_sequence`, `authority_end_block_id`, and `result_kind` are required; `failure_kind = None`; `reason = None`; and `resource_sequence` is the full current live `resource_sequence` after that create/extend operation completes. `RouteExecutor` may inspect `result_kind` and geometry to see whether the armed pending-waypoint target is already contained in the live path, whether best-effort authority grew, or whether the live path remains a valid blocked prefix. It does not advance any route waypoint index from this return payload alone.
- Success means that `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` created a new path record with a non-empty block-terminated live `resource_sequence`, or that `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` either appended at least one new resource to the existing live `resource_sequence` or returned `result_kind = blocked_no_growth` for an existing live path that remains a valid prefix toward the caller's requested target but is temporarily blocked by live occupancy/reservation/lock conflict. A create-path call never succeeds as `blocked_no_growth`; if it cannot store any block-terminated live authority for the chosen candidate, it fails. An extend-path call may succeed without appending only in the explicit `blocked_no_growth` case. Structural failures such as no legal forward suffix, invalid reverse, or missing/inactive path still return `success=False`.
- On failure, `success = False`, `path_id = None`, `resource_sequence = None`, all target fields are `None`, `result_kind = None`, `failure_kind` is required, and `reason` is required.
- `reason` is human-readable diagnostic text for create/extend failures. Callers may display or log it, but they must not branch on exact string equality in v1; control flow branches only on `success`, `result_kind`, and `failure_kind`.

**`cancel_path()` return contract**

- **PathCancellationKind canonical home.** Define `PathCancellationKind` here once as the diagnostic cancellation enum with v1 values `OPERATOR_CANCELLED`, `REVOKED`, `EB`, `OUT_OF_SEQUENCE`, `AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT`, and `POWER_OFF`. All other sections refer back to this declaration by name and must not redeclare values divergently.
- `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` returns a small result object with exactly `success: bool`, `path_id: Optional[str]`, `new_status: Optional[PathStatus]`, `cancellation_kind: Optional[PathCancellationKind]`, and `reason: Optional[str]`.
- On success, `success = True`, `path_id` is required, `new_status = CANCELLED`, `cancellation_kind` is the enum value applied to that path, and `reason` is optional plain-text diagnostic context.
- On failure, `success = False`, `path_id = None`, `new_status = None`, `cancellation_kind = None`, and `reason` is required.
- Failure cases are explicit in v1:
  - missing path -> failure with a plain-text reason such as `"path not found"`
  - already `COMPLETED` path -> failure with reason `"already completed"`
  - already `CANCELLED` path -> failure with reason `"already cancelled"`
- When the path is currently `ACTIVE`, `cancel_path(...)` performs the normal cancellation flow described below and returns success. Callers may display or log `reason`, but they must never branch on exact reason text.

**Resource acquisition**

- Acquire switches first, then blocks, so switch conflicts are discovered before any blocks are reserved. In `best_effort`, dispatcher still uses this switch-first probe to decide the storable prefix: walk the chosen candidate's future switch resources in path order, acquiring main and flank claims until the first switch/flank acquisition failure. If a switch acquisition fails, roll back any switch claims already acquired for that probe, truncate the candidate to the previous block before that failed switch, and then attempt the block reservations for that block-terminated prefix. If every switch in the candidate probe succeeds but block reservation later fails, use the first failing block as the truncation point, roll back, and retry the block-terminated prefix before that block. The final stored prefix is then acquired atomically using the same switch-first, blocks-second order. If that final prefix contains no storable block authority for `create_path(...)`, the create fails; for `extend_path(...)`, no appended prefix may still become `blocked_no_growth` when the existing path remains a valid blocked prefix.
- For each switch in path order, call `switch.acquire_lock(path_id, "main", train_id, required_state)` and immediately acquire any `switch.flank_protection` locks. Each `flank_protection` entry `(neighbour_switch_id, blocking_state)` is acquired as `neighbour_switch.acquire_lock(path_id, "flank", train_id, safe_state)`, where `safe_state` is the neighbour's state whose active segment does **not** point toward the primary switch's track — i.e. the state that keeps the neighbour's diverging path aimed away, preventing a head-on approach. `safe_state` is computable from switch geometry: it is the neighbour state whose selected branch exit edge is not the edge facing the primary switch. In v1, because switches are binary, `safe_state` is simply the opposite of the neighbour's `blocking_state` recorded at placement time. Every acquired flank claim must store `source_switch_id = switch.switch_id` so later switch head-entry can convert only the flank claims sourced from that main switch into train-owned protection.
- Acquire blocks atomically through `BlockManager.reserve_blocks_for_path(...)`.
- Resource acquisition should write `Block.reserved_direction` through `BlockManager.reserve_blocks_for_path(...)` for every reserved block. Tile-level traversal direction is carried by `resource_sequence[*].entry_edge` / `exit_edge` and nested `path_tiles[*].entry_edge` / `exit_edge`; there is no separate `TrackSegment.path_direction` field in the desired model.
- Distinguish retained geometry, static allowed travel, and temporary reservation direction: v1 does not define a track-level `traffic_direction_policy` field. Static allowed travel direction lives on `Block.allowed_travel_directions`; `Block.reserved_direction` is only the dispatcher-owned temporary reservation direction for one path; and the path plan geometry carries the finer-grained per-tile traversal frame inside that block.
- On failure, roll back in reverse acquisition order, including switch locks and block reservations. There is no separate tile-level `path_direction` assignment to clear.
- That rollback releases the acquired claims/reservations only; it does not restore an earlier physical `Switch.state`. The live switch alignment remains at the last successfully written value until some later switch command or path acquisition changes it again.
- Replace direct `block.release()` calls in completion and cancellation with `BlockManager.release_path_block_reservations(...)`.
- In v1, block reservations remain single-train/single-path resources even though `Switch.locks` can have multiple compatible owners. `Block.reserved_direction` therefore has at most one active value per reserved block and should be cleared when that block reservation is released.
- `create_path(..., acquisition_mode: PathDispatcher.AcquisitionMode)` and `extend_path(..., acquisition_mode: PathDispatcher.AcquisitionMode)` must share the same `PathDispatcher.AcquisitionMode` enum, with two v1 behaviors:
  - `AcquisitionMode.all_or_nothing`: dispatcher acquires only the full requested authority or returns failure after full rollback
  - `AcquisitionMode.best_effort`: dispatcher may truncate to the furthest forward acquirable resource prefix, but the acquisition of that chosen prefix is still atomic
- In `best_effort` mode, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` must either create a block-terminated live path record or fail; it never reports no-growth success. Same-block / local movement authorities may still create a one-block synthetic authority when the requested movement has a valid explicit traversal frame. `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` may report no-growth success only as `success=True/result_kind=blocked_no_growth`, and only when the existing live path remains a valid prefix toward the caller's current requested target while the next forward resource is temporarily blocked by live occupancy/reservation/lock conflict. That result updates the path's stored requested target contract and repushes the same-path controller plan. Any other extend attempt that appends no resource fails and leaves the existing path unchanged.
- The special head-on-switch case remains a failure because v1 does not allow storing a head-on-switch live authority end.
- In all acquisition modes, the stored live authority end must be block-terminated. A same-tick acquisition result that reaches a switch but no following block is not a valid stored live authority in v1; dispatcher must roll that trailing switch/flank acquisition back before storing or pushing the path.

**Controller-mediated progress**

- `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` should compute the next `TrainSpan`, next `current_speed_kmh`, and the ordered tile-level delta for that movement, then call `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` at least once every tick. `TrainPhysics` does not call dispatcher progress, completion, or release APIs directly and does not own the stored span.
- `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` should atomically write `span_update.new_span` and `span_update.current_speed_kmh` into controller-owned storage, then detect newly entered blocks, newly entered switches, tail-vacated blocks, and tail-vacated switches by converting `span_update.added_tiles` and `span_update.removed_tiles` through network tile/resource lookup, `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` when authority exists, and current traversal context. It then enqueues one ordered `ControllerProgressReport` into that train's Phase 3 mailbox. Stop completion is not reported from this method; it is evaluated separately later in that same train's Phase 3 consume pass from the post-move controller state.
- The controller progress report payload carries exactly `train_id`, `event_time`, `entered_resources`, `tail_vacated_resources`, `current_head_sequence_index`, `deviation`, `deviation_kind`, and `deviation_reason`. It does not carry `path_id`; `PathDispatcher` resolves active authority from `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`. `current_head_sequence_index` is the index of the train head's current position in `received_path_plan.resource_sequence`, or `None` when no active resource-sequence mapping is available. When live authority exists and the controller cannot map the head to that active `resource_sequence`, the report must set `deviation = true`, `deviation_kind = "out_of_sequence"`, and `current_head_sequence_index = None`; dispatcher treats that report as deviation/cancellation input rather than as ordinary active-path progress. `deviation_kind` is the structured deviation enum (`"out_of_sequence"` or `"beyond_authorized_target"`), and `deviation_reason` is diagnostic plain text only. Each resource event in `entered_resources` and `tail_vacated_resources` carries `sequence_index` when known, `resource_type`, and `resource_id`; block tail-vacate events additionally carry `direction`, where `direction` is the vacated block's forward `exit_edge` (`N`/`E`/`S`/`W`) for the traversal by which the train left that block. For a single report, dispatcher processing order is entered resources first, then deviation cancellation if `report.deviation == true`, then tail-vacated resources.
- Phase 3 is the only normal consumer of these reports. It drains `queued_progress_reports` in order and calls `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` for each one. Within that same drain, each route head-advance callback compares its single reported block advance with the current next waypoint; if that callback consumes a waypoint, the following queued callback is what may discover and test the next waypoint. After queued progress reports have been consumed and any movement-derived deviation cancellation from them has already run, if an active path still exists for that train, `SimulationController` runs the post-physics usable-authority stop check against the controller's current state and, on success, samples the current system Unix timestamp as `event_time` before calling `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)`. Explicit latched teardown such as `pending_cancel_request` or `pending_power_off` is consumed only after that normal completion chance.
- The controller report carries `deviation_kind` as a structured diagnostic for the dispatcher / route callback chain, but v1 route progression does not branch on it. Once the active path has been cancelled in Phase 3, the next eligible Phase 1 always returns to the ordinary covered-waypoint normalization plus the Phase-1 target-arm step flow from the train's current physical position; already-covered pass-through handling and stop handling follow the normal waypoint-kind rules.
- When physical movement crosses multiple resource boundaries within one simulation tick, `TrainPhysics` must fire multiple sequential `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` calls — one per resource boundary crossed, in physical traversal order — rather than batching them into a single multi-resource report. `TrainPhysics` computes each incremental `span_update` up to the next boundary, samples `time.time()` for that boundary event, calls `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` with that system-time Unix timestamp, lets `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` enqueue that step's report, then computes the next boundary update and repeats. After the last boundary in that tick, or on a tick with no boundary crossing, `TrainPhysics` must still emit one final callback for that tick. When no resource boundary was crossed, that final update carries `added_tiles=[]` and `removed_tiles=[]`; when nothing changed physically, it may be an explicit no-change callback. This guarantees that Phase 3 always runs, that latches such as `pending_power_off` have a deterministic consume point, and that downstream side effects (lock consumption, tail-vacate release, route head-advance callbacks, switch state notifications) are later committed in the same physical order as the queued boundary crossings. Within any single `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` call, `entered_resources` and `tail_vacated_resources` therefore typically carry one entry each at most; if a single call legitimately needs to carry more (for example simultaneous head entry and tail vacate at the same boundary), events within each list must still be ordered by physical traversal / vacate order, and dispatcher still processes the entered list before the vacated list.
- Entry ownership is resource-typed and dispatcher-mediated:
  - on block entry, `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` calls `BlockManager.occupy_blocks([block_id], train_id)`; this is the only normal runtime path that writes `Block.occupied_by`
  - on clean switch entry, `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` sets `Switch.occupied_by = train_id`, consumes the path-owned main switch lock, and converts applicable flank locks into train-owned protection; if another train already occupies the switch, this is the hard-failure conflict case and must not overwrite the prior owner
- Dispatcher progress should update `Path.current_resource_index` using the reported path-step `sequence_index`, not by searching for the first matching raw `resource_id`. `Path.current_resource_index` is head-based progress only; it advances when the train head enters a new resource and does not wait for the later tail-based release of earlier resources.
- A live path should remain `ACTIVE` after creation, through intermediate resources, and after entering the final block/resource. Entering the terminal resource should not change lifecycle state by itself.
- Only Phase 3 may attempt dispatcher path completion. After queued progress reports have been consumed and any movement-derived deviation handling from those reports has already run, if an active path still exists for that train, `SimulationController` runs the post-physics usable-authority stop check against the controller's current state. That check is true only when all of the following are true: the controller has live authority, the current authority has been accepted for departure (`departure_ready_path_id == received_path_plan.path_id`), the selected cab/travel frame is heading in the active authority frame toward the current authority end, `TrainController.is_stopped()` is true, `TrainController.eb_active == False`, and the distance-0 lookahead has `atc_limit == 0` with `atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"`. The controller may satisfy that authority-stop predicate either because the train has physically progressed through the final `resource_sequence` entry or because the remaining projected authority distance is too short to grant additional positive clearance after applying the controller safety margin (for example, a very short terminal block is smaller than the margin, so ATC cannot safely command entry all the way to that block edge). It does not require a route-authored centimeter stop target, physical head occupancy in the requested destination block, a requested-destination block comparison, or a second exact-position check. If the post-physics controller stop check is true, Phase 3 samples the current system Unix timestamp as `event_time` and calls `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)`. `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)` still owns the path-level acceptance check: the path completes only when the active `Path.authority_end_block_id == Path.requested_destination_block_id` and the terminal authority block entry's computed block-relative traversal direction equals `Path.requested_destination_direction`; otherwise the path remains live and route-side extension/retry logic continues. `departure_ready_path_id` is the single canonical depart-acceptance gate for route/API departure and for stop-completion eligibility; it is set only by a successful `TrainController.depart()` acceptance for the current `received_path_plan.path_id` and is cleared whenever the current authority is replaced, completed, cancelled, EB-latched, or powered off. Manual driving input may still command motion before `departure_ready_path_id` is set, but such movement is protected by the same ATC lookahead limits and cannot produce dispatcher path completion until `TrainController.depart()` has accepted that authority. This depart gate applies only to stop-completion eligibility. Head-entry route progress, including `task_kind == "waypoint"` consumption through `RouteExecutor.handle_train_advanced(...)`, is not depart gated. This unifies stop completion across ATO, manual, and any explicit dispatcher-owned same-block / stationary repositioning move: any train that has been told to depart and has stopped at the end of usable authority may be eligible for dispatcher completion; a train that never departed cannot. Explicit latched admin teardown such as `pending_cancel_request` and `pending_power_off` is consumed only after this normal completion chance.
- Raw `atc_limit == 0` is not enough for stop completion. No-cab, no-authority, EB, obstruction/conflict, wrong-direction cab, and truncated best-effort authority are not stop completion, even if the distance-0 ATC limit is zero.
- For a stop waypoint whose live authority already reaches its requested target block and direction, a post-physics controller stop at the safety-margin-adjusted usable authority stop completes the path normally. The head may be short of the physical requested block when the safety margin/ATC boundary requires that; ATC remains the higher-priority rule and there is no "go back and try again" prompt for a within-block or before-block safety-margin stop.
- EB activation records `pending_cancel_request` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.EB` when a live path exists, and that request is committed by the same train's Phase 3 consume pass through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)`. While EB is latched, the post-physics usable-authority stop check must fail; after EB release any route continuation uses a fresh path request.
- Controller-reported deviation maps to two diagnostic cancellation kinds: `OUT_OF_SEQUENCE` (wrong resource / cannot map head) and `AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT` (beyond authorized target). Cancellation wins over any same-tick completion attempt. Recovery follows the canonical recovery table; both kinds share the same recovery shell. A `task_kind == "waypoint"` target the train has entered, passed, or later covers counts as served and does not produce a separate overshot-waypoint deviation.
- A best-effort truncated path must not complete just because the train has stopped at the current authority boundary while the current authorized target is still short of the requested target. In that case the same path remains `ACTIVE`, the controller may enter `RUNNING_WAITING` under the still-live authority, and `RouteExecutor` should keep trying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on that same `path_id`.
- Completed paths should release only not-yet-entered block reservations through `BlockManager.release_path_block_reservations(..., completed=True)`, because reservations for already-entered blocks were consumed on head entry. That reservation release may force-clear terminal `CAUTION` only for matching still-reserved owned blocks, but it must not force-clear any physical `OCCUPIED` block early. On this `ACTIVE -> COMPLETED` transition, dispatcher follows the **Active-path completion procedure** ordering. The retained record may remain in `paths_by_id` for history, but physical release of a switch or block still under the train resolves from the resource itself. When a later tail-vacate resource event arrives through `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)`, it clears `Switch.occupied_by` / block occupancy and any train-owned switch protection through the resource-specific release path. `RouteExecutor` treats the dispatcher callback as authoritative completion and does not re-check safety-margin projection itself.
- Remove `_record_completion_event()`, `completed_path_events`, and `get_and_clear_completion_events()`.
- When creating or extending a path, resolve and store the requested target contract on the dispatcher-owned `Path` record first, then set the current authorized target and push `path_id`, `resource_sequence`, and `authority_end_block_id` to the controller via `received_path_plan`:
  - if the live authority already reaches the requested target, `authority_end_block_id == Path.requested_destination_block_id` and the terminal block entry's computed block-relative traversal direction equals `Path.requested_destination_direction`; the controller sees only `authority_end_block_id` in the pushed plan
  - if the live authority is still truncated, `authority_end_block_id` is the current temporary end of `resource_sequence`; the requested target fields remain on the `Path` record only
- Drive resource release from controller-reported tail-vacate observations. `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` should inspect `resource_type` and release it accordingly:
  - vacated blocks go through `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated(block_id, train_id, direction)`
  - vacated switches go through the `Switch` entity after `PathDispatcher` resolves the vacating `train_id` for that `switch_id` from switch state (`clear Switch.occupied_by`, then clear train-owned protection for that source switch through `Switch.release_train_protection_from(source_switch_id=switch_id, train_id=train_id)` on each protected neighbour)
- Route any normal block release through `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated()` so `OCCUPIED -> CAUTION -> FREE` remains a single-manager state machine. Manual/admin reservation cleanup uses the same `BlockManager.release_path_block_reservations(...)` entry point as routine completion/cancellation release; in the desired model both `clear_block()` and `release_block()` are removed.

**Path extension**

- Every `TrainController.receive_path_plan(...)` call (including `blocked_no_growth` repush where geometry is unchanged) must clear the controller lookahead cache, regardless of which fields actually changed. Stale lookahead must never survive a path-plan push.
- A change to `Block.allowed_travel_directions` (static config edit) does **not** invalidate any already-active path's reservations; the live path's `reserved_direction` was set at acquisition time and remains valid for the life of that path. The new direction policy takes effect only at the next `BlockManager.reserve_blocks_for_path(...)` call. Operator/admin code must not edit `allowed_travel_directions` to indirectly cancel a live path; cancellation goes through `pending_cancel_request`.
- Truncated best-effort authority where the train physically stops at the temporary boundary while no resolved target is armed (the one-tick gap between task-completion and the next Phase 1 target arm) is permissible: the controller transitions to `RUNNING_WAITING` against the still-live truncated authority. The `RUNNING_WAITING` entry rule is relaxed to "live authority exists, train stopped, distance-0 ATC clearance is zero," removing the prior gate that required an armed target to be set.
- Keep forward-only extension. **`RouteExecutor` must call `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` only with the same `(requested_destination_block_id, requested_destination_direction)` pair as the currently armed `RouteRun.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`.** Any other target is a route-side invariant violation and must be expressed via cancel-and-replan, never via extend with a different requested target.
- When the path model switches to `resource_sequence`, append new tiles and new resources while skipping duplicated connection/start entries.
- Reject any extension that would cause a resource to appear twice in the combined path.
- `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` should take the same argument contract as `create_path(...)`, except that it identifies the existing live path by `path_id`: `path_id`, `requested_destination_block_id`, `requested_destination_direction`, and `acquisition_mode`. The live path identified by `path_id` is the fixed already-acquired prefix; `extend_path(...)` grows that existing path forward from its current authority end toward the caller-supplied requested target for this extend attempt. Dispatcher does not run a separate shared-walk prefix-compatibility test. If the requested extension cannot continue forward from the existing live path without revisiting an already-held resource or otherwise violating the ordinary path rules, `extend_path(...)` fails and leaves the existing path unchanged.
- The shared request contract between `extend_path(...)` and `create_path(...)` is the same, but `extend_path(...)` adds extra constraints: it requires a live `path_id`, and the newly appended suffix must continue forward from that live path's current authority-end edge in the same travel direction the existing prefix was built in. The caller-supplied `requested_destination_direction` applies only to the new terminal block `requested_destination_block_id`; it is not a license to reverse at the current authority end. A `requested_destination_direction` that would require the train to reverse 180 degrees at the current authority end (for example, the requested new terminal block lies behind the current authority-end edge on the existing prefix's travel frame, or reaching it would require exiting the authority-end block backward out of its current `exit_edge`) is invalid for `extend_path(...)` and must be rejected with `success = False`, `failure_kind = invalid_reverse`, and a diagnostic `reason` such as `"extend would reverse at authority end"`. The existing live path must remain unchanged in that rejection. Callers that actually need the train to turn back at the current terminal must cancel the existing path and issue a fresh `create_path(...)` from the train's current span instead.
- `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` must return `success=False` with `failure_kind = missing_or_inactive_path` and a required `reason` when `path_id` does not exist or when the referenced path is not currently `ACTIVE`.
- In `all_or_nothing` mode, `extend_path(...)` should either acquire the full requested suffix to `requested_destination_block_id` or leave the existing path unchanged. Any outcome that cannot reach the requested target is `success=False`; use `failure_kind = blocked_before_target` when the suffix exists but cannot be fully acquired because of live occupancy/reservation/lock conflict, and `failure_kind = no_viable_path` when no legal suffix exists.
- In `best_effort` mode, `extend_path(...)` should keep the same `path_id` and append the furthest forward acquirable suffix it can add to the current path. The `resource_sequence` therefore grows in place on the same live path instead of creating a new path. When at least one resource is appended, update the stored requested target fields on that same `Path` to the caller-supplied contract for that attempt, even if the live authority remains truncated short of it, and return `result_kind = truncated_grew` or `reached_requested_target` as appropriate. If no resource can be appended but the dispatcher can prove the existing path is still a legal prefix to the requested target and the next resource is blocked only by live occupancy/reservation/lock conflict, return `success=True` with `result_kind = blocked_no_growth`, update the existing path's stored requested target fields to the caller-supplied contract for that attempt, leave `resource_sequence` and `authority_end_block_id` unchanged, repush the refreshed `received_path_plan` for the same `path_id`, and let `RouteExecutor` retry later. If no resource can be appended because no legal forward suffix exists, return `success=False` with `failure_kind = no_viable_path` and leave the existing path unchanged.
- While the path is still truncated short of the caller's current requested target, set `authority_end_block_id` to the current last acquired block.
- Every successful `extend_path(...)` updates the `Path` record and repushes `path_id`, `resource_sequence`, and `authority_end_block_id` through `TrainController.receive_path_plan(...)`. `truncated_grew` / `reached_requested_target` carry the grown `resource_sequence`; `blocked_no_growth` carries the unchanged geometry. When the live authority finally reaches the requested target, `authority_end_block_id` equals `Path.requested_destination_block_id` in the repush; the requested target fields remain on the `Path` record only and are not included in the controller push.
- In `best_effort` mode, a no-growth extend result is therefore branchable without parsing `reason`: `success=True/result_kind=blocked_no_growth` means "valid prefix, temporarily blocked; keep the live path and retry later," while `success=False/failure_kind=no_viable_path` or `invalid_reverse` means "the existing live path cannot continue to this requested target; RouteExecutor must latch cancellation before any fresh create-path attempt."

**Cancellation**

- `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` is the single canonical cancellation entry point in v1. `cancellation_kind` is recorded for diagnostics and propagated through the cancellation callback for logs/UI only; it must not be used as a condition by any planning or routing logic. `reason` is plain-text diagnostic context for logs/UI only and likewise must not be used for condition branching. Recovery branching is driven by controller state, runtime latches (`eb_active`, `pending_power_off`, `received_path_plan`), current span coverage, and the normal waypoint-kind rules, not by the cancellation enum.
- Release owned blocks through `BlockManager.release_path_block_reservations(...)` instead of direct `block.release()` calls. In the desired v1 model, cancellation release clears future reservation ownership only; it never clears physical occupancy.
- Path cancellation or revocation effects are owned entirely by `PathDispatcher`, and the cancellation commit happens only in Phase 3. `RouteExecutor` or `TrainController` may request cancellation only by recording controller-owned Phase-3 handoff state such as `pending_cancel_request`; they must not call `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)`, clear authority, or release resources directly from Phase 1 or Phase 2. If Phase 1 route logic decides that the current live path must be revoked, it records or refreshes that cancel request and then stops planning for that train for that tick; later eligible Phase 1 passes follow the same latch-only rule while the incompatible live path still exists. Only after the same train's later Phase 3 consumes that request, `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` completes, and the ordinary cancellation callback clears route-side live-path correlation may a later eligible Phase 1 resume ordinary create/extend planning. When Phase 3 later commits the cancellation through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)`, dispatcher clears controller authority by calling `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`.
- Power-off uses this same dispatcher cancellation workflow, but in the phase model `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` is a stopped-only controller command that validates acceptance during the tick-entry command drain inside `TrainController.tick(...)` and sets `pending_power_off = True`; it does not run route/dispatcher teardown inline. The later eligible Phase 3 cleanup that consumes `pending_power_off` cancels any active path through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(active_path_id, cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.POWER_OFF, reason="power off")` while the train's `RouteRun` is still registered, lets the ordinary cancellation callback path run (including `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(...)` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.POWER_OFF`), then calls `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(train_id, reason="power_off")`, and only after route/dispatcher teardown performs controller-local OFF cleanup such as clearing cab, EB latch, `eb_released`, `departure_confirmation_required`, `departure_ready_path_id`, `pending_power_off`, and `ato_running`. If `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` is empty when power-off cleanup runs, the `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` step is skipped and Phase 3 proceeds straight to `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(train_id, reason="power_off")`; it must not invent a separate OFF-specific release path.
- On the `ACTIVE -> CANCELLED` transition, `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` runs synchronously and must finish lower-layer teardown before it makes the path externally non-active. Cancellation uses the same canonical terminal-teardown order as completion: release not-yet-entered block reservation ownership, release not-yet-entered switch/flank locks, clear controller authority by calling `TrainController.receive_path_plan(None)`, then mark the path `CANCELLED` and clear `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`. Only after that ordered teardown is complete may `cancel_path(...)` return success and deliver the route-side cancellation callback. Cancellation clears future reservation/authority only; it never clears `Block.occupied_by`, `Block._is_caution`, `Block.caution_direction`, `Switch.occupied_by`, or train-owned switch protection. A block reservation may be cleared even when the train still physically occupies that block because reservation ownership and physical occupancy are independent, but in normal flow same-train reservations for entered blocks have already been consumed on head entry. A same-train new path is allowed once the old path is no longer `ACTIVE`, subject to ordinary live resource conflicts and train-owned physical protection.
- Future switch/flank release for both `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` and `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)` is computed by scanning the whole `Path.resource_sequence`, not by retaining a separate path-level flank list and not by stopping at `Path.current_resource_index`. For each switch resource in the sequence, `PathDispatcher` attempts to release any still-future main switch lock claim owned by that `path_id`; that release is idempotent and no-ops when head entry already consumed the claim into `Switch.occupied_by` / train-owned protection. For each same switch resource, `PathDispatcher` also inspects the switch's current flank-protection configuration and releases matching still-future flank lock claims owned by that `path_id` on each protected neighbour switch, keyed by `(path_id, claim_role="flank", source_switch_id=<main switch id>)`. This scan clears only path-owned future lock claims. It must not clear `Switch.occupied_by` or `Switch.train_protection_claims`; those remain physical/train-owned and are released only by later controller-reported switch tail-vacate.
- Reservation / physical state ownership in v1 should be read literally:
  - dispatcher-owned future reservation state: `Block.reserved_by_path`, `Block.reserved_for_train`, `Block.reserved_direction`, `Switch.locks`, flank-protection lock ownership for resources the train has not entered yet
  - block physical state owned by `BlockManager` and mutated only through `BlockManager` entry/tail-vacate APIs invoked by `PathDispatcher` on controller-reported observations: `Block.occupied_by`, `Block._is_caution`, `Block.caution_direction`
  - dispatcher-mediated switch physical state, written by `PathDispatcher` on controller-reported resource-entry / tail-vacate observations rather than directly by `TrainPhysics`: `Switch.occupied_by` and `Switch.train_protection_claims`
  - a block or switch has at most one physical `occupied_by` train at a time (single-train physical ownership). A single train may hold multiple path records layered over that physical ownership; path membership is tracked separately by `PathDispatcher` on `Path` records, not by duplicating `occupied_by` per path.
  - cancellation / completion release clears only future block reservations and future switch locks that have not already been consumed by head entry
  - only controller-reported physical entry / tail-vacate observations may clear or set `BlockManager`-owned block physical state or dispatcher-mediated switch physical state
  - once a resource is physically occupied, later tail-vacate clears physical occupancy and train-owned protection rather than path-owned residue
- After cancellation clears `received_path_plan`, the controller follows the no-authority braking path defined in the **Controller tick order** (rebuild lookahead to the occupied-block boundary; service-brake to zero, escalating to EB if insufficient; on stop, transition to `IDLE` / `STANDBY`). Physics keeps integrating live network state during that brake; cancellation does not unlock a physically occupied switch.
- After cancelling a live path, the runtime should deliver the canonical cancellation context `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(train_id, path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` only after `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` has finished its own lower-layer mutation for that train. Controller-side `deviation_kind` is normalized inside `PathDispatcher` before this callback and does not cross the dispatcher/route boundary. This notification is mandatory, but the free-form `reason` string is informational only. `RouteExecutor` should inspect whether that cancelled path actually affects its current waypoint / assigned route state, must not treat the old path as finished, and must defer all next-target selection / fresh authority assignment until the next eligible Phase 1 after the stopped no-authority or post-EB recovery point. The enum values are:
  - `PathCancellationKind.OPERATOR_CANCELLED` for ordinary operator/requested cancellation
  - `PathCancellationKind.REVOKED` for dispatcher/resource-loss revocation
  - `PathCancellationKind.EB` for emergency-brake cancellation
  - `PathCancellationKind.OUT_OF_SEQUENCE` for controller-reported wrong-resource / cannot-map-head deviation
  - `PathCancellationKind.AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT` for controller-reported beyond-authorized-target overshoot
  - `PathCancellationKind.POWER_OFF` for the train-power-off release case initiated by `TrainController.power_off_cmd()`

**Path invariants**

- In the desired model, old `PENDING` and `SETTING` disappear entirely because acquisition is synchronous inside `create_path()`, and old `SET`, old `ACTIVE`, and old `CLEARING` collapse into a single live dispatcher state: `ACTIVE`. Whether the train has started moving, entered the terminal resource, or is stopped at the stop point should be represented by controller state, `current_resource_index`, and explicit completion predicates, not by separate path lifecycle values.
- `resource_sequence` replaces both `block_sequence` and `switch_positions`, so existing callers iterating those fields need updating.
- `current_resource_index` should track `sequence_index`, not the first occurrence of a raw resource ID.
- For v1, repeated resources in a single path are invalid and should be rejected during both `create_path()` and `extend_path()`.
- Switch resources contribute `0 cm` to forward-distance accumulation. Any helper that sums per-resource physical length should therefore add `0` for `resource_type == "switch"` and continue the cumulative axis across the surrounding block geometry.
- `FLANK_PROTECTION_DISTANCE_CM` should be a global config, not a per-path setting.
- In v1, switch locks are a multi-owner compatibility set: multiple paths may hold compatible same-state lock claims on the same switch, but conflicting required states are not allowed and physical occupancy stays single-owner.
- Remove `PathDispatcher._release_blocks_behind_train()` in the desired model. Once block release is fully owned by `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated()` triggered from physics, the dispatcher no longer needs a separate behind-train sweep.

## Route And RouteExecutor

**Route data**

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `route_id` | `str` | Unique route identifier |
| `name` | `str` | Display name |
| `tasks` | `List[RouteTask]` | Ordered route definition — see **RouteTask data** |
| `mode` | `RouteMode` | One of `ONE_OFF`, `CIRCULAR`, `ROUND_TRIP` |
| `assigned_train_ids` | `List[str]` | Trains assigned to the route |
| `is_active` | `bool` | Route execution enabled |
| `started_at` | `Optional[float]` | System-time Unix timestamp when activated |
| `route_version` | `int` | Monotonic shared-route revision incremented on any route edit that changes waypoint order or execution policy |
| `reconciliation_policy` | `"nearest_valid_target" \| "restart_from_top"` | How active runs reconcile against later shared-route edits (default `"nearest_valid_target"`) |
| `priority` | `int` | Route-executor scheduling priority, `0..9` where `9` is highest and `0` is lowest (default `5`) |
| `default_departure_mode` | `"auto" \| "manual"` | Ordinary departure policy (default `"auto"`) |
| `acquisition_mode` | `PathDispatcher.AcquisitionMode` | Default route path-acquisition policy (default `AcquisitionMode.best_effort`) |

**Canonical ownership split**

- `Route` is the shared plan definition only: ordered tasks plus route-level policy such as mode, departure defaults, and acquisition mode. It does not own per-train mutable execution cursors.
- A route may be assigned to multiple trains in v1, but each train may execute at most one route task at a time. Each assigned train owns exactly one `RouteRun`, keyed by `train_id`; the run's `route_id` field identifies which shared `Route` definition that train is currently following. That `RouteRun` is the per-train runtime instance of the shared plan and owns only per-train mutable execution state: task cursor, current traversal direction, per-task timing and resolved-target state (`task_runs`), route-version reconciliation state, and current route status. Shared route execution state across trains is not allowed.
- `Route` is live shared execution input in v1, not a snapshot source that is copied into each run. Active runs read the current shared `Route` through `RouteRun.route_id`, and later route edits are consumed through `Route.route_version` plus `Route.reconciliation_policy`.
- In helper prose below, when an active-run helper still shows a `route` parameter, read that as the current shared `Route` resolved from `RouteRun.route_id` unless the paragraph explicitly says a new dedicated route assignment is being created.
- Because multi-train assignment is supported, `RouteRun` is the only canonical runtime progress store. Remove route-level runtime summary fields such as `Route.current_waypoint_index` and `Route.direction` from the target model for now; the future UI summary shape is intentionally left open rather than being baked into the refactor contract.
- Per-train route/path errors belong to `RouteRun.last_error` and `RouteRun.error_time`; `Route` does not keep a shared last-writer-wins error summary in v1.
- Add `default_departure_mode` with two v1 values: `auto` and `manual`, defaulting to `auto` when omitted on the shared `Route`. `RouteExecutor` reads the current shared `Route.default_departure_mode` each time it evaluates departure for an active run.
- Do not add an EB-specific route-level departure override in v1. EB release uses the general controller-owned `departure_confirmation_required` latch: while that latch is set, the next departure decision is treated as manual-confirmation-required even if the shared route or waypoint would otherwise auto-depart. The same general latch can be reused by any future safety workflow that requires a one-shot explicit confirmation before movement resumes.
- Add `acquisition_mode` at route level as the policy for how this route wants dispatcher authority to be acquired (`all_or_nothing` or `best_effort`), with default `AcquisitionMode.best_effort` when the route definition omits it. `RouteExecutor` reads the current shared `Route.acquisition_mode` for both `create_path(...)` and later `extend_path(...)` retries on the same planning chain.
- Add `priority` at route level as the canonical route-executor scheduling priority, with default `5`. Priority values are integers in `0..9`, where `9` is highest and `0` is lowest. `RouteExecutor` sorts runnable route runs by the current shared `Route.priority`; later shared-route edits may therefore legitimately reshuffle later tick order for active runs. Non-route and one-off/manual train work uses effective priority `5` when `SimulationController` merges it into the per-tick runnable-train order.
- `ResolvedRouteTarget` carries exactly `requested_destination_block_id` and `requested_destination_direction`. `requested_destination_direction` in this structure is always the block-relative direction for `requested_destination_block_id`. `Platform` has no direction field, no platform-frame direction, and no direction-conversion helper in v1; station/platform resolution chooses a concrete platform-end block, then derives the resolved direction from `Block.ordered_tiles`, validates it against `Block.allowed_travel_directions`, and stores that block-relative result. There is no persistent per-task resolved-target cache outside `task_runs`: every new route-side path attempt resolves from the current shared `Route`, current platform availability, train span, and task policy. `RouteExecutor._arm_next_target(train_id)` is the only canonical writer of `RouteRun.task_runs[i].resolved_target`; v1 deliberately does not keep a separate target-refresh function. For a logical `station_id` task, `_arm_next_target` may also produce a transient ordered candidate list for the current Phase 1 pass, but it writes only the one currently attempted candidate into `task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`. If `create_path(...)` / `extend_path(...)` fails before authority is created or before `extend_path(...)` grows the live authority, a later retry reruns `_arm_next_target` and may re-enumerate station candidates. A concrete `platform_id` or direct `block_id` target retries the same target category on the later tick rather than trying same-tick alternatives.
