The canonical power-off release/removal order follows the standard phase workflow: `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` immediately clears `ato_running` and sets `pending_power_off = True` during the Phase-2 operator-command slot, and the later eligible Phase 3 cleanup consumes that flag by cancelling any live path through `PathDispatcher`, letting the ordinary cancellation callback run while route execution is still registered, then calling `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(train_id, reason="power_off")`, and finally clearing controller-local OFF state.
- If the next route task is `task_kind == "dwell"`, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` must treat dwell as the active route step before any departure/path planning. If controller state is `IDLE` or `STANDBY`, it writes `TrainController.dwell_entry_requested` and returns for that tick. If controller state is already `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, it returns without new path or departure planning. It must not normalize past the dwell task, arm the next non-dwell task, or request authority until the Phase-3 dwell-complete cleanup marks the `dwell` task finished.
- If controller state is `STANDBY` or `IDLE` and there is no pending/active dwell task, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` should call `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(train, route, exec_state)`. Path creation/retry is allowed from either state. The difference is only departure execution after authority exists: ATO may auto-select/start, while manual mode leaves actual movement to the driver.
- If controller state is `RUNNING_MOVING`, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` should not poll for `stop` task arrival; normal stop arrival comes only from dispatcher-committed path completion after the post-physics controller stop check has passed. `task_kind == "waypoint"` consumption is also not polled inside `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)`; it is driven by the head-advance callback chain `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` -> `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` -> `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` -> `RouteExecutor.handle_train_advanced(...)`. Each `RouteExecutor.handle_train_advanced(...)` callback compares one reported block advance with the current route task pointer and may consume at most that one task; it must not arm the next planning target or create/extend authority. `waypoint` consumption is head-based route progress only; the later physical release of earlier path resources still waits for tail-vacate callbacks. `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` in `RUNNING_MOVING` is therefore limited to best-effort `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` retries toward the currently armed target; it does not re-implement head-entry detection.
- If controller state is `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` should treat the run as dwelling/on hold and return without new path or departure planning. If an implementation keeps a helper such as `RouteExecutor._check_dwell_complete(train, route, exec_state)`, that helper is invoked from the same train's Phase 3 feedback/cleanup pass, not from `RouteExecutor.update(...)`.
- If controller state is `RUNNING_WAITING`, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` should treat the route as on hold and should not request a brand-new path while `received_path_plan` is still present. If that still-live path's `authority_end_block_id` is still short of the route-owned `task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target.requested_destination_block_id`, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` should keep retrying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on that same path using a fresh re-resolution of the same pending task's current requested target contract on each retry.
- More generally, dynamic best-effort extension is allowed in any controller state except `OFF`. `RUNNING_WAITING` is the common zero-clearance retry case, but if a still-live path remains short of its requested target while the train is still moving or otherwise stopped under authority, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` may proactively retry `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on later ticks without waiting for a state change, as long as it does not create a second live path.
- `RouteExecutor` does not call any stop-position resolver and does not pass stop centimeters into dispatcher. Route callers pass only the resolved block/direction request contract.
- `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` is the route executor's normal "make progress toward the current task" entry point. The same function is used for the initial departure attempt and for retries after `PathDispatcher` has cancelled/revoked the previous path once the train has reached a fresh stopped no-authority state. If the cancellation stop escalated into EB, that retry point occurs only after `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` has returned the controller to `IDLE` or `STANDBY` according to cab selection; active-path completion still comes back from `PathDispatcher`, not from `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` polling for end-of-path.
- `RouteExecutor` should resolve one effective departure policy for the current task each time it considers departure:
  - start from `Route.default_departure_mode`
  - override with `RouteTask.departure_mode` when present on the current `stop` task
  - if `TrainController.departure_confirmation_required` is true, override the resolved mode to `"manual"` for this fresh departure decision
  `RouteExecutor` writes `TrainController.departure_requested` if and only if the final `effective_departure_mode == "auto"` and live authority exists for the current route leg. The request is consumed by `TrainController.tick(...)` in Phase 2; Phase 1 does not call `TrainController.depart()` directly.
- If `TrainController.departure_confirmation_required` is true because EB was released, the EB-interrupted path has already been cancelled by the EB activation flow. `RouteExecutor` must wait for EB release, then make a fresh `create_path(...)` or `extend_path(...)` decision from the current route state; it must not try to reuse that cancelled path. Once fresh authority exists, auto departure is suppressed until an explicit departure confirmation queues or writes a `departure_requested` latch that `TrainController.tick(...)` accepts successfully.

- **Departure-to-waypoint flow**

- `RouteExecutor` owns task targeting and departure policy; `PathDispatcher` owns authority acquisition and terminal authority geometry; `TrainController` owns stop/braking behavior from that authority geometry using the controller safety margin. The departure flow is: resolve task block/direction, request or extend dispatcher authority as needed, and only then decide whether to write a controller-owned `departure_requested` latch.
- The stop, same-block, and dwell-entry rules in this subsection apply only to tasks with `task_kind == "stop"`. Tasks with `task_kind == "waypoint"` do not call arrival/dwell helpers; they constrain route geometry only.
- A `task_kind == "waypoint"` task uses the same block/direction resolution rules and the same point-to-point create/extend contract as a `stop` task. The difference is route behavior only: when that task's resolved target is reached, `RouteExecutor` advances immediately without dwell and leaves the next leg to the next planning tick.
- A longer multi-task route is implemented as repeated point-to-point segments across later planning ticks. A pathfinder call never receives "go through all remaining tasks"; after one task target has been resolved and physical route progress actually consumes that target, a later `create_path(...)` / `extend_path(...)` request is made from the then-current live authority end to the next resolved task. Physical route progress still waits for `waypoint` head entry or `stop` dispatcher completion.
- `RouteExecutor` owns task-chain progression on the route side. `PathDispatcher` grows authority geometry; it does not own task progression. A `waypoint` task is consumed only when the train head first enters that task's resolved block. Live `resource_sequence` coverage may be inspected for diagnostics or extension planning, but coverage alone does not advance `current_waypoint_index` and does not retarget dispatcher authority away from the currently armed task. Tail-based resource release is a separate physical-lifecycle concern owned by controller-reported tail-vacate observations and dispatcher-mediated resource release.
- For a train departing from rest under route control, `RouteExecutor` does not try to grow authority past the currently armed task inside the same departure call. Departure uses the current live authority only; later ticks may extend or recreate authority after physical route progress advances and the next task becomes the armed target.
- There is a route-side already-covered skip in v1 only for `task_kind == "waypoint"` targets. If the resolved target block for the current `waypoint` task is already physically covered by the train's current span, `RouteExecutor` treats that task as already missed/skipped, does not request a same-block `create_path(...)`, and immediately advances route planning toward the next task on the current directed route walk. For `task_kind == "stop"`, the block-coverage test is insufficient: a stop is served only by dispatcher-committed stop completion. If a stop's resolved destination block is already covered, the run keeps that stop as the armed target and requests or keeps same-block stop authority as needed; the controller's ordinary safety-margin authority-stop predicate decides completion. Operator/manual same-block reposition uses the ordinary one-off route flow as a `stop` task and is not converted into a covered-task skip.
- `RouteExecutor` does not resolve or compare stop-position targets at all. For any `stop` task that still needs authority, it passes only `requested_destination_block_id` and `requested_destination_direction`. Dispatcher stores authority geometry and the controller owns safety-margin braking from that geometry. The route/task resolution step must still provide `requested_destination_direction` as a route-level constraint; dispatcher must not guess it from same-block geometry, and must not treat it as the exact terminal frame by itself.
- `RouteExecutor` uses `Route.acquisition_mode` for both `create_path(...)` and any later `extend_path(...)` retries while it is still pursuing that same planning chain. `AcquisitionMode.best_effort` is therefore a route policy choice, not a one-off branch invented mid-chain.
- For a `waypoint` task, the create/extend chain is still block-based: the current per-attempt resolved target still carries `requested_destination_block_id` and `requested_destination_direction` just like a `stop` task. The difference is not the path API shape; the difference is that once the train head enters that task's resolved block, `RouteExecutor` consumes the `waypoint` immediately, advances the route cursor, and leaves the next create/extend step to a later planning tick.
- The canonical create-vs-extend decision for `RouteExecutor` is driven by `TrainController.received_path_plan` presence, not by any route-side re-evaluation of whether the current waypoint is reachable on an empty map. For the currently armed waypoint:
  - if `controller.received_path_plan is None`, the train has no live authority, so `RouteExecutor` must call `PathDispatcher.create_path(train_id=..., ...)`
  - if `controller.received_path_plan is not None`, the train is already on a live path, so `RouteExecutor` must call `PathDispatcher.extend_path(path_id=received_path_plan.path_id, ...)` on that same `path_id` rather than synthesizing a second path
  Because `PathDispatcher` is the sole writer of `received_path_plan` and clears it to `None` on cancellation, revocation, and completion release, this rule is well-defined in v1: a cancelled/completed path does not remain on the controller inbox, so the next planning tick after cancellation naturally falls into the `create_path(...)` branch. A pass-through waypoint at which the train happens to be stopped with no live plan is therefore a `create_path(...)` case, not an `extend_path(...)` case.
- When `TrainController.received_path_plan is not None`, `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` must treat that live authority as the current route path for this attempt and must not request a second `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` on top of it. `RouteExecutor` correlates callbacks by `train_id` and the callback-supplied `path_id`; it does not hold a route-side copy of the live path id. If the next route decision wants a subsequent waypoint target on the current directed route walk, `RouteExecutor` may present that new target on the next `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` for `received_path_plan.path_id`. If `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` returns `success=True/result_kind=blocked_no_growth`, the existing live path is still a valid prefix and `RouteExecutor` keeps it, stops Phase 1 for that train, and retries later. If `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` returns `success=False` with a structural `failure_kind` such as `no_viable_path` or `invalid_reverse`, dispatcher cannot continue the existing live path from its current authority end toward that requested target; `RouteExecutor` must latch `pending_cancel_request` for that same live `path_id`, stop Phase 1 for that train, and wait for the ordinary Phase 3 cancel -> callback -> stopped no-authority / post-EB recovery flow. Later eligible Phase 1 passes keep using that latch-only behavior while the incompatible live path still exists. Only after that cancellation flow has completed may a later eligible Phase 1 request a fresh path.
- If `PathDispatcher.create_path(..., acquisition_mode=best_effort)` returns a live path whose current authorized target is still short of the route's current planning target, that is not a failure. It is a degraded but valid live authority: let the controller treat it as normal movement authority toward the current authorized end, and let `RouteExecutor` keep retrying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on the same `path_id` toward that same pending waypoint, re-resolving its current request contract on each retry, on later ticks in any controller state except `OFF`. A best-effort create request counts as success whenever it creates one new path record with a non-empty block-terminated live `resource_sequence`; a best-effort extend request counts as success when it grows authority or returns `result_kind=blocked_no_growth` because the live path is still a valid prefix but temporarily blocked by live resource ownership.
- Once such a live path exists, `RouteExecutor` should still apply the ordinary departure policy by writing `TrainController.departure_requested` when this leg is otherwise route-ready and auto departure is allowed. Any immediate zero-clearance hold or `RUNNING_WAITING` outcome is controller-owned and comes from lookahead/ATC against that live authority rather than from a special route-side suppression rule.
- On successful path, do not write train-side path cache fields from `RouteExecutor`. `PathDispatcher` owns the authoritative path-progress view; any controller/UI-facing path tiles or path-progress reads should be pushed/read as a projection of dispatcher state rather than as a train mirror.
- Do not set destination-block fields directly on `Train` or `TrainController` from `RouteExecutor`; controller authority comes only from the pushed path plan.
- `PathDispatcher` owns `controller.received_path_plan`; `controller.assigned_path_id` should be only a derived read of `received_path_plan.path_id`, so `RouteExecutor` does not write that field.
- On successful path creation, authority growth, or `blocked_no_growth` target refresh, `RouteExecutor` should update `exec_state.current_route_status` for the immediate live target carried by that path. Later planning retries or reconciliation may re-resolve and rewrite that route-owned temporary status from the current shared `Route` and current physical progress only when the previous create/extend attempt failed to create authority, grow authority, or refresh the live path's requested target; callback handling should read the stored route-run status rather than trying to rediscover the path's route meaning after dispatcher state has already changed. Returned `resource_sequence` geometry alone does not advance `current_waypoint_index`, which remains physical route progress, and v1 does not arm a second waypoint target on the same tick.
- After any successful `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` that returns a grown `resource_sequence`, `RouteExecutor` updates route-owned temporary target status for that same currently armed waypoint only. If the grown path is still short of that target, `RouteExecutor` keeps the same `target_waypoint_index` and re-resolves that same pending target later. If the grown path now reaches that target, `RouteExecutor` still keeps the same `target_waypoint_index` until physical route progress consumes the waypoint through head-entry callback, dispatcher-committed stop completion, or explicit skip. The result payload by itself does not advance physical route progress; `current_waypoint_index` still advances only when the train actually reaches/consumes that waypoint.
- Keep `TrainController.depart()` as the controller's Phase-2 departure-acceptance command once a valid path plan exists. `RouteExecutor` and explicit operator departure confirmation reach it by writing `departure_requested`; `TrainController.tick(...)` consumes that latch and runs the acceptance logic. In ATO it may begin movement immediately; in manual mode it marks the current authority ready to go and movement still begins through later driver input.
- When `TrainController.tick(...)` consumes `departure_requested` and the `TrainController.depart()` acceptance result is `success == False`, it must treat that as a failed departure handoff, not as movement. Leave the train in its current stopped controller state, record or emit the diagnostic `reason` through `NotificationCenter`, and let the next planning tick re-evaluate from current controller state and `received_path_plan` presence. If authority was cleared before the request was consumed, the normal create-path branch handles recovery on that later tick; if EB or power/state made departure invalid, the existing EB/power/stopped-state rules decide when retry is allowed.
- Ordinary departure policy (`auto` vs `manual`) controls whether `RouteExecutor` writes `departure_requested` automatically after authority exists. In ATO, successful Phase-2 acceptance can immediately start movement. In manual mode, successful acceptance marks the current authority ready to go and actual movement still waits for driver input. `TrainController.departure_confirmation_required` is a general one-shot safety latch that forces manual confirmation for the next departure even when the route/task policy would otherwise be automatic.

**Concrete departure calls**

- Every `RouteExecutor` access to a train's controller goes through `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train.train_id)` (the shared public accessor) rather than a private `_get_controller` helper or any direct dictionary lookup.
- If the resolved target block for a `task_kind == "waypoint"` task is already physically covered by the train's current span, `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` must not call `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` for that `waypoint` task. It treats the task as already missed/skipped under the same covered-task normalization rule used elsewhere and continues planning toward the next task on the current directed route walk instead. If the resolved target block for a `task_kind == "stop"` task is already physically covered, `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` must still treat the stop as the current target and may request same-block stop authority; the stop is not finished until the normal post-physics usable-authority stop check and dispatcher completion have run.
- For any `stop` task that still needs authority, `RouteExecutor` does not compute or compare a centimeter-level local stopping target. It passes only the task's `requested_destination_block_id` and `requested_destination_direction` into `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)`.
- Explicit same-block/zero-movement authorities are route-backed in v1 through the ordinary one-off `Route` flow with no special run-level flag. A direct operator/API local-reposition request constructs a `RouteMode.ONE_OFF` route with a single `task_kind == "stop"` task whose `task_location` is the destination block/platform/station. Because `current_waypoint_index` initializes to `-1` for every run, `Route.tasks[0]` is the first executable target. `RouteTask.task_direction` for the destination follows the same task-authoring rule as any other route; the operator/API construction step is responsible for resolving "current cab/running direction" into a concrete `"forward"` / `"reverse"` value before the route is stored, because dispatcher never receives `"either"`. If that direction cannot be derived at construction time, the operator/API request fails before any route is stored. The run is executed through the ordinary Phase 1 / `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` flow. If the destination stop block is already covered when planning runs, the run still targets that stop and uses same-block stop authority or immediate usable-authority stop completion under the ordinary stop predicate; it does not skip the destination merely because the block is covered. These authorities carry no separate target-position field; the controller uses the standard safety-margin stop logic.
- When the current attempt uses `AcquisitionMode.best_effort`, compare the live path's current authorized target against the live path's stored requested target each tick (`Path.authority_end_block_id` versus `Path.requested_destination_block_id`). If the live path is still short of that requested target, keep calling `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on that same `path_id` using the newly re-resolved request contract for the same pending waypoint; do not synthesize a new path.
- If `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` sees current live authority already present on the controller (`TrainController.received_path_plan is not None`), do not call `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` again. First perform any eligible `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` retry for the currently armed target; after that create/extend decision is complete for this Phase 1 pass, branch on departure policy:
  - if auto departure is allowed for this case, write `TrainController.departure_requested` for that current live authority; in ATO the later Phase-2 acceptance may start movement immediately, while in manual mode it only marks the authority/route as ready and still leaves motion to the driver
  - if manual confirmation is required, leave the train in `STANDBY` or `IDLE` with the current live authority intact
- Remove the train-side `current_path` / `path_index` write from `RouteExecutor`; `PathDispatcher` should own the authoritative path-progress view and expose any controller/UI-facing path tiles or path-progress display as a pushed/read-only projection.
- Remove the `train.assigned_path_id` write from `RouteExecutor`; `controller.assigned_path_id` should be derived from the path plan that `PathDispatcher` pushes.
- Do not set `controller.ato_path_id` or any destination field directly from `RouteExecutor`.
- Remove the route-controlled fallback where `TrainController.depart()` is reached without dispatcher authority; route-driven movement should require a dispatcher path plan and should reach departure acceptance only through `TrainController.departure_requested`.
- Remove the route-facing `TrainController.wait_for_path(reason)` command in the desired model. The controller should infer "no path authority" from an empty `received_path_plan`; higher layers do not need a separate command to tell it that the path is gone.
- If initial path creation fails before departure in `all_or_nothing` mode, `RouteExecutor` should record that route/path error on `exec_state.last_error` / `exec_state.error_time`, set `exec_state.waiting_for_path = True`, and keep the train in `STANDBY` or `IDLE`; the train never had authority, so no special controller waiting command is needed.
- In `best_effort` mode, only "no authority at all could be created" is a creation failure. A truncated but live path is not a failure path; the train may run under that current authority and later wait at the current authority boundary while `RouteExecutor` keeps retrying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on the same `path_id`.
- On cancel/revoke, `PathDispatcher` clears `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`, the controller follows the no-authority braking path, and recovery follows the canonical recovery table. Phase 1 is gated by controller state and latches (`IDLE`/`STANDBY`, `eb_active == False`, `pending_power_off == False`); it never reads `cancellation_kind`. If the next planning attempt fails, fall through to the ordinary pre-departure route/path error path — no recovery-only state machine.

**Arrival detection**

- Stop using `RouteExecutor._is_train_at_platform()` as the normal arrival gate after dispatcher-owned path completion. In the desired model, the post-physics controller stop check owns stop-complete observation and `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` owns the completion commit; dispatcher-committed completion is the authoritative arrival trigger for that `stop` task.
- Delete `RouteExecutor._check_waypoint_arrival()` from the desired model and replace it with one route-owned arrival-entry helper `RouteExecutor._enter_task_arrival(exec_state, stop_task_index, arrival_time)`. `stop_task_index` is the index in `Route.tasks` of the `stop` task that just completed. `arrival_time` is a system-time Unix seconds timestamp: callers from `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` pass the callback `event_time`; any other caller passes the current system time.
- Remove `TrainController.arrive_at_station()` in the desired model and implement a new `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` acceptance command consumed from the controller-owned `dwell_entry_requested` handoff. This is not a rename: route-level dwell entry should happen only after dispatcher-committed stopped arrival from the post-physics controller stop check. `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` is the universal dwell-entry command for any `task_kind == "stop"` (direct block stop, platform stop, or station-resolved stop) and should be valid from the dispatcher-completed stopped ready state (`IDLE` or `STANDBY`). ATO movement itself still requires a dispatcher path.
- On first arrival from dispatcher-committed completion for a `task_kind == "stop"`, `RouteExecutor._enter_task_arrival(exec_state, stop_task_index, arrival_time)` should: set `exec_state.task_runs[stop_task_index].arrived_at = arrival_time`; if a `dwell` task immediately follows the `stop` task on the current directed route walk, set `exec_state.task_runs[dwell_task_index].arrived_at = arrival_time` as the dwell start time; clear `exec_state.waiting_for_path`; clear `task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`; and set `target_waypoint_index = current_waypoint_index`. It must **not** advance `exec_state.current_waypoint_index` yet: a `stop` task is finished only when its following `dwell` task (if any) exits, or immediately when no `dwell` task follows. It does not arm the next leg, does not call the next-task helper, does not write persistent served-platform bookkeeping, and does not call `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` in the completion callback. This arrival-recording path applies to both ATO and manual trains; the manual/ATO difference is departure behavior only.
- On the next eligible Phase 1 after `_enter_task_arrival(...)` records arrival, `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` must handle the pending `dwell` task before any new path creation, extension, departure, covered-task normalization, or target arming. If the controller is in the dispatcher-completed stopped ready state (`IDLE` or `STANDBY`), `RouteExecutor` writes `TrainController.dwell_entry_requested` and then returns immediately for that tick; the next Phase-2 controller tick consumes the request and transitions into `RUNNING_AT_STATION` if its preconditions still hold. If the controller is already `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, it returns. The train does not plan a new leg while dwell is pending or active.
- If an implementation keeps a helper such as `RouteExecutor._check_dwell_complete(train, route, exec_state)`, it is a Phase 3 helper invoked from the same train's general feedback/cleanup pass after normal progress/completion handling, not from `RouteExecutor.update(...)`. It runs only while controller state is `RUNNING_AT_STATION` and calls `RouteTask.should_depart(exec_state.task_runs[dwell_index].arrived_at, current_time_sec)`, where both timestamps are system-time Unix seconds.
- A `stop` task with no following `dwell` task (or with a `dwell` task whose `task_time` is absent) still enters the normal dwell path; the same train's later Phase 3 dwell-complete check finishes dwell immediately since `should_depart(...)` is immediately true. Fresh next-leg planning still waits for a later Phase 1 tick.
- Remove `RouteExecutor._advance_to_next_waypoint()` from the desired model. Stop arrival no longer advances `current_waypoint_index`; dwell exit is the one place that marks the combined stop+dwell pair finished.
- A route is complete only after the terminal task sequence (stop + any following dwell) has finished. Route completion handling never fires before the terminal dwell (if any) exits.
- If dwell/timing says depart, the Phase 3 dwell-complete helper: sets `exec_state.task_runs[dwell_index].finished_at = current_time`; sets `exec_state.task_runs[stop_index].finished_at = current_time` (or to `arrived_at` if no dwell was present); sets `exec_state.current_waypoint_index = dwell_task_index` (or `stop_task_index` when no dwell follows); sets `exec_state.direction` from the route walk; sets `target_waypoint_index = current_waypoint_index`; calls `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train.train_id).finish_dwell()`; and leaves the next leg to be refreshed by the next planning tick.
- If no task remains after the completed stop+dwell pair, that Phase 3 dwell-complete helper calls `_handle_route_complete_for_train(train, route, exec_state)`. By this point dwell exit (`finish_dwell()`) has already run, so `_handle_route_complete_for_train(...)` does not call `finish_dwell()` itself.
- `_handle_route_complete_for_train()` resolves the controller through `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train.train_id)`, calls `route.unassign_train(train.train_id)`, clears `train.assigned_route_id` when it matches, removes `execution_states[train.train_id]`, and calls `route.deactivate()` for completed one-off routes with no assigned trains. It does not call `finish_dwell()` (the dwell-complete helper has already done that) and does not force a custom completion state; once route bookkeeping is removed, the train remains in the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`STANDBY` with a cab selected, `IDLE` otherwise).
- `finish_dwell()` is a **dwell-exit state-transition command only**, named `finish_dwell` rather than `depart_from_station` because it does not start movement. It transitions the controller out of `RUNNING_AT_STATION` to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state. The normal caller is the same train's Phase 3 dwell-complete cleanup. `RouteExecutor` is still responsible for deciding whether more route work remains, clearing route assignment when complete, and removing the execution-state entry; those are route-level bookkeeping, not controller state.
  `TrainController.finish_dwell()` must not touch `received_path_plan` (only `PathDispatcher` writes it; authority for the completed leg was already cleared by `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` before dwell began), must not call `TrainController.depart()` / `TrainController.ato_go()`, and must not initiate physics movement. The next leg's route-side restart happens on the following tick through `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)` -> `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` and then, if departure policy allows, `RouteExecutor` writes `departure_requested` for Phase-2 acceptance. In ATO that successful acceptance can begin movement immediately. In manual mode the route remains armed but stopped until the operator selects a cab if needed and applies manual driving input; `TrainController.depart()` marks the current authority ready to go but does not start physics movement by itself.

**Dispatcher completion handling**

**route path-event callback schema**

- This is the single canonical callback contract for `PathDispatcher -> RouteExecutor` path-terminal notifications.
- The callback payloads are exactly:
  - completion context: `train_id`, `path_id`, `authority_end_block_id`, `requested_destination_direction`, `event_time`
  - cancellation context: `train_id`, `path_id`, `cancellation_kind`, `reason`
  `RouteExecutor` correlates these callbacks by `train_id` and the callback-supplied `path_id` and must not rediscover path identity from torn-down controller state.
- In that completion context:
  - `requested_destination_direction` is the active path's stored block-relative requested target direction (`"forward"` or `"reverse"`) at completion time
  - `event_time` is sampled from the current system Unix clock at the moment Phase 3 calls `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)`, and that sampled value is used as the dwell-entry arrival time
  - the callback does not carry a second route-owned target block. `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` obtains the stop target block from `RouteRun.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target` (or falls back to stale/degraded diagnostics when no armed target exists). This keeps route target meaning owned by `RouteRun`, while the dispatcher payload carries only path-completion facts.
- In that cancellation context:
  - `cancellation_kind` is the `PathCancellationKind` enum carried strictly for diagnostics. v1 route-side recovery does not branch on it. EB recovery timing is gated by controller state (Phase 1 cannot run until `release_emergency_cmd()` has returned the controller to `IDLE` / `STANDBY`); power-off teardown is driven by `pending_power_off` and the controller transitioning to `OFF`; every other case shares the same recovery shell.
  - `reason` is optional plain-text diagnostic context only and must not be used for branching
- The single v1 recovery table for cancellation / mismatch cases is:

  | Case | Detection point | Immediate effect | Next eligible Phase 1 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | any cancellation kind (movement-derived `OUT_OF_SEQUENCE` / `AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT`, or explicit `OPERATOR_CANCELLED` / `REVOKED` / `EB`) | `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(...)` | clear `task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`, preserve only confirmed physical progress, record `cancellation_kind` for diagnostics only | rerun covered-waypoint normalization plus the Phase-1 target-arm step from current physical position once the controller is in `IDLE` / `STANDBY`; pass-through skip and stop retarget/completion behavior follow the normal waypoint-kind rules |
  | completion callback mismatch / stale route-path meaning | `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` mismatch branch | clear stale route/path bookkeeping, do not advance waypoint, record diagnostics on `last_error`/`error_time`, leave `state = RUNNING` | rerun ordinary planning from unchanged physical progress |
  | route edit / retarget invalidates a live path | Phase 1 latch + Phase 3 cancel / replan flow | clear `task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target` and temporary target state | rerun ordinary planning under the current `route_id` |
  | EB interruption | EB cancellation plus release flow | clear live path | the `RUNNING_MOVING` → stop → `release_emergency_cmd()` chain naturally returns the controller to `IDLE` / `STANDBY`, at which point Phase 1 replans normally; the EB enum value itself is not read |
  | power off | power-off cleanup flow | cancel live path if any, remove `RouteRun`, perform OFF cleanup | the `RouteRun` no longer exists and the controller is `OFF`; Phase 1 has nothing to do regardless of `cancellation_kind` |

- `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` should follow the **Active-path completion procedure** when the post-physics controller stop check has already passed and the active path requested-target check succeeds: release not-yet-entered block reservations, release not-yet-entered switch/flank locks, clear controller authority by calling `TrainController.receive_path_plan(None)`, call `TrainController.on_path_complete()`, transition the `Path` to `COMPLETED`, clear `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`, and then deliver the canonical completion payload through the `SimulationController` runtime event interface to `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(train_id, path_id, authority_end_block_id, requested_destination_direction, event_time)`.
- `handle_path_completion()` should treat dispatcher completion as authoritative and should not perform a second exact-stop or live-controller-state check. It correlates the callback `path_id` against the dispatcher's `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`; if they differ, the callback is stale for this route and should be ignored.
- If no `RouteRun` exists for `train_id`, `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` is a stale/recovery no-op other than optional diagnostics. In normal v1 runtime, every live authority path should have a matching `RouteRun`, including one-off operator routes.
- It should clear only route-executor-owned bookkeeping such as `exec_state.waiting_for_path`. Active path tiles, reserved resources, and other authority residue should not be stored or cleared on `Train` by `RouteExecutor` in the desired model. It should no longer clear `train.ato_destination_block_id` (that field should be removed), `train.assigned_path_id` (that field moves to the controller and is cleared by `PathDispatcher`), or `train.reserved_blocks` (reserved resource ownership stays dispatcher-owned).
- `PathDispatcher` clears controller authority by calling `controller.receive_path_plan(None)` as part of path release before `controller.on_path_complete()` and before calling `handle_path_completion()`. The derived reads `controller.assigned_path_id`, `controller.ato_path_id`, and `controller.ato_destination_block_id` then disappear automatically.
- `controller.transition_to_standby()` should be removed from `handle_path_completion()`. `TrainController.on_path_complete()` owns the post-path stop transition and should leave the train in the state-machine-derived stopped ready state; `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` records pending dwell context and the next eligible `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` call performs dwell entry.
- Controller destination-block/path release should remain dispatcher-owned through `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`; `TrainController.on_path_complete()` reacts to that completion while `RouteExecutor` records the completed stop as a pending dwell step. `RouteExecutor` remains responsible for entering dwell (`RUNNING_AT_STATION`) on the next eligible Phase 1 and for marking the stop task finished only after Phase 3 dwell-complete cleanup.
- `handle_path_completion()` must not need to query live train/controller path state after dispatcher release. It should use the completion context passed in by `PathDispatcher` plus route-executor-owned state only.
- `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` must not read `PathDispatcher.paths_by_id[path_id]` or any other dispatcher path record. After the `path_id` correlation check passes, it uses only the completion context and `exec_state.task_runs`; it must not perform fresh target resolution in the completion callback.
- Let `armed_target = exec_state.task_runs[exec_state.target_waypoint_index].resolved_target` when one exists. If `Route.tasks[exec_state.target_waypoint_index].task_kind == "waypoint"`, do not treat the callback as stop arrival or as a completion-mismatch deviation by itself. A later stop completion at or beyond that waypoint boundary is a safety-stop/truncated-authority event, not waypoint arrival. In that case, `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` must clear `exec_state.waiting_for_path`, clear `exec_state.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`, leave `exec_state.current_waypoint_index` unchanged, set `target_waypoint_index = current_waypoint_index`, optionally record diagnostics, and return. Ordinary next-eligible Phase 1 covered-task normalization then decides whether that waypoint task has already been entered, passed, or physically covered and therefore counts as served. Dispatcher-committed completion plus the passed completion context remain authoritative; `RouteExecutor` does not re-check safety-margin projection from live train/controller state.
- If `armed_target` is absent, if `Route.tasks[exec_state.target_waypoint_index].task_kind != "stop"`, if `armed_target.requested_destination_block_id` does not equal `authority_end_block_id`, or if `armed_target.requested_destination_direction` does not equal `requested_destination_direction`, treat the callback as a route/dispatcher invariant violation or degraded safety-stop diagnostic recovery case. `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` must clear `exec_state.waiting_for_path`, clear `exec_state.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`, leave `exec_state.current_waypoint_index` unchanged, set `target_waypoint_index = current_waypoint_index`, record diagnostics on `exec_state.last_error` / `exec_state.error_time`, and return. It must not write `state = DEVIATED` (the train is already stopped at the dispatcher-completed boundary; the next eligible Phase 1 plans normally from current span coverage), must not auto-skip to the next task, must not invent a second live-authority cancellation branch inside the completion callback, must not extend or reuse the completed/mismatched path, and must not set `RouteRun.path_cancelled` for this case. The next eligible Phase 1 re-enters ordinary planning from that unchanged physical progress cursor with no special branching.
- If `Route.tasks[exec_state.target_waypoint_index].task_kind == "stop"` and both `armed_target.requested_destination_block_id == authority_end_block_id` and `armed_target.requested_destination_direction == requested_destination_direction`, call `RouteExecutor._enter_task_arrival(exec_state, stop_task_index=exec_state.target_waypoint_index, arrival_time=event_time)` immediately to record pending dwell and dwell timing, but do not advance `current_waypoint_index` and do not call `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` from the completion callback. The next eligible Phase 1 must process that recorded pending dwell before any new departure/path planning, so there is no route-owned chance to misread the bare stopped ready state as a fresh departure opportunity. The stop task becomes finished only when the later Phase 3 dwell-complete cleanup exits dwell (or immediately if no dwell task follows).
- Add `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(train_id, path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` as the cancellation-side counterpart. The lower-to-upper ordering is canonical: `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` finishes its own path/resource/controller mutation first, and only after that commit may the canonical cancellation context be handed upward to this route callback. Movement-driven cancellations detected after physics are therefore initiated and committed in Phase 3 while queued reports are being drained. Explicit authority cancellations and EB-triggered cancellations are latched earlier through `pending_cancel_request`; the same train's later Phase 3 consume pass calls `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` only after the normal queued-progress and completion opportunity has already been processed. Explicit route abort is a separate `pending_route_abort` latch consumed after authority cancellation. Phase 1 route edits, retargeting, and other live-segment replacements use that same latch-cancel-and-replan workflow described in the Route Edit And Reconciliation section. In all cases, the callback is bookkeeping only: it must not choose a fresh target or create fresh authority, and it must not branch on `cancellation_kind` to alter route progression or decide route removal. `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(...)` should first correlate the callback `path_id` against `PathDispatcher.active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`; if they differ, the callback is stale for this route and should be ignored. For the matching live path, it should clear `exec_state.waiting_for_path`, record route/path error context on `exec_state.last_error` / `exec_state.error_time` when relevant, set `RouteRun.current_route_status.state = DEVIATED`, record `RouteRun.current_route_status.last_cancellation_kind = cancellation_kind` strictly for UI/diagnostics (no consumer reads this field for branching), clear `exec_state.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target` for the interrupted leg, set `RouteRun.path_cancelled = True`, and reset `target_waypoint_index = current_waypoint_index`. When the train later reaches the stopped recovery state, Phase 3 consumes `path_cancelled`, clears `DEVIATED`, and leaves the next leg unarmed. A later Phase 1 planning attempt must rerun ordinary covered-task normalization plus `_arm_next_target` from the train's current physical position and create a brand-new path rather than trying to preserve or reuse the cancelled one; already-covered waypoint handling and stop handling follow the normal task-kind rules. It must NOT advance the task cursor, synthesize arrival, choose the next task, or create authority at cancellation time while the train is still braking.
- All cancellation kinds (`OPERATOR_CANCELLED`, `REVOKED`, `EB`, `OUT_OF_SEQUENCE`, `AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT`, `POWER_OFF`) share the same `handle_path_cancellation(...)` body. There is no per-kind branch inside the callback. The recovery timing differences observed at the system level emerge from runtime gates that exist independently of the enum:
  - the Phase 1 eligibility precondition only allows replanning when the controller is in `IDLE` / `STANDBY`, which naturally defers EB recovery until `release_emergency_cmd()` has run;
  - the power-off Phase 3 cleanup separately calls `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(train_id, reason="power_off")`, which removes the `RouteRun` entirely so no later Phase 1 has anything to replan; `handle_path_cancellation(...)` for the `POWER_OFF` case still records the same diagnostic state, but the run is unassigned moments later by the same Phase 3 cleanup.
  Planning and routing logic must rely on these runtime gates (controller state, latches, presence of `RouteRun`, current span coverage, and task-kind rules), not on inspecting `cancellation_kind`.
- Route edit / reconciliation uses this same temporary-status-clear model only after the current live path has already left `ACTIVE`. When the active run's `route_id` changes or a shared-route reconciliation changes the active target chain, the current live path must be treated as potentially stale because the system cannot prove that the already-acquired authority is still correct for the edited route. Phase 1 therefore latches `pending_cancel_request`, stops planning for that train, and waits for the ordinary Phase 3 cancel/callback/stopped-recovery flow. The canonical `cancellation_kind` selection rule is: operator-driven retargeting and explicit operator-edited route swaps use `PathCancellationKind.OPERATOR_CANCELLED`; system-detected invariant violations (such as the route-executor / dispatcher `path_id` mismatch in step `RouteExecutor._try_depart_to_waypoint(...)`) and shared-route edits initiated by non-operator actors (such as automated reconciliation) use `PathCancellationKind.REVOKED`. Only after that live path is no longer active may `RouteExecutor` clear or rewrite the affected route-owned temporary target state and restart ordinary planning from the train's current position under the reconciled route.
- Completion handling may observe a train still physically occupying the terminal block or switch footprint. That is not a contradiction: `RouteExecutor` treats dispatcher completion as authoritative arrival, while physical resource release still waits for the later tail-vacate callbacks.

## Train Entity

**Train ownership**

- Remove live position/span and live speed from `Train`. In v1, `Train` is the persistent/configuration entity, not the runtime physical-location store.
- `TrainController` owns the live physical span object for its train, such as `current_span`, together with the live `current_speed_kmh` value. `TrainPhysics` computes movement and sends `span_update` deltas to the controller; the controller applies those updates to its own storage. `TrainPhysics` does not make `Train` the storage owner.
- Remove train-side mirrors for `target_speed` and `speed_limit_reason`; those should become reads from controller lookahead at distance 0.
- Remove `manual_target_speed` entirely; the new manual model should not keep a per-train target speed field.
- Remove `train.state`; operational state lives entirely on `TrainController.state` and must not be mirrored on `Train`. Every consumer that wants operational state should read `controller.state` directly.
- Move live power/cab ownership to `TrainController`. Old train-side live power or cab fields are not imported into v1 controller state; after startup, the only live power/cab owner is `TrainController`.
- Move live placement/span fields such as `position`, `occupied_tiles`, `occupied_blocks`, end locators, and per-end in-tile distances out of `Train` and into the controller-owned span. `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train_id)` does NOT seed `TrainController.current_span` from persisted data; its job is only "make sure a controller object exists and is registered for this `train_id`." Initial placement of `TrainController.current_span` is owned by the API `SimulationController.add_train(...)` flow, which constructs the train, builds its initial physical span from placement input, obtains the controller, and writes the span directly into `TrainController.current_span` before any simulation tick runs. The span builder reuses the existing repo placement-to-position derivation (the current code already produces precise locators for both physical ends from placement input — head tile, head entry edge, train length, etc.); `SimulationController.add_train(...)` calls that existing helper, packages the produced precise `end_a` / `end_b` locators plus `occupied_tiles` into a `TrainSpan`, and writes it into `TrainController.current_span`. V1 does not keep or read persistent train-side placement mirrors for live runtime state after this call; old saved live placement mirrors are outside the v1 contract rather than a second runtime input. After that initial placement, `TrainPhysics` is the only normal producer of movement `span_update` values and `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` is the normal writer for `TrainController.current_span`; live movement updates never write back to `Train`.
- Remove `train.assigned_path_id`; the controller-side derived read `TrainController.assigned_path_id` (from `received_path_plan.path_id`) is the only runtime source.
- Remove `train.ato_destination_block_id`; destination and authority-end blocks should travel inside the controller path plan pushed by `PathDispatcher`.
- Remove train-side `current_path` and `path_index`; active path tiles and path-progress reads should come from a controller/UI projection owned by `PathDispatcher` instead of a train mirror written by `RouteExecutor`.
- Remove `train.reserved_blocks` as runtime authority residue in the desired model; reserved block/resource ownership should stay on dispatcher path records and controller-facing projections, not on `Train`.
- Keep `Train` focused on persistent/configuration data only, and move live physical span, speed, authority, and operational diagnostics to `TrainController`.
- Persistent/configuration fields kept on `Train` in v1 include identity, consist (including length), `max_speed`, `assigned_route_id` (the persistent route assignment, distinct from the removed runtime `assigned_path_id`), and other non-live train-configuration data already on the entity. Runtime-only fields listed above (live position/span, speed, state, ATO/path mirrors, reserved-block residue, manual-target-speed) are removed; persistent route assignment is not removed and remains the field that `_handle_route_complete_for_train(...)` clears on completion when it matches.
- Bootstrap from persisted runtime data also flows through `SimulationController.add_train(...)`. Persistence-loading code calls `add_train(...)` with the saved placement payload for each restored train; there is no parallel `seed_train_span(...)` helper, and persisted-train fields that previously stored live position/span are no longer consulted at load time.


## TrainController

**Controller state**

- `TrainController` owns live power/cab state in the desired model: `power_on`, active cab selection, and the live direction selection implied by that cab. These are not re-synced from `Train` on every tick.
- `TrainController` owns the train's live physical span and speed in the desired model. `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` computes movement and emits `PhysicsSpanUpdate`; `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` is the normal writer that applies the new span/speed to controller-owned storage. `Train` itself does not store live position/span.
- Add `manual_mode_type`, such as `simulation` or `power_percent`.
- `TrainController.travel_direction` is derived from the active cab: the cab is the driver's cab, and the direction it faces is the train's forward travel direction. The mapping is computed from the consist orientation: the cab's physical end (`end_a` or `end_b` per `TrainSpan`) is the head, and `travel_direction` is the network-frame direction pointing from the opposite physical end through the cab end and onward into the next span resource ahead. `TrainController.travel_direction` is updated atomically with any successful write to `cab`: writers are `TrainController.set_cab(...)` (sets cab and recomputes `travel_direction` from current span; `set_cab(None)` clears `travel_direction` to `None`) and `TrainController.depart()` auto-cab-selection (sets cab and recomputes `travel_direction` from current span and the active `received_path_plan` authority frame). When no cab is selected, `travel_direction` is `None`. There is no other writer.
- Add `manual_power_percent` for `power_percent`-mode manual control. In v1 this is an integer `0..100` voltage-style command rather than a signed traction/brake command.
- Add `departure_confirmation_required: bool` as a generic one-shot safety latch. EB release sets it to `True` so the next fresh authority departure requires explicit confirmation even when the route/waypoint departure policy is automatic. Future safety workflows may reuse this same latch; it is not route-specific and not tied to a cancellation enum. `TrainController.depart()` clears it only after a successful departure acceptance, and Phase-3 power-off cleanup clears it during OFF cleanup.
- Add `eb_released: bool` as a controller-owned Phase-3 handoff written by `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` after EB is cleared and the controller has returned to the stopped ready state. This latch lets the same train's later Phase 3 recovery cleanup coordinate with any existing `RouteRun` without a synchronous controller-to-route call. It is cleared by that Phase 3 cleanup, or by power-off cleanup.
- Add `pending_power_off: bool` as a stopped-only shutdown latch set by `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` during the tick-entry command drain inside `TrainController.tick(...)`, and consumed by the later eligible Phase 3 OFF cleanup. It is the canonical handoff that moves power-off from command acceptance into the standard per-train phase workflow.
- `TrainController` owns the per-train Phase-3 mailbox / flag table in v1. Logically it contains `queued_progress_reports`, optional `pending_cancel_request`, optional `pending_route_abort`, `eb_released`, and `pending_power_off`. It also owns the separate Phase-1-to-Phase-2 handoff flags `departure_requested` and `dwell_entry_requested`; those booleans are consumed by `TrainController.tick(...)`, not by Phase 3 cleanup. The retained-field schema below lists those logical members even if an implementation stores the Phase-3 members under one nested object such as `TrainController.phase3_mailbox`.
- Define the `IDLE` / `STANDBY` trigger explicitly: `IDLE` is the controller's state when the train is powered on but no cab is selected. In `IDLE`, the train has no manually selected forward/backward direction because no cab is active. A controller enters `IDLE` from `OFF` when power is turned on with no cab selected. Selecting a cab transitions the controller immediately to `STANDBY`. Route authority may still be assigned while the controller is `IDLE`, and `RouteExecutor` must not treat `IDLE` as a blocker for `create_path(...)`. In both ATO and manual modes, `TrainController.depart()` may auto-select the cab from the directed authority geometry carried by `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` when no cab is currently selected. In ATO, that auto-selection plus `ato_go()` begins movement immediately. In manual mode, the same auto-selection on a successful `depart()` acceptance transitions the controller to `STANDBY` and writes `departure_ready_path_id`, but does not begin physics movement; manual motion still requires the human driver to apply input, and is always constrained by ATC lookahead. Manual input may therefore move the train even before `departure_ready_path_id` is set, but such movement cannot satisfy path stop-completion until `TrainController.depart()` has accepted that authority. Such pre-depart manual movement may still produce head-entry route progress such as `task_kind == "waypoint"` consumption; only stop-completion remains depart gated. The unsupported exception is same-tile synthetic movement with no cab selected: dispatcher rejects that request before any authority is pushed.
- Keep manual driving as `control_mode == manual`; do not keep a separate `MANUAL` operational state in the desired model.
- Add `assigned_path_id: Optional[str]` only as a computed read-only property derived from `received_path_plan.path_id`. It replaces `train.assigned_path_id` as the controller-facing runtime read, but it should not be stored or written independently.
- Add `received_path_plan` as the controller inbox for the active path plan pushed by `PathDispatcher`.
- `received_path_plan` is a projection written only by `PathDispatcher`; it is not the source of truth for dispatcher path ownership, and `TrainController` should not mutate it directly.
- `TrainController` is also the canonical query and storage home for the train's current occupied span. The live span is produced by `TrainPhysics` and written into `TrainController.current_span`; block/switch resources still carry their own physical `occupied_by` ownership, but tile-level ends and ordered occupied tiles live on the controller span rather than on `Train`. `RouteExecutor` and `PathDispatcher` ask the controller for one cab-independent physical span read instead of deriving it independently from `Train`. That span read must expose the ordered occupied span in physical order (`occupied_resources`, `occupied_blocks`, and `occupied_tiles`) plus the two explicit physical end locators needed for later ordering comparisons. The occupancy list is the block/resource-level ownership view; the end locators are the tile-level precise geometry view. It does not depend on a currently selected cab.
- Exception: same-tile synthetic movement without a selected cab remains unsupported in v1. `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` should reject that request before a plan is pushed; `TrainController` does not infer or invent the cab direction for that case after the fact.

**Controller retained field schema**

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `state` | controller state enum | Canonical operational state (`OFF`, `IDLE`, `STANDBY`, `RUNNING_MOVING`, `RUNNING_WAITING`, `RUNNING_AT_STATION`) |
| `power_on` | `bool` | Live controller-owned power state |
| `cab` | `Optional[str]` | Active cab selection (`"A"`, `"B"`, or `None`) |
| `control_mode` | `"ato" \| "manual"` | High-level driving mode selector |
| `manual_mode_type` | `"simulation" \| "power_percent"` | Manual-mode submode selector. Construction default is `simulation`. Mutated only through `TrainController.set_manual_mode_type(...)`. |
| `travel_direction` | `Optional[str]` | Live direction selection implied by the active cab |
| `handle_position` | controller handle enum/value | Active handle command for ATO and manual `simulation` mode |
| `manual_power_percent` | `int` | Active manual driver command for manual `power_percent` mode (`0..100`) |
| `eb_active` | `bool` | Emergency-brake latch |
| `ato_running` | `bool` | Whether an ATO run is presently active (the train has been commanded to run under ATO and has not yet been halted, dwelled, EB'd, or otherwise stopped). This is a run-active flag, not a mode flag; the mode flag is `control_mode`. |
| `departure_confirmation_required` | `bool` | Generic one-shot safety latch that forces the next departure to require explicit confirmation even if route policy is auto; set by EB release, cleared by successful `TrainController.depart()` or power-off cleanup |
| `eb_released` | `bool` | Controller-owned Phase-3 handoff written by `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` after EB is cleared; consumed by Phase-3 stopped-recovery cleanup step when controller is `IDLE`/`STANDBY` and `received_path_plan is None` |
| `departure_requested` | `bool` | Controller-owned Phase-1-to-Phase-2 departure flag set by `RouteExecutor` route policy or explicit operator departure confirmation after live authority exists; consumed and cleared by `TrainController.tick(...)` after operator commands and the EB gate by running the `TrainController.depart()` acceptance logic for the current `received_path_plan.path_id` |
| `dwell_entry_requested` | `bool` | Controller-owned Phase-1-to-Phase-2 dwell-entry flag set by `RouteExecutor` after dispatcher-recorded stopped arrival; consumed and cleared by `TrainController.tick(...)` after operator commands and the EB gate by running the `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` acceptance logic |
| `queued_progress_reports` | `List[ControllerProgressReport]` | Ordered post-physics progress reports waiting for that same train's Phase 3 consume pass |
| `pending_cancel_request` | `Optional[PendingCancelRequest]` | Explicit non-movement Phase-1/Phase-2-to-Phase-3 cancellation payload for the train's current active path; carries `cancellation_kind`, optional `reason`, and optional `expected_path_id` diagnostics |
| `pending_route_abort` | `Optional[PendingRouteAbortRequest]` | Explicit route-run removal latch consumed by Phase 3 after active authority has been cancelled or found absent; carries `reason` and optional `expected_path_id` diagnostics |
| `pending_power_off` | `bool` | Stopped-only shutdown latch set by `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` and consumed by Phase 3 OFF cleanup; remains `True` until that cleanup completes |
| `departure_ready_path_id` | `Optional[str]` | Controller-owned ready-to-go marker written only by successful `TrainController.depart()` acceptance for the current authority and cleared when that authority is cleared (set to `None`), replaced by a different `path_id`, completed, or cancelled, EB is latched (operator-issued or ATC-derived), the active cab selection changes via `set_cab(...)`, or power is turned off. A same-`path_id` extension refresh keeps the marker (even when `requested_destination_block_id` or `requested_destination_direction` changes on that same live path). `set_mode(...)` does not clear it. |
| `received_path_plan` | `Optional[received_path_plan]` | Controller's read-only active-authority projection written only by `PathDispatcher` |
| `current_span` | `TrainSpan` | Controller-owned live physical span, including ordered occupied resources/blocks/tiles and both physical end locators |
| `current_speed_kmh` | `float` | Controller-owned live physical speed written by `TrainPhysics` |
| `lookahead` | canonical lookahead payload cache | Cached canonical lookahead payload produced by the latest post-physics refresh; reused at the next tick start and rebuilt on demand if absent |

- The table above is the canonical retained controller field set for v1. If a field is not in this table or in a later canonical payload schema, it should not remain as an independently written live controller field.

**Controller state machine**

| State | Meaning | Canonical entry triggers | Canonical exits |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `OFF` | Power off | Phase-3 power-off cleanup consuming `TrainController.pending_power_off` from a stopped powered state; startup default | `TrainController.power_on_cmd()` |
| `IDLE` | Powered on, no cab selected | `TrainController.power_on_cmd()` with no cab; `TrainController.set_cab(None)` from a stopped state; `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()`, `TrainController.on_path_complete()`, or stopped no-authority recovery when no cab is selected | `TrainController.set_cab("A" or "B")`; `TrainController.depart()` may auto-select a cab in ATO or manual when live authority exists |
| `STANDBY` | Stopped, cab selected, not dwelling, not waiting | cab selection while stopped; post-path `TrainController.on_path_complete()` when a cab is selected; successful no-authority stop after cancellation/revocation when a cab is selected; `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` when a cab is selected; `TrainController.finish_dwell()`; reactive speed-based evaluation on the next tick when a previously moving manual train reaches zero speed without the RUNNING_WAITING conditions | ATO `TrainController.depart()` / movement start; reactive speed-based evaluation on the next tick when `current_speed_kmh > 0` (see below); `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`; `TrainController.set_cab(None)`; `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` |
| `RUNNING_MOVING` | Moving or actively commanded under authority/manual driving | reactive speed-based evaluation on the next `TrainController.tick()` when the controller observes `current_speed_kmh > 0` from the previous tick's `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` write while in `STANDBY` or `RUNNING_WAITING` — this is the canonical mechanism for both manual-mode and ATO-mode `STANDBY` / `RUNNING_WAITING` → `RUNNING_MOVING`. ATO's `TrainController.depart()` accepts the depart command and `TrainController.ato_go()` starts producing nonzero handle commands, but the controller stays in `STANDBY` until physics actually integrates motion; if ATO cannot move the train (e.g. zero ATC clearance, blocked authority, calibration error), the controller correctly remains in `STANDBY` and the lack of motion is itself the diagnostic signal. | `RUNNING_WAITING` when stopped under the same live authority with zero clearance; state-machine-derived stopped ready state on post-path completion or no-authority stop; `RUNNING_AT_STATION` only through route-owned dwell entry after confirmed arrival; reactive speed-based evaluation to STANDBY/IDLE on the next tick when a manual train reaches zero speed without the RUNNING_WAITING or RUNNING_AT_STATION conditions |
| `RUNNING_WAITING` | Stopped while the same live authority still exists, ATC is holding the train at the current live-authority boundary, and that authority boundary is short of the current route-owned armed stop target | `TrainController.tick()` when the train is stopped, `received_path_plan` is still present, the selected cab/travel frame is aligned with that same live authority, the distance-0 lookahead reports zero usable clearance at that current authority boundary (`atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"` for that same live authority), and the current authority boundary is short of `RouteRun.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target` for this live authority. A missing armed target is not a `RUNNING_WAITING` condition: a route-managed run either has an armed target, is in the one-tick gap before Phase 1 arms the next target, or has finished and is being removed. In ATO this is determined by ATC clamping the train to zero. In manual mode, `RUNNING_WAITING` is entered only when the train head is on the block where clearance ended and that boundary is short of the currently armed route stop target; a manual driver who voluntarily stops anywhere else under positive clearance, or who voluntarily stops at the armed destination stop boundary, does not enter `RUNNING_WAITING`. This state is not used for no-cab, wrong-direction cab, authority loss, missing route target, or EB stops. | back to `RUNNING_MOVING` via the reactive speed-based evaluation when `current_speed_kmh > 0` on the next tick after clearance returns and physics produces movement; authority loss (`receive_path_plan(None)`) follows the no-authority braking path — reactive evaluation on the next tick when `current_speed_kmh == 0` and RUNNING_WAITING conditions no longer hold transitions to `IDLE` or `STANDBY`; stopped-only operator commands `set_cab(None)` → `IDLE`, `set_cab("A" or "B")` → `STANDBY`, `set_mode(...)` → `STANDBY` (cab selected) or `IDLE` (no cab), `power_off_cmd()` → writes `pending_power_off`; Phase-3 power-off cleanup then exits through `STANDBY`/`IDLE` to `OFF` |
| `RUNNING_AT_STATION` | Route-managed dwell at a confirmed stop task | `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` after dispatcher-committed completion from the post-physics controller stop check, reached by consuming `dwell_entry_requested` | `TrainController.finish_dwell()` → state-machine-derived stopped ready state; `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` when EB was activated while dwelling → state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected). EB during dwell is not a special case: dwell is interrupted by EB just like any other state, and after release the next eligible Phase 1 sees the dwell task is still active (because dwell was not exited via `finish_dwell()`) and re-enters dwell through the ordinary dwell-pending branch by writing `TrainController.dwell_entry_requested` again. `RouteRun.task_runs[dwell_index].arrived_at` is preserved across the EB cycle. `power_off_cmd()` → writes `pending_power_off`; Phase-3 power-off cleanup calls `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(...)` which calls `finish_dwell()` to exit dwell before OFF teardown. |

- **Reactive speed-based state evaluation**: `TrainController.tick()` evaluates state-machine transitions reactively based on the speed written by the previous tick's `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)`. The controller cannot detect movement before physics has run and updated `current_speed_kmh`, so these transitions are always observed on the tick after physics produces the speed change. The evaluation runs once per tick as a state-machine check at the beginning of `TrainController.tick()`, after the operator-command drain, EB gate, and Phase-1-to-Phase-2 handoff consume slot, and before pre-physics lookahead consumption. The rules are:
  - If `state == STANDBY` and `current_speed_kmh > 0`: transition to `RUNNING_MOVING`. This is the canonical mechanism for manual-mode STANDBY → RUNNING_MOVING; the driver's `set_handle()` or `set_manual_power_percent()` command was processed on a prior tick, physics moved the train, and the controller now observes the result.
  - If `state == RUNNING_WAITING` and `current_speed_kmh > 0`: transition to `RUNNING_MOVING`. This is the canonical mechanism for RUNNING_WAITING → RUNNING_MOVING in both ATO and manual modes; clearance returned, the prior tick's physics produced movement (ATO via `ato_running` handle calculation, manual via the driver's still-active input), and the controller now observes the result.
  - If `state == RUNNING_MOVING` and `current_speed_kmh == 0` and the RUNNING_WAITING entry conditions are met (live authority, aligned cab, zero clearance at authority boundary): transition to `RUNNING_WAITING`.
  - If `state == RUNNING_MOVING` and `current_speed_kmh == 0` and the RUNNING_WAITING entry conditions are NOT met and the train has no live authority: transition to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` / `STANDBY`). This is the no-authority braking completion path.
  - `RUNNING_MOVING` with `current_speed_kmh == 0` under live authority where clearance is positive (driver simply stopped the train manually) stays in `RUNNING_MOVING`; zero speed alone does not exit RUNNING_MOVING when the driver has voluntarily stopped under positive clearance.
- The state machine above is the single canonical operational-state contract. Shared logic must not introduce additional live route/runtime state enums for path completion or cancellation recovery in v1.
- Stopped-only operator commands (`power_off_cmd()`, `set_cab(...)`, and `set_mode(...)`) may be issued from any physically stopped controller state. In v1 they disengage any active ATO run and land in the command-defined stopped target state (`OFF` after pending power-off cleanup, `IDLE`, or `STANDBY`); they do not cause automatic movement just because clearance later returns or dwell later finishes.
- Stopped recovery commands must not hard-code `STANDBY`. When a stopped recovery needs the ordinary powered, non-dwell, non-waiting state, `TrainController` derives it from the table: powered with no cab selected becomes `IDLE`; powered with a cab selected becomes `STANDBY`; powered off remains `OFF`.

**`ato_running` meaning and lifecycle**

- `ato_running` means "an ATO run is currently in progress": the train has been commanded to move under ATO via `TrainController.depart()` / `TrainController.ato_go()`, and that run has not yet been ended by arrival-at-dwell, path completion, authority clear, EB, or any stopped-state command (power / cab / mode). It is a **run-active** flag, not a **mode** flag. The mode flag is `control_mode` (`ato` vs `manual`), which says "what kind of driving the controller is configured for"; `ato_running` says "is an ATO run currently driving the train." A train in ATO mode is not necessarily `ato_running`: before the first `depart()`, between runs, during dwell, and after any run-ending event, `control_mode == ato` but `ato_running == False`. The one nuance is `RUNNING_WAITING`: the train is stopped with zero clearance but the same live authority still exists, so the run has not ended — `ato_running` stays `True` so the controller auto-resumes when clearance returns. A train in manual mode is never `ato_running`.
- `ato_running = True` is set in exactly one place in v1: `TrainController.ato_go()`, which is called only from a successful `TrainController.depart()` acceptance path when `control_mode == ato` and `received_path_plan is not None`. No other command, tick handler, or callback flips this flag to `True`. In particular, `TrainController.set_cab(...)`, `TrainController.set_mode(...)`, `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()`, `TrainController.on_path_complete()`, `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`, `TrainController.finish_dwell()`, and `TrainController.receive_path_plan(...)` never set `ato_running = True`.
- `ato_running` has exactly one writer: `TrainController`. Neither `RouteExecutor` nor `PathDispatcher` nor any train-side mirror writes this flag directly; train-running state is not a route executor's concern. `ato_running = False` is set by `TrainController` on every canonical run-ending event, all of which it owns: successful start of dwell (`enter_station_dwell()`), controller path completion (`on_path_complete()`), any authority clear (`receive_path_plan(None)`), EB activation (inside the controller's EB entry path), `power_off_cmd()` immediately on acceptance before later OFF cleanup, `set_cab(...)`, and `set_mode(...)`. Resuming ATO after any of these events requires another `depart()` / `ato_go()` acceptance. Dispatcher-driven path cancellation reaches this flag only indirectly: `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)` clears the controller inbox through `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`, and it is that controller call — not dispatcher code — that drops `ato_running`.
- `RUNNING_WAITING` does not by itself clear `ato_running`. `RUNNING_WAITING` is a temporary zero-clearance hold under the same live authority; the flag stays `True` so that when the distance-0 ATC limit becomes positive again the controller resumes forward motion without a fresh departure decision. Only the run-ending events listed above (path completion, dwell entry, EB, authority clear, power/cab/mode changes) toggle `ato_running` back to `False`.
- Manual mode never sets `ato_running`. In manual, `TrainController.depart()` marks the current authority ready to go and does not call `ato_go()`; `ato_running` stays `False` throughout manual driving.

**Power/cab/mode command contracts**

- `TrainController.power_on_cmd()` is the explicit `OFF -> powered` transition. It is valid only when power is currently off and is processed in the train's Phase-2 operator-command slot. When a train starts its transaction `OFF`, `SimulationController` skips that train's route Phase 1 because planning cannot run before command drain. The Phase-2 command slot still runs so power-on can be accepted. If `power_on_cmd()` succeeds, the controller is no longer `OFF`, so the transaction continues through the remaining controller logic, `TrainPhysics.tick(...)`, and the normal Phase 3 feedback/cleanup pass; only fresh route planning waits for a later Phase 1 tick. If no cab is selected it transitions to `IDLE`; if a cab is already selected it transitions directly to `STANDBY`.
- `TrainController.power_off_cmd()` is valid only while the train is stopped, and it remains valid when `eb_active == True`. In the phase model it is a stopped-only controller command that validates acceptance during the tick-entry command drain inside `TrainController.tick(...)`, immediately disengages any active ATO run by setting `ato_running = False`, and sets `pending_power_off = True`. It does not cancel dispatcher authority, clear EB, or remove route execution inline. The actual route/dispatcher teardown then follows the **Power-off procedure** through the later eligible Phase 3 cleanup that consumes `pending_power_off`: first cancel the train's active path through `PathDispatcher` and let the ordinary cancellation workflow notify `RouteExecutor` while the route execution is still registered, then call `RouteExecutor.remove_train_route_run(train_id, reason="power_off")`, and only then transition the controller to `OFF`, clear the active cab selection, clear any EB latch, clear controller-owned restart-related state such as `eb_released` and `departure_confirmation_required`, clear `departure_ready_path_id`, and clear `pending_power_off`. The controller turning off does not stop `TrainPhysics`; world physics continues to run.
