- `TrainController.set_cab(cab)` is valid only while the train is stopped. It disengages any active ATO run (`ato_running = False`), clears `departure_ready_path_id` because cab selection is part of the depart-acceptance contract and changing cab invalidates the previous "ready to go" decision, and does not create motion or clear any currently assigned dispatcher authority. Setting `cab` to `"A"` or `"B"` transitions the controller to `STANDBY`; setting `cab = None` transitions the controller to `IDLE`. If the selected cab points away from the current live authority, the controller must not rewrite that authority. Instead it rebuilds lookahead in the selected travel frame; if that frame has no forward authority, the distance-0 ATC limit becomes `0`, so ATC prevents wrong-direction movement under the mismatched cab choice. A fresh `TrainController.depart()` acceptance is required to re-arm departure.
- `TrainController.set_mode(mode)` is valid only while the train is stopped. It switches between `ato` and `manual`, disengages any active ATO run (`ato_running = False`), and does not create or clear dispatcher authority. `set_mode(...)` does NOT clear `departure_ready_path_id`: the depart-acceptance gate is scoped to the current authority and cab, not to the driving mode, so a stopped `ato` ↔ `manual` switch leaves the previously-accepted departure intact (the only practical effect is that ATO will not auto-resume movement because `ato_running` is now `False`). `manual` is a control mode, not a separate operational state in the desired model: after a stopped mode switch the controller remains `STANDBY` when a cab is selected, otherwise `IDLE`.
- `TrainController.set_manual_mode_type(mode_type)` is the canonical setter for `manual_mode_type`. It is valid only while the train is stopped, powered, and `control_mode == manual`. Side effects: when entering `power_percent`, reset `handle_position` to neutral; when entering `simulation`, reset `manual_power_percent = 0`. It preserves cab selection and `received_path_plan`, does not write `departure_ready_path_id`, and does not start movement. The construction default for `manual_mode_type` is `simulation`. The frontend/API and editor/UI must expose this setter through the same operator-input route as `set_handle()` / `set_manual_power_percent()`.
- `TrainController.set_handle()` accepts only when `control_mode == manual`, `manual_mode_type == simulation`, `power_on == True`, a cab is selected, and `eb_active == False`; otherwise it returns `success=False`. `TrainController.set_manual_power_percent(percent)` accepts under those same driver-input conditions except `manual_mode_type` must be `power_percent`. It clamps `percent` to `0..100`, writes `manual_power_percent`, preserves `received_path_plan` and `departure_ready_path_id`, and does not start movement by itself. `TrainController.set_handle()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == power_percent`. `TrainController.set_manual_power_percent()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == simulation` or when any of the shared acceptance preconditions fail.
- There is no separate `TrainController.set_travel_direction(...)` command in v1. Manual direction selection is only by cab selection through `TrainController.set_cab(...)`.
- `TrainController.activate_emergency_cmd()` is the canonical operator-issued EB command and intentionally has no other precondition: emergency means it must be available at all times. Its only acceptance gate is `eb_active == False` (idempotent rejection of a duplicate activation while EB is already latched). It is valid in any motion state, any operational state including `OFF`, with or without a cab, with or without a live path. On acceptance it performs the canonical EB controller-local effects: set `eb_active = True`, force handle to `EB`, clear `ato_running`, clear `departure_ready_path_id`, and when a live path exists write `pending_cancel_request` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.EB`.
- `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` accepts when `power_on == True`, `eb_active == True`, `TrainController.is_stopped() == True`, no EB `pending_cancel_request` remains to be consumed, and `PathDispatcher.active_path_id_by_train[train_id]` no longer references the pre-EB authority. Otherwise it returns `success=False` and leaves controller state unchanged. This gate prevents same-command-drain EB activation/release from setting recovery latches before Phase 3 has committed the EB cancellation.

**received_path_plan schema**

- This is the single canonical schema for the controller's active path-plan payload. Every producer/consumer should refer back to this subsection by name instead of restating the field list elsewhere.
- `received_path_plan` carries exactly `path_id`, `resource_sequence`, and `authority_end_block_id` in v1. It is the controller's only active path-plan payload. `resource_sequence` uses the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema**, and each nested `path_tiles` slice uses the canonical **path_tile entry schema**, so the controller can extend lookahead along the actual geometry without reading `Train.current_path` and without inferring terminal-frame direction from tile coordinates alone. The requested target (`requested_destination_block_id`, `requested_destination_direction`) is stored on the dispatcher-owned `Path` record but is not included in `received_path_plan`; the controller does not see those fields. Completion enforcement against the requested target is internal to `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)`.
- When the live authority already reaches the dispatcher-stored requested target, `authority_end_block_id` in `received_path_plan` equals `Path.requested_destination_block_id`; when the path is a best-effort truncated authority, `authority_end_block_id` is the temporary current boundary.
- `received_path_plan` does not carry a separate cab or head-side field. The ordered `resource_sequence` is the combined already-occupied physical span plus forward path authority in tail-to-authority order. "Tail" means the physical end of the current span farther from `authority_end_block_id`; "authority side" means the physical end closer to the destination and pointing into the path authority. When `TrainController.depart()` is asked to run in ATO or manual with no cab selected, it derives the cab by comparing `TrainSpan.end_a` and `TrainSpan.end_b` against that ordered seeded span and chooses the cab whose physical end is on the authority side. `end_a` is not a general preferred end in this model; it is only the final dead-heat tie-break when every authority-side comparison between the two physical ends remains exactly tied.
- Treat `assigned_path_id`, `ato_path_id`, and `ato_destination_block_id` as computed reads derived from `received_path_plan`, not as independently written runtime fields. The alias mapping is explicit in v1:
  - `assigned_path_id := received_path_plan.path_id`
  - `ato_path_id := received_path_plan.path_id`
  - `ato_destination_block_id := received_path_plan.authority_end_block_id`
  When `PathDispatcher` clears authority by calling `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`, all three disappear automatically.
- Keep `lookahead`, but make it the canonical cached series used by ATC, ATO, UI, and monitor consumers.
- Remove standalone scalar fields such as `target_speed` and `effective_limit` once those values are represented through lookahead-at-distance-0 reads. Keep the top-level exported `speed_limit_reason` only as a compatibility projection for current UI/monitor consumers; its canonical source remains the distance-0 lookahead speed-curve metadata.

**canonical lookahead payload**

- `TrainController.get_lookahead()` is the single canonical exported lookahead payload for ATC, ATO, UI, and monitor consumers. Every producer/consumer should refer back to this subsection by name instead of restating the field list elsewhere.
- In v1, `get_lookahead()` carries exactly:
  - top level: `train_id`, `head_tile`, `head_distance_cm`, `travel_direction`, `cab`, `speed_limit_reason`, `lookahead`
  - nested `lookahead`: `blocks`, `signals`, `switches`, `stations`, `clearance_cm`, `safety_margin_cm`, `authority_boundary_delta_cm`, `speed_curves`
- `head_tile` is the current train head tile coordinate or `None` when unavailable. `head_distance_cm`, `clearance_cm`, `safety_margin_cm`, and `authority_boundary_delta_cm` are centimeters. `travel_direction` is the train's current forward travel direction in the same frame used by movement/traversal. `cab` is the currently selected cab or `None`.
- `blocks`, `signals`, `switches`, and `stations` are ordered forward projections for UI/debug consumers. They are informational read models only; control decisions come from the same payload's clearance and speed-curve data.
- `lookahead.stations` keeps the current compatibility payload name in v1, but each entry refers to a concrete physical platform ahead on the track, not to a logical multi-platform station group.
- Each `lookahead.blocks` entry carries exactly:
  - `id`
  - `name`
  - `state`
  - `start_cm`
  - `end_cm`
  - `length_cm`
  - `tile_count`
  - `speed_limit_kmh`
- Each `lookahead.signals` entry carries exactly:
  - `id`
  - `name`
  - `state`
  - `distance_cm`
- Each `lookahead.switches` entry carries exactly:
  - `x`
  - `y`
  - `name`
  - `position`
  - `distance_cm`
  - `locked`
  where `position` is `"main"` or `"diverging"` for the live switch alignment at that tile, and `locked` is the exported boolean monitor view of whether that switch currently has any future lock claim or train-owned protection claim that blocks conflicting acquisition.
- Each `lookahead.stations` entry carries exactly:
  - `platform_id`
  - `station_id`
  - `name`
  - `distance_cm`
  - `length_cm`
  - `tile_count`
  - `top_speed_kmh`
- `platform_id` in this compatibility payload is the concrete physical platform identifier on the track.
- `station_id` in this compatibility payload is the logical station-group identifier that owns that platform, or `None` when no station group exists. `station_id` must not be reused as the physical platform identifier anywhere in the desired model.
- Each `lookahead.speed_curves` entry carries exactly:
  - `distance_cm`
  - `atc_limit`
  - `operational_limit`
  - optional `atc_limit_reason_code`
  - optional `atc_limit_reason_detail`
  The limit fields are in `km/h`.
- The canonical `atc_limit_reason_code` enumeration is defined here once. Every consumer (`is_stopped()` predicate, post-physics usable-authority stop check, `RUNNING_WAITING` entry classification, no-authority braking diagnostic, monitor/UI display) cross-references this set by name and must not use arbitrary strings or redefine the values elsewhere:
  - `"authority_stop"` — zero-speed restriction at the safety-margin-adjusted end of live authority; the sole code that satisfies the usable-authority stop check for path completion
  - `"caution"` — approaching or inside a caution zone (block in `CAUTION` state or forward block occupied)
  - `"obstruction"` — an in-path resource conflict that blocks forward progress below safe clearance distance
  - `"no_authority"` — no live path authority; stopping at the current occupied-block boundary
  - `"no_cab"` — no cab selected; ATC limit is zero regardless of authority
  - `"wrong_direction_cab"` — selected cab points away from live authority direction; ATC limit is zero
  - `"eb"` — emergency brake latched; ATC limit is zero
- `atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"` is the canonical reason code for the zero-speed restriction at the active stop/end-of-authority boundary. The controller stop-completion predicate must key on this code, not on arbitrary reason strings.
- `authority_boundary_delta_cm` is the live signed separation from the train head to the safety-margin-adjusted authority boundary in the same forward distance axis as the `speed_curves[*].distance_cm` samples and the authority-boundary projection helper. With a live path it is derived from `authority_end_block_id` plus the terminal frame; without a live path it is derived from the current occupied-block boundary. It is diagnostic/control lookahead data, not a route-authored stop-position field.
- Consumers asking "what is the target speed right now?" sample `lookahead.speed_curves` at distance `0`: `operational_limit` at distance `0` is the controller target speed, and `atc_limit` plus its reason metadata at distance `0` is the controller ATC limit/reason. The top-level `speed_limit_reason` field remains part of the exported v1 payload shape for compatibility with current monitor/UI consumers.
- The empty/no-cab/no-train form must preserve this same top-level and nested key shape, with empty lists and zero distances instead of returning a different payload shape. If a compatibility producer cannot compute one of the nested boundary/safety fields on an empty payload, it should still emit the key with `0.0` rather than omitting it.

**Controller tick order**

1. If no train, return immediately.
2. Drain queued operator commands as the first substep of `TrainController.tick(...)`. After that drain, if power remains off, keep the controller in `OFF` and return from the controller tick's powered decision work; `SimulationController` still runs `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` and `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` for storage/physics continuity, but that callback must not enqueue dispatcher/route progress reports and Phase 3 is skipped for that still-off transaction. If `TrainController.power_on_cmd()` succeeded during the drain, the controller is no longer `OFF`, so `TrainController.tick(...)` continues into normal powered controller logic, `SimulationController` runs `TrainPhysics.tick(...)`, and Phase 3 runs normally for that same transaction. Route Phase 1 remains skipped only for the transaction that began while the train was `OFF`; route planning resumes on the next eligible tick.
3. If emergency brake is active:
  - Set handle to `EB`, clear `departure_ready_path_id`, and if a live path exists record or keep `pending_cancel_request` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.EB` for that same train's Phase 3 consume pass, then early-exit the tick while EB remains latched.
  - Stay in the early-exit loop until the train has stopped.
  - Require explicit `release_emergency_cmd()` to clear `eb_active`.
  - While `eb_active == True`, `TrainController.tick()` does not perform post-EB restart logic. `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` owns the transition to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` when powered with no cab selected, or `STANDBY` when powered with a cab selected) and writes `eb_released = True` plus `departure_confirmation_required = True`; that release step does not clear route-owned interrupted-leg state, and the same train's later Phase 3 / next Phase 1 flow observes the latches.
4. Consume controller-owned Phase-1-to-Phase-2 handoff flags in fixed order: `dwell_entry_requested`, then `departure_requested`. This step is skipped when step 2 returned because the controller remains `OFF`, and it is skipped while step 3's EB early-exit path remains active. `TrainController.tick(...)` clears each consumed flag after accepting or rejecting it. Dwell entry runs the `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` acceptance logic and, on success, transitions to `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Departure runs the `TrainController.depart()` acceptance logic for the current `received_path_plan.path_id`; on success it writes `departure_ready_path_id` and starts ATO only through `TrainController.ato_go()` when `control_mode == "ato"`. If both flags are set in the same tick, this is a Phase-1 invariant violation (Phase 1 must never set both because dwell-pending detection short-circuits departure planning); emit a diagnostic through `NotificationCenter` with severity `error`, leave both flags set, and skip the handoff substep so the next eligible Phase 1 can correct the invariant. Do not silently pick a winner. If a queued operator command in step 2 made either flag invalid, emit or record the failed handoff through `NotificationCenter`, leave dispatcher/route state unchanged, and let the next eligible Phase 1 re-evaluate.
5. Run the reactive speed-based state evaluation defined above the state-machine table. This step is skipped when step 2 returned because the controller remains `OFF`, and it is skipped while step 3's EB early-exit path remains active. Otherwise it runs after the command drain, EB gate, and Phase-1-to-Phase-2 handoff consume slot, before pre-physics lookahead consumption.
6. At the beginning of Phase 2, use the cached lookahead produced by the previous post-physics refresh when it exists. If no cache exists yet for the controller's current state, build the canonical pre-physics lookahead now from the current span/speed.
7. If no cab is selected, force ATC limit to zero and return the canonical empty lookahead payload for that tick.
8. Otherwise use that pre-physics canonical lookahead payload for controller decisions on this tick:
  - With a received path plan, lookahead should extend along the path to the current `authority_end_block_id` boundary adjusted by the controller safety margin.
  - Without a path plan, lookahead should extend only through the forward boundary of the current occupied block in the train's current travel frame. For a train spanning multiple blocks, "forward" means the side where the train/path would continue next, not the trailing side.
9. Build ATC state from that pre-physics lookahead payload, including structured reason metadata.
10. Apply ATC overspeed logic.
11. Calculate ATO info.
12. In ATO mode:
  - If `ato_running`, calculate handle with `_ato_calculate_handle()`.
  - If not running and live authority still exists, choose braking handle toward zero and transition to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state once stopped. If live authority has been cleared while rolling, the no-authority braking and possible EB escalation branch in step 20 owns the behavior instead of this generic not-running branch.
13. In manual mode, write a controller-owned command and let physics convert it to speed:
  - In `simulation` submode, store the driver's handle notch in `handle_position` and allow ATC to override with braking.
  - In `power_percent` submode, store the driver's percentage power in `manual_power_percent` as an integer `0..100` and let physics map it through `SpeedProfile.power_speed_curve` when no braking override is active. This value is positive only and represents absolute applied track/motor voltage and its calibrated immediate running speed rather than signed traction vs brake effort. `manual_power_percent` is set-and-forget: once written by a driver `set_manual_power_percent(...)` command or overwritten to `0` by a controller braking override, it stays at that value across ticks until the driver issues a new `set_manual_power_percent(...)`. The controller does not store a separate "driver-commanded" copy and does not auto-restore a prior driver level after a braking override clears; if ATC/service braking/EB forced `manual_power_percent = 0`, the train remains at `0` until the driver explicitly commands a non-zero value again.
  - At any given tick there is only one effective propulsion/brake command for the active mode. In ATO and manual `simulation` mode, the controller/physics speed solution is authoritative and `power_percent` / `track_voltage` are derived from that operating point. In manual `power_percent` mode, `manual_power_percent` is authoritative only while no safety braking override is active; it maps to track voltage and the calibrated absolute speed for that same movement update, so the normal acceleration/deceleration-rate fields do not shape that manual-power command. Raising it immediately commands the higher calibrated voltage/speed point, lowering it immediately commands the lower calibrated voltage/speed point, and holding it steady holds the same commanded power/speed point. In manual `power_percent` mode there is no fallback to handle-based braking: if ATC, service braking, or EB intervenes, the controller forces `manual_power_percent = 0` for that tick (with EB additionally engaging `eb_active`), and physics applies the calibrated zero-speed/zero-voltage outcome from `power_speed_curve` for that tick. `set_handle()` is unavailable while `manual_mode_type == power_percent`, so `handle_position` is not the braking path. There are no negative power-percent values.
14. Stop computing standalone `effective_limit` and `target_speed` scalars; consumers should read controller lookahead at distance 0 instead.
15. `TrainController.tick()` does not latch path completion. It builds the current state-machine outputs from the controller-owned pre-physics lookahead, but it must not mutate `Path` records, release resources, clear authority, or call route callbacks itself. The paired `TrainController.on_physics_updated(...)` callback performs the regular post-physics lookahead refresh from the just-updated span/speed and stores that refreshed payload as the canonical cache for the same train's later Phase 3 stop check and for the next tick's Phase 2 start. Controller-side path completion mutations (standalone path completion or path-backed completion helpers) are removed.
16. Let waiting decisions come from controller lookahead plus the route-owned armed target projection, and let path completion commits come only from the later Phase 3 consume pass. During Phase 3, after earlier queued progress reports have been consumed and any movement-derived deviation handling from them has already run, `SimulationController` runs the post-physics usable-authority stop check against the controller's current state and `RouteRun.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`, when one exists. On success, it samples the current system Unix timestamp as `event_time` before calling `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)`. Any explicit latched teardown such as `pending_cancel_request` is handled only after that normal completion opportunity. `TrainController.tick()` itself does not commit completion.
17. `RUNNING_WAITING` is controller-owned operational state, but the stop-versus-wait classification uses the route-owned armed target through the runtime interface. To enter it: `received_path_plan` is still present, the selected cab/travel frame is aligned with that same live authority, the train has fully stopped, the distance-0 lookahead reports zero usable clearance at the current live-authority boundary (`atc_limit_reason_code == "authority_stop"` for that same live authority), and the live authority boundary is short of the currently armed `RouteRun` stop target (`RouteRun.task_runs[target_waypoint_index].resolved_target`). A missing armed target is never enough to enter `RUNNING_WAITING`: after a route progress event, the next Phase 1 either arms the next target, records/continues dwell, or finishes/removes the route run. If the live authority reaches the route-owned armed stop target and the usable-authority stop check passes, the state is stop completion, not waiting.
18. Do not enter `RUNNING_WAITING` for no-cab, wrong-direction cab, authority loss, or EB-caused stops. Do not enter it solely because the driver manually stopped the train. A driver-commanded manual stop under live authority should remain in `RUNNING_MOVING`; zero speed alone does not change the operational state. In manual mode, `RUNNING_WAITING` is entered only when the train head is on the block where clearance ended (the truncated authority boundary) and the live authority boundary is short of the currently armed route stop target. A manual driver who voluntarily stops anywhere else under positive clearance, or who voluntarily stops at the armed destination stop boundary itself, stays in `RUNNING_MOVING`.
19. `TrainController.tick()` is also the only component that clears `RUNNING_WAITING`: when the same authority still exists and the current zero-clearance-at-the-authority-boundary condition clears so that distance-0 usable clearance becomes positive again, the controller returns to `RUNNING_MOVING` through the reactive speed-based evaluation defined above the state-machine table. The mechanism is the same in both ATO and manual modes: clearance returns, the next tick's physics produces movement (ATO via `ato_running` handle calculation, manual via the driver's still-active handle/power input), and the following tick's reactive speed-based evaluation observes `current_speed_kmh > 0` from `RUNNING_WAITING` and transitions to `RUNNING_MOVING`.
20. A best-effort truncated path stopping at its current authority boundary is a normal source of `RUNNING_WAITING`, not completion, when that boundary is short of the route-owned armed stop target. In that case the same `received_path_plan.path_id` remains live while `RouteExecutor` keeps retrying `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on it.
21. Do not enter `RUNNING_WAITING` for an EB-caused stop. If `received_path_plan` is cleared while the train is moving, treat that as path-authority loss rather than ordinary waiting. The essential event is "authority removed": `PathDispatcher` clears `received_path_plan(None)`, then on the next `TrainController.tick()` after that clear — which is the next tick's Phase 2 controller tick because cancellation now commits in Phase 3 after the current tick's controller/physics work — the controller rebuilds lookahead with no live authority and therefore only to the occupied block boundary. The controller must command ordinary braking toward zero at that current-block boundary using the no-authority ATC limit derived from that rebuilt lookahead. If the no-authority braking / overspeed check determines service braking is insufficient to keep the train within that current block, it escalates to EB. If ordinary braking succeeds and the train reaches a full stop with `eb_active == False`, transition to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected). This is the cancellation/revocation path, not `RUNNING_WAITING`, and it subsumes the generic ATO not-running branch for no-authority rolling trains.
22. After `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()`, transition to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), set `eb_released = True`, and set `departure_confirmation_required = True`. EB activation has already cancelled any live path, so restart requires a fresh path-creation plus fresh explicit departure confirmation rather than automatic controller self-resume.

**ATC speed limit**

- Stop reading signal state directly for ATC restriction selection. In the desired model, CAUTION restriction should come from a single controller-owned global cap such as `CAUTION_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH`, applied when `TrainController` samples a block whose computed `Block.state` is `CAUTION`; signals remain display state derived from blocks rather than an independent ATC input.
- In manual mode with a received path plan, include `_calculate_authority_boundary_limit()` so the effective ATC limit enforces the current authority end correctly: brake to the `authority_end_block_id` boundary in the current live authority frame using the controller safety margin.
- Treat ATC reason reporting as structured metadata on the lookahead/curve payload, not only as a free-form controller string.

**ATO handle calculation**

- Stop rebuilding monitor lookahead inside `_ato_calculate_handle()` and read the canonical cached controller lookahead payload instead.
- If the sampled operational speed curve at distance `0` is already at or below the stop threshold, calculate a direct braking notch immediately.
- Replace the current multi-handle forward simulation wording with a two-point sampling approach:
  - sample the operational speed curve at distance `0`
  - sample one future point at a delta-scaled horizon such as `speed_cm_s * self._tick_delta * LOOKAHEAD_TICKS`
  - combine those samples with the active ATC and block caps to choose power, coast, or brake
- Once the two-point horizon is in place, remove the old fixed-horizon wording and any special near-stop fast-path that only exists to compensate for the old horizon logic.

**Speed curves**

- Build a unified restriction set for the full clearance/authority area and take the minimum of all relevant curves and local limits at each sample point.
- Carry source metadata on each `lookahead.speed_curves` datapoint, such as `atc_limit_reason_code` and `atc_limit_reason_detail`.
- Preserve metadata for which restriction category produced the current minimum ATC limit so monitor/debug UI can explain the displayed number.

**Driver/operator inputs**

- Keep `TrainController.set_handle()` as the active manual-driving command for `simulation` submode manual control. The command may be accepted while manual mode is active, `manual_mode_type == simulation`, the train is powered, a cab is selected, and EB is not latched; the effective command is still clamped by ATC lookahead before physics receives it. `set_handle()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == power_percent`.
- Add `TrainController.set_manual_power_percent()` as the active driver input for `power_percent` submode manual control, and add the matching API endpoint/client method plus train/editor UI control so operator input reaches the controller-owned field through the same route as other manual commands. It accepts under the same manual-input conditions as `set_handle()` except `manual_mode_type` must be `power_percent`: manual mode active, train powered, cab selected, and EB not latched. Like handle input, the command may be accepted before `departure_ready_path_id` exists, but ATC remains authoritative and can override by forcing `manual_power_percent = 0` for that tick. The command clamps to `0..100`, writes `manual_power_percent`, and does not start movement by itself. `set_manual_power_percent()` is rejected (`success=False`) when `manual_mode_type == simulation` or when any of the shared acceptance preconditions fail.
- `TrainController.depart()` is reached through the controller-owned `departure_requested` handoff. `RouteExecutor` writes that handoff according to route departure policy, including one-off operator routes created from direct UI/API movement requests, and explicit operator departure confirmation queues or writes the same handoff. No other component should synthesize departure acceptance by writing `departure_ready_path_id` directly.

**Dispatcher/route-executor inputs**

- Add `receive_path_plan(path_plan)`, called by `PathDispatcher` after successful `create_path()`, after any `extend_path()` that appends one or more new resources to the live authority on the same `path_id`, and with `None` on cancellation or completion release. `received_path_plan` should mirror only the active authority path referenced by `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`.
- `path_plan` must follow the canonical **received_path_plan schema**. Its nested authority geometry must follow the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema** and **path_tile entry schema**; the controller must not be asked to recover terminal-frame direction later from tile coordinates alone.
- In v1, the controller path plan always carries both requested and authorized block targets. The live stop the controller should brake to on this tick is derived from `authority_end_block_id`, the terminal frame in `resource_sequence`, and the controller safety margin.
- `path_plan` should not carry a second lifecycle status enum in the desired model. For the controller, movement authority is binary: `received_path_plan is not None` means there is live authority, and `received_path_plan is None` means there is not.
- Add `on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)`, called by `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` after it computes the next span/speed and tile delta. `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` applies the update to controller-owned `current_span` / `current_speed_kmh`, runs the regular post-physics lookahead refresh from that just-updated span/speed, then builds the canonical progress report from the tile delta, refreshed canonical lookahead, and `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` when live authority exists, and enqueues that report into the per-train Phase 3 mailbox. `SimulationController` later drains that mailbox in Phase 3 by calling `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` in order. This method owns span storage and progress interpretation only; it does not mutate dispatcher path records or route state directly.
- Add `on_path_complete()`, called by `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` when the post-physics controller stop check has passed, dispatcher verifies that the active path reaches its requested target block and direction, and dispatcher commits completion. `TrainController.on_path_complete()` should transition the controller into the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), but it must NOT clear `received_path_plan` itself. `PathDispatcher`, as the sole owner/writer of the pushed path plan, clears authority immediately before the callback by calling `controller.receive_path_plan(None)`. `TrainController.on_path_complete()` must not enter station dwell by itself; `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)` records pending dwell context, and the next eligible `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` call performs dwell entry. `TrainController.on_path_complete()` is not called while EB is still latched; EB activation cancels the path, and post-EB continuation always uses fresh authority.
- Remove `arrive_at_station()` in the desired model and replace it with a new `enter_station_dwell()` acceptance command, used only after a confirmed stopped arrival from dispatcher-committed completion on a path and reached through `dwell_entry_requested`. `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` must accept dispatcher-completed stopped ready state (`IDLE` or `STANDBY`) in the desired model.
- Remove `wait_for_path(reason)` from the desired controller API. Missing path authority is represented entirely by `received_path_plan is None`.
- Keep `TrainController.depart()`, `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()`, and `TrainController.finish_dwell()` as controller acceptance/transition commands, but `RouteExecutor` reaches the first two by setting controller-owned handoff flags rather than by calling them directly in Phase 1. `TrainController.finish_dwell()` is the end-of-dwell state transition (see the Route-executor section for full semantics); it lands in the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), does not clear `received_path_plan`, and does not start movement. Cab selection is preserved across route completion.
- Manual trains should also enter dwell through `dwell_entry_requested` on the next eligible `RouteExecutor.tick_train_phase1(...)` call after confirmed arrival is recorded. The difference from ATO is departure behavior only: in manual mode `TrainController.depart()` records that the current authority has been accepted and the train is ready to go, but it does not itself start movement or call `TrainController.ato_go()`.
- `PathDispatcher` calls `RouteExecutor.handle_path_cancellation(train_id, path_id, cancellation_kind, reason=None)` only after the dispatcher cancel mutation has committed. The callback records diagnostic context and clears live-path correlation; recovery follows the canonical recovery table. Both `cancellation_kind` and `reason` are diagnostic only.
- `RouteExecutor` should not set controller path or destination fields directly; controller authority should be derived entirely from `receive_path_plan(...)`.
- Replace `transition_to_standby()` and `arrive_at_station()` as completion hooks with `on_path_complete()` plus route-owned dwell-entry once completion release is dispatcher-owned and the controller reacts through callbacks only.
- `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` should only clear EB when the train is already stopped and powered, no EB cancellation latch remains pending, and dispatcher no longer has the pre-EB active path for that train. It transitions the controller to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state: `IDLE` if no cab is selected, or `STANDBY` if a cab is selected. Because the earlier Phase 3 EB-cancellation commit has already cancelled any live path, `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` never evaluates a stale path completion predicate. On success it writes the controller-owned latches `eb_released = True` and `departure_confirmation_required = True`; it does not call `RouteExecutor`, does not inspect route target state directly, and does not clear route-owned task-run state or pending cancellation diagnostics itself. The same train's Phase 3 stopped-recovery cleanup clears route-owned cancellation diagnostics once the controller is observed in the stopped ready state, and the next eligible Phase 1 only performs covered-task normalization plus fresh planning.
- After any EB stop, the old active path must not be reused. EB activation requests cancellation of the active path by writing `pending_cancel_request` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.EB`; the same train's later Phase 3 consume pass commits that request through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)`. `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` only clears the EB latch, transitions to the stopped ready state, and writes `eb_released` / `departure_confirmation_required`. A successful explicit new departure decision after fresh authority clears `departure_confirmation_required`. There is no station/terminal exception to retain or complete the pre-EB path; normal route covered-task normalization and replanning handle the train's stopped position after EB release.

**route/dispatcher command contracts**

- `TrainController.receive_path_plan(path_plan)` is an atomic full replacement of the controller's active authority projection. `PathDispatcher` uses it both to push a brand-new path and to refresh the same `path_id` after extension; `None` clears the projection immediately. It is valid while the train is stopped or moving because it is a projection update, not a physics state transition. Every call to `TrainController.receive_path_plan(...)` clears the controller's cached lookahead projection immediately, including same-`path_id` refreshes after `extend_path(...)`; the method does not rebuild lookahead inline. The next Phase 2 pre-physics lookahead read rebuilds it on demand from current span/speed and the new `received_path_plan`. If `path_plan is None` or `path_plan.path_id` differs from the current `received_path_plan.path_id`, `TrainController.receive_path_plan(...)` must clear `departure_ready_path_id`; when `path_plan is None`, it must also clear `ato_running = False` because authority clear is a run-ending event. Any same-`path_id` extension refresh keeps the ready marker and preserves `ato_running`, even when `authority_end_block_id` changes on that same live path.
- `TrainController.depart()` accepts an existing authority when `TrainController.tick(...)` consumes `departure_requested`; it does not create authority and should not be called directly by Phase 1 route code. Returns `(success: bool, reason: Optional[str])`. Acceptance preconditions: `received_path_plan is not None`, `power_on == True`, `eb_active == False`, state is `IDLE` or `STANDBY`, and no accepted same-tick power-off handoff is pending. Failure leaves controller state unchanged. Success writes `departure_ready_path_id = received_path_plan.path_id` (the canonical depart-acceptance gate for stop completion), clears `departure_confirmation_required`, and auto-selects a cab from `received_path_plan.resource_sequence` against `TrainSpan.end_a` / `end_b` if no cab is currently selected (final tie-break: `end_a`). In ATO, success also calls `TrainController.ato_go()` which is the single setter of `ato_running = True`, and the controller transitions to `RUNNING_MOVING`. In manual mode, success transitions to `STANDBY` (or stays in `STANDBY`) without starting physics movement; driver input is still required.
- `TrainController.on_path_complete()` is valid only after `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` verifies that the post-physics controller stop check has already passed, verifies the active path requested-target check, and commits path completion. It transitions the controller to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), must not clear `received_path_plan`, and must not enter dwell itself.
- `TrainController.enter_station_dwell()` is valid only when `TrainController.tick(...)` consumes `dwell_entry_requested` after confirmed stopped arrival from dispatcher-committed path completion for any task with `task_kind == "stop"`. Despite the legacy name, it is the universal stop-task dwell-entry command in v1. It accepts either dispatcher-completed stopped ready state (`IDLE` or `STANDBY`), transitions the controller to `RUNNING_AT_STATION`, and must not create movement or mutate `received_path_plan`.
- `TrainController.finish_dwell()` is valid only from `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. It transitions back to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), preserves cab selection, does not call `TrainController.depart()`, and does not mutate `received_path_plan`. The normal caller is the same train's Phase 3 dwell-complete cleanup.

**Read accessors**

- Add `get_lookahead()` as the canonical accessor for UI and debug consumers. Any consumer asking for target speed or speed-limit reason should sample this payload at distance `0`.
- Add `get_current_span()` as the canonical controller accessor for occupied-span queries. It reads `TrainController.current_span`, the v1 storage home for live physical occupancy and end locators. Keep the payload general and geometry-first: at minimum it must expose `occupied_resources`, `occupied_blocks`, and `occupied_tiles` in physical span order from one physical end of the train to the other, plus explicit end locators for both physical ends. The ordered occupancy is the block/resource-level ownership view; the end locators are the tile-level precise geometry view. Each end locator must carry the end's current `tile`, the current `block_id` when that end is inside a block, the current `resource_id` / `resource_type`, and optional precise in-tile position when available. Convenience reads such as `end_a_resource_id`, `end_b_resource_id`, `end_a_block_id`, `end_b_block_id`, `end_a_tile`, and `end_b_tile` may be exposed as derived fields from that span. `end_a` and `end_b` are the train's two **physical** ends and are cab-independent: they are stable geometric labels for the two ends of the consist and do NOT imply head/tail. The "head" end is whichever physical end corresponds to the currently selected cab in the controller's travel frame; without a selected cab, `current_span` still carries both physical ends but no head/tail labeling. `RouteExecutor` and `PathDispatcher` consumers that need head-side reasoning derive it from controller cab/travel state plus the span; they must not assume `end_a == head`. `TrainPhysics` is the only normal producer of movement `span_update` values, while `TrainController` is the only normal writer of `TrainController.current_span`; `RouteExecutor` / `PathDispatcher` must use `TrainController.get_current_span()` whenever they need the train's current span or physical ends.

**TrainSpan schema**

This is the canonical v1 shape of `TrainController.current_span` and the payload returned by `TrainController.get_current_span()`.

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `end_a` | `SpanEndLocator` | Stable physical end A of the consist. It is cab-independent and does not imply head/tail. |
| `end_b` | `SpanEndLocator` | Stable physical end B of the consist. It is cab-independent and does not imply head/tail. |
| `occupied_tiles` | `List[SpanTile]` | Tile-level coverage in physical order from `end_a` to `end_b`. |
| `occupied_resources` | `List[SpanResourceInterval]` | Contiguous resource coverage intervals in the same `end_a` -> `end_b` physical order. Blocks and switches are both represented. |
| `occupied_blocks` | `List[str]` | Unique block IDs currently covered by the train, in first-seen `end_a` -> `end_b` order. Switch resources are excluded. |

`SpanEndLocator` carries exactly:

- `end_id` — `"A"` or `"B"`
- `tile` — current tile coordinate for that physical end
- `resource_type` — `"block"` or `"switch"` when the end is on a known resource, otherwise `None`
- `resource_id` — block ID or switch ID for that resource, otherwise `None`
- `block_id` — current block ID when the end is inside a block, otherwise `None`
- `entry_edge` — edge used as the end's current in-tile distance frame, when known
- `distance_cm_from_entry` — precise in-tile distance from `entry_edge`, in centimeters, when known

`SpanTile` carries exactly:

- `tile`
- `resource_type`
- `resource_id`
- `block_id`
- `entry_edge`
- `exit_edge`
- optional `start_distance_cm_from_entry`
- optional `end_distance_cm_from_entry`

`SpanResourceInterval` carries exactly:

- `resource_type`
- `resource_id`
- `block_id` for block resources, otherwise `None`
- `start_tile_index` into `occupied_tiles`
- `end_tile_index` into `occupied_tiles`, inclusive

The `occupied_resources` list contains one entry per contiguous physical interval. If a resource ever appears in two disconnected intervals, that is represented as two intervals rather than silently merging them. Consumers that need the forward/head side derive it from selected cab/travel state plus `end_a` / `end_b`; `TrainSpan` itself never relabels `end_a` or `end_b` as head/tail.

`PhysicsSpanUpdate` is the canonical payload from `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` into `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)`. It carries exactly:

- `new_span: TrainSpan`
- `current_speed_kmh: float`
- `added_tiles: List[SpanTile]` in physical traversal order for tiles newly covered by the updated span
- `removed_tiles: List[SpanTile]` in physical vacate order for tiles no longer covered by the updated span
- traversal-frame metadata on each added/removed tile sufficient for `TrainController` to derive block tail-vacate `direction`, where that `direction` is the vacated block's forward `exit_edge` (`N`/`E`/`S`/`W`) for the traversal by which the train left that block

`TrainPhysics` computes this delta by comparing the previous controller-owned span it read at the start of the movement step with the newly integrated span. `TrainController` is the only normal writer of the stored `current_span`: it applies `span_update.new_span`, derives resource entry/release observations from `added_tiles` / `removed_tiles`, packages them into one `ControllerProgressReport`, and enqueues that report into the train's Phase 3 mailbox for later delivery to `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)`.

`TrainPhysics.tick(...)` must emit a `PhysicsSpanUpdate` on every tick, even when `added_tiles` and `removed_tiles` are both empty and the integrated `new_span` / `current_speed_kmh` are unchanged. When nothing changed physically, this is an explicit no-change callback. The always-callback rule keeps `TrainController.current_span` / `current_speed_kmh` canonical and gives Phase 3 a deterministic consume point for queued progress consumption, the post-physics stop check, and latches such as `pending_power_off`.

- Add one shared neighbor/edge helper such as `RailwayNetwork.get_connection_edges(tile_a, tile_b) -> Optional[Tuple[str, str]]`, which answers two questions at once: are the two tiles neighbors, and if so, which edge on each tile connects to the other. `TrainPhysics` and traversal helpers should use this instead of re-deriving neighbor-edge pairs ad hoc.
- Add one shared backend network-graph candidate-query / walk-metrics home in the common network/pathfinding layer, such as `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(...) -> List[GraphWalkCandidate]` if the current dispatcher search is extracted into a class. Thin adapter methods such as `RailwayPathfinder.find_ordered_walk(...)` or `RailwayPathfinder.measure_along_walk(...)` may still exist, but they must all delegate to this same operational graph home. The candidate query takes tile-precise locators, an explicit switch-handling mode (`respect current state` versus `topology-only` search for later acquisition), always skips disabled blocks/switches, and returns zero or more directed walk candidates with whatever ordered tile/block walk payload the caller requested.
- `GraphWalkCandidate` carries exactly this payload:

  | Field | Type | Meaning |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `ordered_walk` | `List[GraphWalkStep]` | Canonical directed walk over graph steps in traversal order. Each step contains enough resource/tile/frame data for dispatcher acquisition, route target comparison, and later distance projection. |
  | `ordered_tiles` | `Optional[List[Tuple[int, int]]]` | Ordered tile IDs for callers that requested tile payload. `None` when `return_tiles=False`. |
  | `ordered_blocks` | `Optional[List[str]]` | Ordered block IDs visited by the walk, with consecutive duplicate block steps collapsed only when they represent the same directed block traversal. `None` when `return_blocks=False`. |
  | `start_locator` | `GraphLocator` | The resolved tile-precise start locator used by the graph search, including block/tile membership and optional in-tile position when supplied by the caller. |
  | `terminal_locator` | `GraphLocator` | The resolved tile-precise terminal locator reached by the candidate. |
  | `terminal_block_id` | `str` | Block ID of the terminal block reached by the candidate. |
  | `terminal_block_direction` | `"forward" \| "reverse"` | Terminal block-relative traversal direction derived from the candidate's directed frame against `Block.ordered_tiles`. |
  | `terminal_frame` | `DirectedBlockFrame` | Terminal directed block frame, including path tiles plus `entry_edge` / `exit_edge` metadata needed by dispatcher/controller projection helpers. |
  | `tile_count` | `int` | Number of tile steps in the directed walk; first comparison key. |
  | `walk_length_cm` | `float` | Centimeter length along the directed walk; second comparison key. |
  | `candidate_rank` | `int` | Stable deterministic tie-break assigned by `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(...)` after `tile_count` and `walk_length_cm`; third comparison key. |

  Every caller that compares candidates must choose by `(tile_count, walk_length_cm, candidate_rank)`. `walk_length_cm` is the common physical-distance metric regardless of caller input type: a waypoint/station/platform input is first resolved into one or more concrete terminal `GraphLocator` values and compared by the same walk distance, while a direct block/tile input already supplies the terminal locator used for the same distance calculation. `candidate_rank` is only for exact ties after those graph metrics. It is assigned by the shared graph home using one deterministic ordering of the directed walk identity: ordered resource IDs, ordered tile coordinates, entry/exit edge sequence, terminal locator identity, and then any remaining stable insertion order from the graph query. Callers must not replace `candidate_rank` with waypoint-specific, UI-order, or layer-local heuristics. If those metrics still remain exactly tied across the remaining current-span end choices, use `end_a` as the final deterministic dead-heat tie-break outside `GraphWalkCandidate`; no other layer-local tie-break is allowed.

- `GraphLocator` carries exactly this payload:

  | Field | Type | Meaning |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `tile` | `Tuple[int, int]` | Tile coordinate anchoring the locator |
  | `resource_type` | `Optional["block" \| "switch"]` | Resource type containing the locator, when known |
  | `resource_id` | `Optional[str]` | Resource ID containing the locator, when known |
  | `block_id` | `Optional[str]` | Convenience block ID when the locator is inside a block; `None` for switch-only locators |
  | `edge` | `Optional["N" \| "E" \| "S" \| "W"]` | Tile edge when the locator is anchored at a graph boundary or caller knows the relevant edge |
  | `distance_cm` | `Optional[float]` | In-tile distance from `edge` along the directed traversal when the caller has a precise position; `None` for tile-only searches |
  | `physical_end` | `Optional["end_a" \| "end_b"]` | Set only for current-train-span start locators so the caller can apply the final `end_a` dead-heat tie-break outside the graph query |

- `DirectedBlockFrame` carries exactly this payload:

  | Field | Type | Meaning |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `block_id` | `str` | Block whose directed traversal is represented |
  | `block_direction` | `"forward" \| "reverse"` | Direction relative to `Block.ordered_tiles` |
  | `path_tiles` | `List[path_tile entry]` | Ordered tile slice through the block using the canonical path-tile entry schema |
  | `entry_edge` | `"N" \| "E" \| "S" \| "W"` | Edge by which the traversal enters the block frame |
  | `exit_edge` | `"N" \| "E" \| "S" \| "W"` | Edge by which the traversal exits the block frame |

- `GraphWalkStep` carries exactly this payload:

  | Field | Type | Meaning |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `step_index` | `int` | Zero-based step index inside `ordered_walk` |
  | `resource_type` | `"block" \| "switch"` | Resource traversed by this step |
  | `resource_id` | `str` | Block or switch ID for the traversed resource |
  | `tile` | `Tuple[int, int]` | Tile coordinate for this graph step |
  | `entry_edge` | `"N" \| "E" \| "S" \| "W"` | Edge used to enter this tile/resource step |
  | `exit_edge` | `"N" \| "E" \| "S" \| "W"` | Edge used to exit this tile/resource step |
  | `block_id` | `Optional[str]` | Block ID when `resource_type == "block"` |
  | `block_direction` | `Optional["forward" \| "reverse"]` | Block-relative traversal direction when the step is inside a block |
  | `switch_required_state` | `Optional[int]` | Required switch state for switch traversal when topology search determines one |
  | `length_cm` | `float` | Physical length contribution of this step |

**Pathfinding-home migration**

- The current operational backend path search lives in `PathDispatcher.find_paths(start_block_id, end_block_id, train_id=None) -> List[PathSegment]` / `PathDispatcher._bfs_find_paths(...)`. That contract is insufficient for the v1 contracts above because it is block-to-block and distance-sorted only; it does not support direction-aware terminal rejection, locator-level (tile-precise) start positions, explicit switch-handling mode, the canonical `(tile_count, walk_length_cm, candidate_rank, final end_a dead-heat tie-break)` comparison rule, or measure-along-walk projection. The refactor must extend or extract that existing backend pathfinding home so it exposes at least:
  - `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(start_locator, end_locator, *, switch_handling, requested_destination_direction=None, exclude_resources=None, return_tiles=True, return_blocks=True, ...) -> List[GraphWalkCandidate]` — direction-aware network-graph search that accepts tile-precise start and end locators (not just block IDs), skips disabled blocks/switches, either respects current switch state or searches topology-only according to `switch_handling`, rejects candidates whose terminal traversal does not satisfy `requested_destination_direction` or `Block.allowed_travel_directions`, and returns comparable walk candidates plus whichever ordered tile/block walk payload the caller asked for.
  - `RailwayPathfinder.find_ordered_walk(...) -> Optional[GraphWalkCandidate]` — optional thin adapter over `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(...)` for callers that specifically need the chosen ordered walk payload.
  - `RailwayPathfinder.measure_along_walk(ordered_walk, locator_a, locator_b) -> float` — in-walk centimeter distance projection between two locators on an already-chosen walk, used by lookahead and platform-end pair comparison.
  - the `GraphWalkCandidate` table above as the only candidate payload contract.
- Once that shared graph home is in place, every layer that needs pathfinding migrates to it: `RouteExecutor` platform-end selection, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` start-side and forward candidate selection, `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` suffix selection, and the boundary-projection helpers all share this single home. The legacy block-to-block `PathDispatcher.find_paths(...)` contract is removed or made a thin adapter over the new graph query in v1; it must not remain as a parallel source of operational path choices.
- The implementation must use the following function table as the canonical operational pathfinding surface. If the shared graph home is implemented as methods on `PathDispatcher` rather than an extracted `RailwayPathfinder` class, keep the inputs/outputs and single-home rule identical.

  | Function | Inputs | Output | Required callers / need | Current nearby implementation |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `RailwayNetwork.get_connection_edges(tile_a, tile_b)` | adjacent tile coordinates | `Optional[Tuple[str, str]]` edge on `tile_a`, edge on `tile_b` | `TrainPhysics.tick(...)`, graph traversal, directed frame construction | Current code has `RailwayNetwork.get_neighbor(...)` plus `TrackSegment.edges`; no single helper currently returns both edges |
  | `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(...)` | `start_locator`, `end_locator`, `switch_handling`, optional `requested_destination_direction`, optional `exclude_resources`, payload flags | `List[GraphWalkCandidate]` sorted/comparable by candidate metrics | `RouteExecutor` platform-end selection, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` start-side selection, `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` candidate selection, `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` suffix selection | Closest backend code is `PathDispatcher.find_paths(...)`, `PathDispatcher._bfs_find_paths(...)`, `PathDispatcher._find_extension_paths(...)`, and `PathDispatcher._bfs_find_extension_paths(...)`; these must be extended/extracted. `static/js/pathfinders.js` has `ConstructionPathfinder.findPath(...)` and `ExistingTrackPathfinder.findPathOnExistingTracks(...)`, but those are editor/construction helpers and are not the runtime operational home. |
  | `RailwayPathfinder.find_ordered_walk(...)` | Same as `RailwayPathfinder.query_graph_walks(...)` plus selection policy when needed | `Optional[GraphWalkCandidate]` chosen by canonical comparison | Thin convenience adapter for callers that need one chosen walk rather than all candidates | No exact current equivalent; closest is `PathDispatcher.find_paths(...)[0]` after sorting, but that lacks new frame semantics |
  | `RailwayPathfinder.measure_along_walk(ordered_walk, locator_a, locator_b)` | chosen `ordered_walk` and two `GraphLocator` values on that walk | signed or absolute centimeter distance as specified by caller | lookahead projection, platform-end pair comparison, graph-nearest route reconciliation | Current `PathSegment.distance_cm` is whole-path only and is not sufficient for locator-to-locator projection |
  | `RailwayNetwork.project_authority_boundary_delta(head_tile, head_entry_edge, head_distance_cm, authority_end_block_id, terminal_frame, safety_margin_cm)` | live head locator data plus exact `DirectedBlockFrame` for the authority end | signed centimeter delta; positive means head is past safety-margin boundary, negative means still short | controller braking diagnostics and stop-boundary projection | No exact current equivalent; must use shared graph/geometry helpers rather than dispatcher path records |

**Manual mode and movement-authority boundary**

- In `simulation` submode, the driver should call `set_handle()`, the controller should store `handle_position`, and `TrainPhysics` should integrate it into speed while ATC can still override with braking.
- In `power_percent` submode, the driver should call `TrainController.set_manual_power_percent()`, the controller should store `manual_power_percent`, and `TrainPhysics` should convert it through `SpeedProfile.power_speed_curve` in the same tick whenever no braking override is active.
- In that same `power_percent` submode, the converted power-percent command is the absolute voltage/speed command and overrides the normal acceleration/deceleration-rate fields while no safety braking override is active. `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` must set the `span_update.current_speed_kmh` for that tick to the calibrated speed implied by `manual_power_percent`; the model assumes negligible momentum under direct voltage control, so there is no service-rate ramp between manual power-percent settings. Service braking and EB do not use a handle-based braking flow in `power_percent` submode: when controller safety logic requests braking (ATC override, service braking, or EB), the controller forces `manual_power_percent = 0` for that tick (with EB additionally engaging `eb_active`), and physics applies the calibrated zero-speed/zero-voltage outcome from `power_speed_curve`. There is no signed negative power-percent command.
- Remove `manual_target_speed` entirely; no hidden shim or compatibility field should remain.
- In manual mode with a received path plan, add `_calculate_authority_boundary_limit()` so the controller brakes to the current authority end boundary using the controller safety margin.
- Without a received path plan, the current-block boundary should remain the fallback authority limit.

**Completion ownership and timing**

- Make `TrainController` the sole owner of post-physics usable-authority stop interpretation and `PathDispatcher` the sole owner of path completion commit. The controller exposes whether the train is now stopped at the usable end of live authority; dispatcher decides whether that completed stop applies to the active path and then mutates path/resource/route state.
- Let `TrainController` react to dispatcher-committed completion through `on_path_complete()`, but stop letting the controller decide independently that a path-backed run is complete.
- Keep `PathDispatcher` as the sole writer of `TrainController.received_path_plan`; `on_path_complete()` is a reaction hook, not an inbox-mutating hook.
- Remove helper logic such as `path_backed_run_active` / `_is_standalone_path_complete()` once controller-reported, dispatcher-committed completion is authoritative.
- Tighten the invariant so path completion is emitted only after the train has already moved for that tick, the post-physics controller stop check says the train is now stopped at the usable end of live authority under the canonical usable-authority stop predicate, and `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` accepts it for the active path by verifying both that `Path.authority_end_block_id == Path.requested_destination_block_id` and that the terminal authority traversal direction equals `Path.requested_destination_direction`. The controller safety margin remains a braking/diagnostic target; it is not a separate centimeter-tolerance completion gate.

**Controller invariants**

- There should be no `self.target_speed` field once lookahead-at-distance-0 becomes the canonical source of target speed.
- Controller-owned live control/physical state and train-owned persistent/configuration state should be cleanly separated.
- Remove the runtime fallback read `self.ato_destination_block_id or getattr(train, "ato_destination_block_id", None)` from controller ATO logic; in the new model destination state comes exclusively from `received_path_plan`.
- `SimulationController.get_or_create_controller(train_id)` should preserve the 1:1 train/controller pairing without recreating authority from train-side runtime mirrors. Its job is only "make sure a controller object exists and is registered for this `train_id`." In v1, controller existence is a hard precondition for any dispatcher authority push: callers must ensure the controller exists before `create_path(...)`, `extend_path(...)`, or any `receive_path_plan(...)` write. `SimulationController` must not construct or write the plan payload itself, and there is no late-bind recovery hook such as `PathDispatcher.on_controller_available(train_id)`.

## TrainPhysics

**Movement-authority input in `TrainPhysics`**

- `TrainPhysics` should continue to execute physical integration (speed update, span/end-locator update, occupancy tracing, and tail tracing), but the canonical live storage is `TrainController.current_span` and `TrainController.current_speed_kmh`. `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` computes each `span_update` (`new_span`, `current_speed_kmh`, ordered `added_tiles`, ordered `removed_tiles`, and traversal frame metadata) and passes it to `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)`, which applies the controller-owned storage write. `TrainPhysics` should not own path identity, dispatcher authority state, progress interpretation, completion, or a separate canonical position store on `Train`.
- To choose exits on ambiguous geometry, `TrainPhysics` normally needs a controller-provided, read-only authority-geometry projection derived from `TrainController.received_path_plan.resource_sequence`. The minimum movement data is the ordered `resource_sequence` / `path_tiles` geometry from that active plan, plus enough local direction context to choose the correct next edge through the current tile.
- `TrainPhysics` does **not** need dispatcher ownership fields such as `path_id` to step the train, and it does not need requested/authorized centimeter stop targets for physical integration. Centimeter stop-target fields are not part of the v1 path/controller contract; controller braking logic uses authority-boundary geometry plus the controller safety margin.
- `TrainPhysics` should obtain that geometry projection from `TrainController.received_path_plan` rather than from a separate `TrainController.get_path_tiles()` API. `Train.current_path` should not remain a movement input in the desired model.
- `TrainPhysics` should read the current live position through the controller-owned span and should write movement only by sending `span_update` to `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)`, not through any train-entity position field. Initial placement is established by the API `SimulationController.add_train(...)` flow writing `TrainController.current_span` before the first tick. V1 does not define a legacy save-data placement migration path; old runtime placement mirrors must not remain a runtime `Train` field after the controller span has been initialized.
- Exception for cancellation/revocation braking: if `PathDispatcher` has already cleared `received_path_plan` to revoke authority while the train is still rolling, `TrainPhysics` must still keep moving the train according to speed and the current live network state. That post-revocation movement relies on the physical `Switch.state` / live track geometry that exists in the network at that moment, not on retained authority locks or a second copy of path ownership surviving in the controller inbox.
- Once live switch state ownership moves to `Switch`, `TrainPhysics._advance_end()` and tail tracing must call the updated `TrackSegment` traversal helpers using the resolver input defined by the `TrackSegment` helper contract above.

**Block occupancy**

- Route normal tail-vacated transitions through `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` and `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)`, which routes block releases through `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated()` so `OCCUPIED -> CAUTION -> FREE` stays in one state machine.
- `TrainPhysics` must not write `Block` fields directly and must not call block-state mutators for normal occupancy, CAUTION, or release. It computes movement `span_update` values, and the controller/dispatcher feedback chain performs all block writes through `BlockManager`.
- `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` must not include a block in `tail_vacated_resources` just because the tail left one tile of that block. `PathDispatcher` may call `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated()` only after the controller report proves the train no longer occupies **any** tile in that block.
- Replace direct `clear_block()` calls for normal CAUTION clearing with the cascade inside `on_block_tail_vacated()` via `_reevaluate_caution_blocks()`, not a separate `release_block()` step.

**Tail-vacate flow in `TrainPhysics`**

- Remove direct calls to `block_manager.set_block_to_caution()` and `block_manager.clear_block()` for normal vacate transitions.
- On tail-vacate, `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` includes a typed resource-release observation in `tail_vacated_resources`, packages it into the same `ControllerProgressReport`, and enqueues that report for the train's later Phase 3 consume pass. For blocks, the controller must report the vacate only after the updated `current_span` proves the train no longer occupies any tile in that block; tile-level vacate is not enough. For switches, tile-level vacate of the switch resource is sufficient. The resource event obtains `resource_id` from the same tile-lookup mechanism already used for entry detection: block IDs come from the `RailwayNetwork` tile-to-block reverse index computed from `Block.ordered_tiles`, and switch IDs come from `TrackSegment.switch_id` on switch tiles. The controller report carries block tail-vacate `direction` as the vacated block's forward `exit_edge` for the traversal by which the train left that block. During Phase 3, `PathDispatcher` routes the release according to `resource_type`: blocks go through `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated(block_id, train_id, direction)` so `OCCUPIED -> CAUTION -> FREE` stays in one state machine; switches go through the `Switch` entity by clearing `Switch.occupied_by` and then clearing train-owned protection sourced from that vacated switch.
- `_caution_blocks` tracking in `TrainPhysics` becomes unnecessary once `BlockManager` handles the full release lifecycle internally; remove that legacy field and its associated maintenance logic in the desired model.

**Voltage behavior**

- Stop reading `controller.target_speed` and `train.manual_target_speed` once those standalone fields are removed.
- Keep the terminology explicit and simple: `track_voltage`, `power_percent`, and `speed_kmh` are three different values in the desired model. **Canonical home for `track_voltage` is `TrainPhysics`**: it is computed each tick from the authoritative source for the active mode (controller/physics speed solution in ATO/manual-simulation; `manual_power_percent` mapped through `SpeedProfile.power_speed_curve` in manual `power_percent` mode). `track_voltage` is not a stored field on `TrainController` or persisted on `Train`; it is per-tick derived state owned by `TrainPhysics` and emitted to `BlockPowerController` as that tick's hardware output.
- `TrainPhysics` should pass the current `track_voltage` value to `BlockPowerController` as the software-to-hardware output layer for block voltage emission.
- The active mode determines which value is authoritative. In ATO and manual `simulation` mode, `speed_kmh` is the controller/physics source of truth and `power_percent` / `track_voltage` are derived from that current operating point. In manual `power_percent` mode, `manual_power_percent` is the source of truth and `speed_kmh` / `track_voltage` are derived from that commanded power level in the same movement update; the manual power command sets the absolute calibrated voltage/speed point instead of being integrated through the normal service acceleration/deceleration rates.
- In manual `power_percent` mode, that authority applies only while no controller braking override is active. There is no handle-based speed-up/speed-down integration in this submode: without a braking override, `TrainPhysics` uses the calibrated `power_percent -> speed_kmh` result directly as the movement speed for that tick. If ATC/service braking or EB is active, the controller forces `manual_power_percent = 0` for that tick (with EB additionally engaging `eb_active`), and `TrainPhysics` uses the calibrated zero-power / zero-speed outcome for that tick rather than a handle-based braking command.
- In `power_percent` manual mode, `TrainPhysics` should use the profile's voltage/power/speed calibration directly rather than reading removed scalar target-speed fields or applying the service acceleration/deceleration rates to the manual-power command. The calibration must be usable in both directions so physics can convert between the authoritative value for the active mode and the derived outputs it needs to maintain. These mappings are owned by `SpeedProfile` and should be documented exactly this way:
  - `track_voltage <-> power_percent`
  - `power_percent <-> speed_kmh`
- When `TrainPhysics` needs `track_voltage`, it derives it from the current authoritative mode input: `speed_kmh -> power_percent -> track_voltage` in ATO and manual `simulation` mode, or `manual_power_percent -> track_voltage` plus the calibrated absolute `speed_kmh` command in manual `power_percent` mode. In manual `power_percent` mode without a braking override, that calibrated `speed_kmh` is written into the same `PhysicsSpanUpdate.current_speed_kmh` that carries the movement result.
- Do not introduce extra names for these concepts in the desired model. Keep the doc wording in terms of voltage, power percent, and speed, and keep the mappings distinct.

**Physics invariants**

- `TrainPhysics` should not own manual-mode policy; it should execute controller commands and maintain physical state only.
- `TrainPhysics` no longer relies on a separate track-level path-lock field. The future-authority role is now played by `Block.reserved_by_path` / `Block.reserved_for_train` on the block side and `Switch.locks` on the switch side. Movement remains geometry-driven; once the train enters a resource, switch integrity during traversal is enforced by `Switch.occupied_by` plus any `Switch.train_protection_claims` rather than by a global track-level path lock or retained path claim. This is not a transitional/migrated version of the old field — the mechanism is different, so no transitional phase is needed.

## SpeedProfile

`SpeedProfile` carries motion-rate data plus per-train calibration in the vocabulary the rest of the system should use directly: voltage, power percent, and speed. The calibration defines bidirectional mappings between `track_voltage` and `power_percent`, and between `power_percent` and calibrated `speed_kmh`. Keep those mappings explicit in the doc instead of introducing extra terminology.

- Keep these fields on `SpeedProfile`:
  - `profile_id`: unique profile identifier.
  - `name`: display name for UI/debug/profile selection.
  - `startup_voltage`: minimum `track_voltage` that produces movement. Below this threshold the train remains stopped.
  - `max_voltage`: top of the usable `track_voltage` range for the first mapping stage.
  - `power_speed_curve`: stored calibration mapping `(power_percent, speed_kmh)` for the second stage.
  - `lighting_voltage`: voltage used for stopped lit states.
  - `acceleration_rate`: service acceleration rate in `km/h/s`.
  - `deceleration_rate`: service braking rate in `km/h/s`.
  - `emergency_brake_rate`: emergency-brake deceleration rate in `km/h/s`.
  - `friction_rate`: passive/coasting deceleration rate in `km/h/s`.
  - `operational_decel_ratio`: comfort-braking fraction used when ATO builds operational braking curves.
  - `air_resistance_enabled`: whether the drag model is active.
  - `drag_coefficient`: drag strength used by the drag model.
  - `reference_speed_kmh`: reference speed used to scale the drag model.
- Remove `speed_voltage_curve` and `min_start_voltage` from the desired model. They belong to the old one-stage speed-to-voltage design and must not remain in the target schema.

**SpeedProfile constraint**

- `SpeedProfile` is still not a speed limit object under the desired model. It expresses motion-rate data and per-train calibration (`startup_voltage`, `max_voltage`, `power_speed_curve`), not any form of speed ceiling. Train maximum speed and block maximum speed remain separate, independently enforced constraints.

**Speed-profile addition**

- Keep the desired-model calibration wording in three explicit values: `track_voltage`, `power_percent`, and `speed_kmh`.
- The calibration has two bidirectional mappings and both should be stated directly in the doc:
  1. `track_voltage <-> power_percent`
  2. `power_percent <-> speed_kmh`
- `startup_voltage` and `max_voltage` define the voltage range used by the first mapping. Below `startup_voltage`, the train does not move. `max_voltage` is the top of the usable range. In v1, `track_voltage <-> power_percent` is linear across that usable range, so reversing from `power_percent` back to `track_voltage` uses the same linear interpolation.
- `power_speed_curve` is the stored `(power_percent, speed_kmh)` mapping used by the second step. `power_percent` is the intermediate normalized control value between voltage and speed; it is not a second name for either of them. In v1 this may be linear by default, but it is still stored explicitly as profile calibration data rather than inferred ad hoc elsewhere. Reverse lookup from `speed_kmh` to `power_percent` uses the inverse interpolation of this same stored curve.
- The motion-rate fields (`acceleration_rate`, `deceleration_rate`) govern ATO, manual `simulation` handle integration, service/emergency braking, and coasting; they do not shape manual `power_percent` response (that submode applies the calibrated speed immediately, see TrainPhysics).
- Every train should have a default `SpeedProfile` carrying these calibration fields so the `power_percent` manual mode always has a defined voltage/power/speed mapping, even before per-train tuning is customized.

## Signals

**Signals**

- Signals remain display-only in the desired model. CAUTION handling moves to `TrainController.CAUTION_SPEED_LIMIT_KMH` (enforced via the lookahead / ATC path), not to signal aspects. `RailwayNetwork.update_signals_from_blocks()` still produces aspect values for the UI, but no controller or physics layer consumes them as a speed restriction.

## Power

**Power-controller role**

- Keep `BlockPowerController` focused on the software-to-hardware boundary only: it accepts a voltage number and applies it to block outputs.
- `TrainPhysics` computes `track_voltage` from the current authoritative operating input for the active mode: `speed_kmh -> power_percent -> track_voltage` in ATO and manual `simulation` mode, or `manual_power_percent -> track_voltage` plus the calibrated absolute `speed_kmh` command in manual `power_percent` mode. In manual `power_percent` mode without a braking override, the calibrated speed is effective immediately in `PhysicsSpanUpdate.current_speed_kmh`. `BlockPowerController` applies that already-computed voltage to the hardware-facing block outputs.
- Do not let it own manual-mode state or read `manual_target_speed`.

## Data Flow To UI

**API payload ownership**
