- Each runtime/entity owner should expose its own API payload through its own `to_dict()` (or equivalent read-model method) rather than merging changed live fields back into another entity's payload. `Train.to_dict()` owns persistent/configuration train data. `TrainController.to_dict()` owns live onboard/control/physical diagnostics. Dispatcher-owned path progress is exposed through `PathDispatcher.get_active_path_progress(train_id)`.
- In v1, any composite runtime snapshot that currently returns the network/train view must expose three sibling payload groups with this exact ownership split: `trains` as the list of `Train.to_dict()` payloads, `controllers_by_train_id` as a map `train_id -> TrainController.to_dict()`, and `dispatcher_progress_by_train_id` as a map `train_id -> {path_id, status, current_resource_index}` resolved from `PathDispatcher.get_active_path_progress(train_id)`. Other detail endpoints may present the same owned data in a different wrapper, but they must preserve this ownership split and must not merge controller-owned or dispatcher-owned live fields back into `Train.to_dict()`.

**`Train.to_dict()` payload**

- `to_dict()` should expose all canonical fields owned by the `Train` entity for convenience.
- Export only persistent/configuration train state from `Train.to_dict()`, because those are the fields owned by `Train`.
- Remove `position`, `current_speed`, `state`, `target_speed`, `speed_limit_reason`, `assigned_path_id`, `ato_destination_block_id`, `current_path`, `path_index`, ATO info, lookahead data, and other live physical/control diagnostics from `Train.to_dict()` when those values are no longer canonical `Train` fields in the refactor.
- Read live operational diagnostics from `TrainController.to_dict()` instead.

**`TrainController.to_dict()` payload**

- `to_dict()` should expose all canonical fields owned by the `TrainController` entity for convenience.
- Export all live operational state, including `handle_position`, `manual_mode_type`, `manual_power_percent`, and `departure_ready_path_id`.
- Export live physical state from the controller-owned storage, including `current_span` and `current_speed_kmh`, for UI/debug consumers that previously read position or occupied tiles from `Train.to_dict()`.
- Export ATO state using `ato_destination_block_id` derived from `received_path_plan.authority_end_block_id`.
- Export `received_path_plan` when present using the canonical **received_path_plan schema**. This is the sole controller/UI source of truth for the active path plan, and its nested authority geometry must continue to follow the canonical **resource_sequence entry schema** and **path_tile entry schema**.
- Do not make `TrainController.to_dict()` the owner of live path-progress state in v1. `PathDispatcher` remains the sole owner of `current_resource_index`; if UI needs that progress projection, expose it through a dispatcher-owned read model such as `PathDispatcher.get_active_path_progress(train_id) -> {path_id, status, current_resource_index}`, resolved from the dispatcher's own active-path registry for that `train_id`, rather than copying ownership into `TrainController` or pushing per-tick sequence-index writes into the controller.
- Do not keep `TrainController.get_path_tiles()` in v1. UI/debug consumers that need active authority geometry should read `received_path_plan.resource_sequence`; consumers that need current physical occupancy should read `TrainController.get_current_span()`.
- Expose the full canonical lookahead payload as the single source of truth for current target speed, ATC limit, speed-limit reason, and forward curve rendering.

**UI data-flow anchor**

- Treat controller payload as the source of truth for live operational/manual/physical state, and train payload as the source for persistent/configuration state.
- A UI asking "what is the train's target speed right now?" should call `controller.get_lookahead()` and sample at distance `0`.

## End-To-End ATO Path Run

**End-to-end path-authority handoff**

- `PathDispatcher` pushes the canonical **received_path_plan schema** via the controller path plan; `controller.assigned_path_id` is a derived read of that plan's `path_id`.
- `RouteExecutor` should ensure the train's `TrainController` exists before this path-authority handoff happens in Phase 1.
- `RouteExecutor` should not write `assigned_path_id` or `ato_destination_block_id` directly in the desired model.

**End-to-end ATO run**

This is a high-level walk-through; canonical contracts live in the relevant sections (see **Active-path completion procedure**, **Planning Kernel**, **Route-run state**, **Phase ownership table**).

1. Phase 1: covered-waypoint normalization, arm the next waypoint, resolve one concrete `(requested_destination_block_id, requested_destination_direction)`, and call `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)` (or `extend_path(...)` if a live path exists), using `acquisition_mode = route.acquisition_mode`. If a resolved pass-through target block is already covered, normalization skips it and advances to the next; if a resolved stop target block is already covered, the stop remains armed and uses ordinary same-block stop authority/completion.
2. Phase 2: controller lookahead / ATC / ATO command generation, then physics integrates and emits boundary-by-boundary `PhysicsSpanUpdate` callbacks.
3. Phase 3: drain queued progress reports (movement-derived cancellation commits inline); run the post-physics usable-authority stop check; on success call `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(train_id, event_time)` per the **Active-path completion procedure**; then dwell-complete, stopped-recovery, `pending_cancel_request`, `pending_route_abort`, and `pending_power_off` in the canonical order.
4. Best-effort retries call `PathDispatcher.extend_path(...)` on the same `path_id` until the live authority reaches the requested target. `blocked_no_growth` keeps the live path; structural `failure_kind` (`no_viable_path`, `invalid_reverse`) latches cancel and triggers cancel-and-replan.
5. Stop arrival records pending dwell. The next eligible Phase 1 writes `TrainController.dwell_entry_requested`, and the following Phase 2 consume slot enters `RUNNING_AT_STATION`. Phase 3 dwell-complete checks `RouteTask.should_depart(...)`, calls `TrainController.finish_dwell()`, marks the dwell task finished, and either removes the route run if no tasks remain or leaves the next leg unarmed.

**End-to-end manual-arrival note**

- Manual trains use the same dispatcher-committed arrival and next-eligible-Phase-1 `TrainController.dwell_entry_requested` path as ATO trains. The only difference is departure behavior: in manual mode `TrainController.depart()` marks the current authority ready to go and does not call `TrainController.ato_go()` or start movement by itself.

**End-to-end post-EB restart note**

- EB recovery follows the canonical EB workflow state machine below. The pre-EB path is never reused; after EB release, `TrainController.departure_confirmation_required` forces the next fresh-authority departure to require explicit confirmation even if the route or waypoint policy is automatic.

**EB workflow state machine**

This is the single canonical step-by-step contract for EB activation, release, and post-EB restart in v1. Shared contracts must not invent separate EB-recovery state flags on `TrainController` or `RouteExecutor` beyond the ones listed below.

| Step | Trigger | Actor | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. EB activation | ATC overspeed escalation, no-authority insufficient-service-braking escalation, or operator-issued `TrainController.activate_emergency_cmd()` | `TrainController.tick()` (Phase 2) for all EB paths: operator-issued EB is accepted during the tick-entry command drain, while ATC / no-authority EB is raised later in the same tick call | Sets `eb_active = True`, forces handle to `EB`, clears `ato_running = False`, clears `departure_ready_path_id`, records `pending_cancel_request` with `cancellation_kind=PathCancellationKind.EB` if a live path exists, and enters the early-exit tick loop. The same train's later Phase 3 consume pass commits that cancellation through `PathDispatcher.cancel_path(...)`, sharing the same release path as every other cancellation kind; if the active path was already cleared earlier in the same Phase 3 pass, the dispatcher cancel mutation is skipped per the **Phase-3 latched-cancel consumption when no active path** rule and the latch is still consumed (controller-local EB state stays owned by the EB workflow). Ordinary tick work is skipped until the train stops and EB is released. |
| 2. Rolling stop under EB | Physics integrates EB deceleration | `TrainPhysics.tick()` | Train decelerates according to `SpeedProfile.emergency_brake_rate`; `current_span` and `current_speed_kmh` continue to update normally. No additional authority completion/cancellation side effects fire during rolling beyond the earlier EB-cancellation request, but physics remains authoritative: `TrainPhysics.tick(...)` continues emitting span updates, and the same train's Phase 3 consume pass continues committing physical entry and tail-vacate resource updates while EB is latched. |
| 3. Full stop under EB | `TrainController.is_stopped()` returns `True` | `TrainController.tick()` | Controller remains in the EB early-exit loop with `eb_active == True`; no state transition yet. Dispatcher path completion is explicitly suppressed while `eb_active == True`. |
| 4. EB release | `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` called while stopped and powered after the EB cancellation latch has been consumed and dispatcher has cleared the pre-EB active path | operator | Clears `eb_active = False`; transitions controller to `IDLE` (no cab) or `STANDBY` (cab selected), sets `eb_released = True`, and sets `departure_confirmation_required = True`. If the EB cancellation is still pending or the old live path still exists, the release command fails/defer-reports through `NotificationCenter` and does not set the recovery latches. `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()` does not call `RouteExecutor`, does not inspect or complete the cancelled pre-EB path, and does not clear route-owned interrupted-leg state itself. |
| 5. EB restart/replan | `TrainController.eb_released == True` and/or `RouteRun.path_cancelled == True`, and the same train's later Phase 3 stopped-recovery cleanup observes the controller in `IDLE` / `STANDBY` with `received_path_plan is None`, EB and power-off latches clear | Phase 3 cleanup followed by the next eligible `RouteExecutor._execute_route_for_train(...)` Phase 1 tick | Phase 3 clears `eb_released`, clears `RouteRun.path_cancelled` when a route run exists, resets diagnostic route status to `RUNNING`, and leaves the next leg unarmed. The next eligible Phase 1 reruns the ordinary planning kernel (`RouteExecutor._advance_past_covered_waypoints(...)` → `_arm_next_target(train_id)` → fresh `PathDispatcher.create_path(...)`). Because `departure_confirmation_required == True`, `RouteExecutor` resolves the effective departure mode to manual confirmation and does not automatically write `TrainController.departure_requested` for that fresh authority. |
| 6. `departure_confirmation_required` clear | Successful explicit `TrainController.depart()` acceptance (ATO or manual) after fresh authority exists | `TrainController.depart()` | On success, `TrainController.depart()` writes `departure_ready_path_id` for the current authority and clears `departure_confirmation_required = False`. Failed departure attempts leave the flag set. Phase-3 power-off cleanup also clears `departure_confirmation_required`; `RouteExecutor` never writes it. |

- `eb_active` is controller-owned, but EB activation must request dispatcher cancellation through `pending_cancel_request` immediately when a live path exists. The actual cancellation side effects still run only inside `PathDispatcher` during the same train's later Phase 3 consume pass; `TrainController` never releases resources itself.
- EB recovery uses the canonical stopped-recovery latches: `RouteRun.path_cancelled` for route-owned interrupted-leg cleanup and `TrainController.eb_released` / `TrainController.departure_confirmation_required` for controller-owned EB-release handoff and manual departure confirmation. No EB-specific route hook or auto-depart flag exists in v1.

## Logic Review Checklist

**Review checklist**

- Is the controller receiving the canonical **received_path_plan schema** when path-end stopping matters?
- Does each `resource_sequence` entry carry the ordered `path_tiles` slice using the canonical **path_tile entry schema**, with explicit `entry_edge` / `exit_edge` on both the resource entry and each nested tile entry, needed for controller lookahead and UI geometry after `Train.current_path` is removed?
- Is controller lookahead the only source of target speed and speed-limit reason?
- In manual mode, does ATC enforce the end of the assigned path as a hard stop through `_calculate_authority_boundary_limit()`?
- Can any path-complete event still fire while the train is braking or creeping toward the terminal authority end, instead of waiting for the post-physics controller stop check to say the train is stopped at the usable end of live authority and `PathDispatcher.try_complete_active_path(...)` to verify that the active authority reaches the requested target before committing completion?
- Does `_ato_calculate_handle()` read only the current tick's canonical lookahead payload, not stale scalar fields?
- Does `RouteExecutor` read controller state rather than mirrored runtime state on `Train`?
- Does `RouteExecutor` resolve waypoint-authored block-relative direction preferences into one concrete `requested_destination_direction` before calling `PathDispatcher.create_path()` or `PathDispatcher.extend_path()`, without relying on platform-level direction fields?
- Do `RouteExecutor` and `PathDispatcher` use the same shared pathfinder/current-span workflow, including the same deterministic tie-break, for start-side and platform-end selection?
- Do `RouteExecutor` and `PathDispatcher` obtain head/tail/current-span data only through `TrainController.get_current_span()`, with `TrainPhysics` remaining the movement-update producer and `TrainController` remaining the writer for controller-owned span state?
- For a pass-through waypoint whose resolved target block is already physically covered, does `RouteExecutor` skip that waypoint under covered-waypoint normalization, while a stop waypoint whose block is already covered remains a stop task requiring ordinary same-block stop authority/completion and dwell exit?
- For explicit local/manual same-block or same-tile dispatcher movement, does the request carry an explicit `requested_destination_direction`, and does dispatcher pathfinding reject ambiguous or wrong-direction candidates rather than guessing?
- Are repeated resources rejected during both `create_path()` and `extend_path()`?
- In `best_effort` mode, does the system keep one live `path_id`, extend `resource_sequence` in place, and avoid treating a truncated-but-live authority as a creation failure?
- Does `best_effort` creation fail instead of storing a live authority when the head is on a switch and no forward block can be acquired?
- Do dispatcher progress/completion/release callbacks all identify active authority by `train_id` rather than by caller-supplied `path_id`, with `PathDispatcher` resolving active progress from its own database rather than from physics or train mirrors, while delayed release of a still-occupied block or switch resolves from the resource itself after authority release?
- Are multi-train routes using per-train `RouteRun` objects (current-code class may still be `RouteExecutionState`) as the only canonical progress store, with no route-level runtime progress summary fields retained in the target model for now?
- Does `RouteExecutor._advance_past_covered_waypoints(...)` resolve station/platform targets as needed, skip already-covered `task_kind == "waypoint"` tasks, and avoid skipping `task_kind == "stop"` tasks solely because the target block is covered?
- On active-run plan replacement, skip-to-waypoint, or other retarget reconciliation, does `RouteExecutor` clear the affected `RouteRun.current_route_status` target fields and replan from the train's current physical position without synthetic progress jumps?
- Does stop completion require the post-physics usable-authority stop check, and are controller deviation cases split into `PathCancellationKind.OUT_OF_SEQUENCE` and `PathCancellationKind.AUTHORITY_OVERSHOOT`?
- Does the normal `CAUTION -> FREE` reevaluation require the forward neighbour (in `caution_direction`) to be non-occupied, while still allowing the explicit completion-time `force` exception in `release_path_block_reservations(..., completed=True)`? (`occupied_by` is always `None` in `CAUTION` — guaranteed by `on_block_tail_vacated()` clearing it — so a separate non-occupied check is vacuous. `reserved_by_path` is explicitly not a gating condition; if `_is_caution` has cleared but `reserved_by_path` remains set, the computed block state is `RESERVED`, not `FREE`.)
- Can any completion or cancellation path still unlock a block or switch that the train physically occupies at full stop?
- After `TrainController.release_emergency_cmd()`, does the system return to the state-machine-derived stopped ready state (`IDLE` with no cab selected, `STANDBY` with a cab selected), restart through fresh path creation after EB cancellation, and require explicit departure confirmation through `departure_confirmation_required`?
- Do train-owned flank-protection claims record `source_switch_id` so tail-vacate can release only the protection created from the vacated main switch?

**Notes**

- In the desired model, ordinary switch release happens when the train tail vacates the switch tile, not on full path completion. `TrainController.on_physics_updated(event_time, span_update)` reports the vacated resource in `tail_vacated_resources`, and `PathDispatcher.on_controller_progress(report)` releases it according to `resource_type`: blocks go through `BlockManager.on_block_tail_vacated(...)`, while switches clear `Switch.occupied_by` and release train-owned protection sourced from that vacated switch through `Switch.release_train_protection_from(...)`. The neighbour-side `source_switch_id` filter protects claims created by other occupied main switches. Both completion and cancellation must respect physical occupancy: if a switch is still physically occupied, its train-owned protection stays in place until the later tail-vacate event.
- `assigned_path_id` moves from `Train` to `TrainController` as a read-only derived property of `received_path_plan.path_id`. `PathDispatcher` owns the underlying `received_path_plan`: it sets that plan when the active path is established, mirrors only the single active authority path referenced by `active_path_id_by_train[train_id]`, and clears it on path release by calling `controller.receive_path_plan(None)` before calling `RouteExecutor.handle_path_completion(...)`.
